| Colour Mixer, guided filter on (pre-pass + display, fit) | Bayer 24 MP | 21.9 ms | 1.84 ms | 590 MB |
| Colour Mixer, guided filter off | Bayer 24 MP | 4.36 ms | ≈0 | 558 MB |
| Colour Grading on (display, fit) | Bayer 24 MP | 4.90 ms | 0.066 ms | 558 MB |
| Detail: denoise (profiled), full size | Bayer 24 MP | 85.4 ms | 40.8 ms | 1.56 GB |
| Detail: capture sharpening, Amount 40, full size | Bayer 24 MP | 103 ms | 41.9 ms | 1.56 GB |
| Display pass, fit / 100 %, with or without Tone Curve | Bayer 24 MP | 2.8–5.8 ms | ≈0 | 0.8–1.0 GB |
| Remove: one spot (CPU fill search) | Bayer 24 MP | 577 ms | 0.655 ms | 1.50 GB |
| Export render, full size | Bayer 24 MP | 73.1 ms | 10.0 ms | 1.62 GB |
| Export end to end (Stage A + Detail + warp + render; no encoding) | Bayer 24 MP | 342 ms | 66.7 ms | 2.19 GB |
| JPEG encode (jpegli q90, CPU worker) | Bayer 24 MP | 908 ms | — | 1022 MB |
| Thumbnail 512 px | Bayer 24 MP | 13.1 ms | ≈0 | 1.05 GB |
| Stage A | Bayer 45 MP | 125 ms | 27.2 ms | 2.30 GB |
| Detail denoise / sharpening, full size | Bayer 45 MP | 151 / 162 ms | 79.4 / 83.1 ms | 2.95 GB |
| Remove: one spot | Bayer 45 MP | 1020 ms | 2.10 ms | 2.84 GB |
| Export end to end | Bayer 45 MP | 626 ms | 125 ms | 4.15 GB |
| JPEG encode | Bayer 45 MP | 1822 ms | — | 1.89 GB |
| Stage A, Markesteijn 1-pass | X-Trans 26 MP | 116 ms | 73.5 ms | 1012 MB |
| Stage A, Markesteijn 3-pass | X-Trans 26 MP | 345 ms | 262 ms | 1.04 GB |
| Export end to end | X-Trans 26 MP | 277 ms | 118 ms | 1.26 GB |
| Stage A (JPEG input) | sRGB 24 MP | 13.2 ms | 1.18 ms | 626 MB |
| JPEG encode | sRGB 24 MP | 803 ms | — | 382 MB |

Peak memory per test image over the whole run: Bayer 24 MP 2.19 GB, Bayer 45 MP 4.15 GB, X-Trans 26 MP 1.26 GB, sRGB 24 MP 748 MB; 0 MB after `unload()` in every case.

Run 2 was close to run 1 on most rows (for example Stage A, Bayer 24 MP: 83.6 ms wall, 21.7 ms GPU; Remove one spot: 572 ms; export end to end: 311 ms; JPEG encode: 911 ms). The exception is X-Trans Markesteijn 3-pass, 266 ms wall and 185 ms GPU (demosaic 181 ms) against 345 / 262 ms (255 ms) in run 1; the cause of that difference is not known.

What the numbers say:
- **The GPU stages are fast** on this adapter: a 24 MP RAW develops in about 76 ms, the Colour Mixer's guided filter costs about 1.8 ms of GPU time (so it stays on), and every display pass is under 6 ms.
- **The slow steps run on the CPU**: JPEG encoding (about 0.9 s per 24 MP photo, 1.8 s per 45 MP) takes longer than the whole GPU export (0.34 s), and Remove's fill search takes about 0.58 s per spot at 24 MP (1 s at 45 MP) while the GPU part is under 1 ms. These are the targets of the performance build (Remove on several workers, batch export overlap, a shared CPU budget).
- **Memory**: a 45 MP export peaks at 4.15 GB of the engine's own allocations, inside this 12 GB GPU; smaller GPUs have not been measured.

Limits of these results: one GPU, one browser, two runs, synthetic images.

## Part 17: Colour Grading

**What it does.** A **Colour Grading** group after the Colour Mixer and before Detail (jump bar: three small wheels, "Grading") with Lightroom's Color Grading controls:
- four wheels, **Shadows**, **Midtones**, **Highlights** and **Global**, each with its hue and saturation as a point on the wheel, a **Luminance** slider and Hue/Saturation number fields;
- **Blending** and **Balance** under the wheels in every view;
- views **3-Way** (Midtones on top, Shadows and Highlights below) and one large wheel each for **Shadows · Midtones · Highlights · Global**;
- darktable's settings under **Advanced**.

The engine is darktable's **color balance rgb** (`src/iop/colorbalancergb.c`, commit `6271f53`, GPL-3.0-or-later) translated to WGSL, per pixel, before the sigmoid and after the Colour Mixer. It is off by default and skipped when it changes nothing: an edit without it is exactly an edit from before this part and renders byte for byte as before (below). A new tool that is off by default needs no new process version, so the current version stays 2.

This part is numbered 17: Part 18 is the culling helpers' build.

Owner's decisions (2026-10-14, after the research in [research/COLOUR_GRADING.md](research/COLOUR_GRADING.md)):
1. **Engine:** Lightroom's Color Grading on darktable's color balance rgb in WGSL, before the sigmoid, after the Colour Mixer. Shadows → lift, Midtones → power, Highlights → gain, each with hue, saturation (chroma) and luminance. Blending → the masks' fall-off; Balance → the middle-grey fulcrum. The rest of darktable's module under Advanced.
2. **Global = an even tint over all tones** (darktable's offset style), tuned to look like Lightroom's Global.
3. **Basic's Vibrance and Saturation stay as they are:** no move, no new process version.
4. **Defaults:** slider scales the closest match, tuned by eye on test photos; Blending 50 and Balance 0 by default; Blending 100 for old Split Toning sidecars; the stage skipped entirely when neutral, and its gamut mapping not applied when every wheel is at zero, so old edits are byte-identical.
5. **UI:** three wheels plus Global in Lightroom's layout (3-up and single-wheel views), a point per wheel, a Luminance slider, number fields; full keyboard and screen-reader semantics, double-click to reset, Alt-drag fine control; Blending and Balance; help and guidance; a jump-bar entry; after the Colour Mixer.
6. **Storage:** `colourGrading` in the develop settings, absent at defaults; no process version.
7. **Paste/Sync** item in the Colour group, ticked by default; presets with Amount.
8. **Lightroom sidecars:** the hybrid Split Toning / ColorGrade fields read and written under Part 7's rules, closest match, unverified ranges marked.
9. **GPU robustness** (Part 14) for every new resource, and a benchmark row (Part 16).

### What Adobe says about Lightroom's Color Grading

Checked by Cowork at Adobe's own pages on 2026-10-13/14 (report `scratchpad/cowork-b2/verify-2026-10-13b/C_COLOUR_GRADING.md`, quotes with URLs). LightBench follows these; the rest of Lightroom's behaviour isn't public.

| Fact | Source |
|---|---|
| Released in Lightroom Classic 10.0 and Camera Raw 13.0, October 2020. | [LrC October 2020 feature summary](https://helpx.adobe.com/lightroom-classic/help/whats-new/2021.html); [ACR 13.0](https://helpx.adobe.com/camera-raw/using/whats-new/2021.html); [Adobe blog, 2020-10-20](https://blog.adobe.com/en/publish/2020/10/20/introducing-color-grading) |
| Blending's default is 50; each wheel's Luminance slider runs −100 to 100; the handle at the wheel's edge is saturation 100. | Adobe blog (Max Wendt) |
| Balance is signed around 0: "Values greater than 0 will increase the effect of highlights", below 0 the shadows'. Its numeric range isn't stated. | ACR 13.0 help |
| Hue: examples use values such as 240 and 120, but no range or unit is stated. | Adobe blog |
| Gestures: Shift = saturation only, Ctrl/⌘ = hue only, Alt/Option = Fine Adjust; double-clicking a handle resets that control; Alt-drag on Blending boosts the saturation to 100 for a preview; Adobe uses a soft "spoke" constraint that Fine Adjust skips. | Adobe blog; LrC help; jkost.com (secondary, an Adobe evangelist's blog) |
| Global: "a color tint plus luminance adjustment to the entire image, across all the ranges"; "Blending and Balance have no effect on the toning applied by the Global wheel"; luminance works without a tint. | Adobe blog |
| Split Toning "always used what we now call Blending = 100"; existing Split Toning images "look exactly the same". | Adobe blog; LrC help |
| XMP: Shadows/Highlights hue and saturation and Balance stay in `crs:SplitToning*` ("also used for newer ColorGrade settings"); the rest is `crs:ColorGrade*`; there is no `ColorGradeShadowHue` or `ColorGradeHighlightHue`. | ExifTool 3.79 `XMP.pm` and its tag table |
| **Not public:** the maths of the wheels, Global and Luminance; the hue scale of the XMP fields; Balance's range; what an absent `ColorGradeBlending` means. | — |

### Where it acts

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Position | In the display shader (`develop-display.wgsl` `developBeforeCurve`): exposure → tone equalizer → Vibrance/Saturation → Colour Mixer → Point Color → **Colour Grading** → sigmoid → Tone Curve → output profile, on scene-referred linear Rec.2020. | Owner's decision 1; darktable's v5.0 order (`src/common/iop_order.c` lines 380-388: colorequal 41.2, colorbalancergb 41.5, sigmoid 45.3) |
| Off | With no wheel's saturation or luminance moved and darktable's Advanced sliders at 0, `colourGradingGpu` returns null, the Grade uniform is all zero and the shader skips the stage. Blending, Balance, a hue with saturation 0, the fall-offs or the fulcrums alone change nothing, so they don't turn it on. | Owner's decision 4 |
| Not over the mixer's Visualize | While the Colour Mixer's Visualize view is on, the grade isn't drawn (it would tint the greyed colours). | LightBench |
| Everywhere | The uniform travels in `displayUniforms` (`colourGrading`): Develop at fit and 100 %, the histogram and Auto, the Tone Curve's input histogram (the grade is before the curve), the white balance loupe, thumbnails, culling previews, the background developer and Export. | Part 1's rule |

### darktable's color balance rgb, as translated (`engine/develop/colour-grading.ts`, `engine/gpu/colour-grading.wgsl`)

| Step | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Spaces | Rec.2020 → XYZ D65 → CIE 2006 LMS (Kirk's matrix) → Filmlight Yrg → Ych; the grading RGB of Filmlight; darktable UCS 22 for perceptual saturation and brilliance and the gamut map. | `colorspaces_inline_conversions.h` 1082-1330; `colorbalancergb.c` `process` 668-948 |
| Masks | darktable's `opacity_masks` on Y^0.41 (middle grey at 50 %): logistic shadows and highlights masks with steepness 2 + 2 × fall-off, a bell for the mid-tones. At the defaults: −4 EV 0.94/0.02/0.06, 0 EV 0.50/0.50/0.50, +2 EV 0.04/0.01/0.96 (shadows/mid-tones/highlights; the brief's table, unit test). Shadows + highlights = 1 when their fall-offs are equal; the three sum to 1.5 at middle grey (darktable's design: the mid-tone mask weighs only chroma, saturation and brilliance). | `opacity_masks` 550-577 |
| Order per pixel | Hue shift → linear chroma and vibrance (with the masks) → `gamut_check_Yrg` → to grading RGB → **global offset** → **lift and gain** under the masks, `RGB × ((1 − β)(1 − α + α·shadows) + β·highlights)` → **power** `(|RGB|/white)^midtones × white` (unmasked) → Y power (Midtones luminance) → contrast around its fulcrum → perceptual saturation and brilliance (darktable UCS) with darktable's soft limit → gamut map (saturation soft-clipped between 80 % and 100 % of Rec.2020's boundary, from darktable's gamut table) → Rec.2020. | `process`, darktable UCS formula (darktable's default) |
| **Only what the grade adds** (LightBench's change) | The output is the pixel plus (graded − the same steps with neutral settings). darktable's module changes colours merely by being enabled (its manual says so): it clips negative values, its saturation step soft-limits very saturated colours and its gamut map is always on. Subtracting the neutral run applies those only as much as the grade adds: a neutral grade is exactly no change, a tiny change a tiny difference, and a colour the grade doesn't push out of gamut keeps its own saturation. Cost: the steps run twice per pixel. | Owner's decision 4 ("avoid that") |
| Matrices (LightBench's) | Rec.2020 → XYZ is LightBench's D65 matrix (darktable: its profile's D50 matrix and CAT16), as the Colour Mixer. The Yrg white point is computed from it: (0.219020, 0.543668) against darktable's constants (0.21902143, 0.54371398), which come from sRGB's D50-adapted white; so a grey is exactly neutral in Yrg. The LMS → XYZ and LMS → grading RGB matrices are computed as exact inverses of darktable's forward ones (darktable writes both directions to 6-8 digits). | LightBench |
| Not offered | darktable's JzAzBz saturation formula (darktable UCS, its default since 2022, is used), its checkerboard mask previews and its "pick the opposite colour" picker. | LightBench |

### The wheels → darktable's parameters (LightBench's closest match)

| Control | darktable | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| Hue (0–359) | the wheel's colour T | Lightroom-style: the hue of an sRGB colour (0 red, 120 green, 240 blue). It becomes a Yrg angle through the sRGB colour at that hue (HSV saturation 50 %, as the Colour Mixer's band centres). darktable's own hue convention (red near 0, Yrg angle + 30°) is shown nowhere; the Advanced hue shift is darktable's. **Lightroom's hue scale is unverified** (Adobe gives none; darktable's importer divides by 255, not copied). |
| Saturation (0–100) | Yrg chroma of T | ×0.10 (Shadows), ×0.08 (Midtones), ×0.12 (Highlights), ×0.08 (Global) at 100: darktable's chroma slider reaches 1.0, far stronger. Tuned by eye (below). |
| Shadows, Highlights | lift and gain slopes | darktable: 1 + (T − white) + luminance. **LightBench: T / white + luminance**, per channel. Grading RGB's white isn't (1, 1, 1), so darktable's absolute tint turned a grey towards another hue, by a strength that depended on the hue: in a probe (chroma 0.25 at saturation 100) on a deep-shadow grey, an orange wheel (hue 30) gave sRGB hue 15–17, and a teal wheel at saturation 50 was already fully saturated where orange at 100 was not. With T / white a grey in the shadows becomes exactly T (unit test: every 15° of hue, within 3°). |
| Midtones | power | darktable: exponents 1 / (1 + T − white). **LightBench: exponents chosen so middle grey becomes T** (p = ln(T·x₀) / ln(white·x₀), x₀ = 0.1845 over the white fulcrum); the power stays darktable's (unmasked, normalised by the white fulcrum, so Blending and Balance don't shape it). |
| Global | offset | darktable's global offset (T − white + white × luminance), **added in proportion to each pixel's own luminance** (LightBench's change, owner's decision 2): every tone gets the same tint, and Global luminance becomes a gain. A plain offset tints the shadows far more than the highlights. Unmasked, so Blending and Balance don't touch it (as Adobe says of Lightroom's Global; unit test). |
| Luminance (−100…100) | darktable's luminance | ×0.5 (Shadows, Highlights: slopes 0.5…1.5), ×0.25 (Midtones: the Y exponent 1 / (1 ± 0.25)), ×0.4 (Global: a gain 0.6…1.4). On a grey: Shadows −100 → ×0.55 at −5 EV; Highlights +100 → ×1.40 at +1 EV; Midtones +100 → ×1.40 at 0 EV, Midtones −100 → ×0.57 at 0 EV and much darker in the deepest shadows (a power). |
| Blending (0–100, default 50) | both masks' fall-off | 50 = darktable's 1.0 (its default), 100 = 0 (softest), 0 = 3 (steepest), linear between. Advanced can set the two fall-offs apart. |
| Balance (−100…100, default 0) | the masks' middle-grey fulcrum | Positive favours the highlights' tint (Adobe's direction): the fulcrum moves from 18.45 % down by 2 EV at +100, up by 2 EV at −100. |

The scales were tuned by eye on 420 px renders of the D700, the ILCE-7M2 and the JPEG landscape (`looks-grading.mjs`): a first round (Shadows 0.25, Highlights 0.20, Midtones 0.12, Global 0.12) put full-strength teal into the dark ILCE-7M2 frame at Saturation 50, so they were lowered. The final scales give a clear but moderate tint at 30–50 and a strong one at 100. **Not compared with Lightroom.**

### The Colour Grading group (`develop/ColourGradingPanel.tsx`)

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Views | **3-Way · Shadows · Midtones · Highlights · Global**, remembered in this browser; a dot marks a view whose wheel moved. 3-Way: 112 px wheels; single views: a 196 px wheel with 25/50/75 % rings. | Lightroom's views (the brief) |
| The wheel | A hue ring painted with sRGB HSL colours (hue 0 at the right, rising anticlockwise: LightBench's layout, Lightroom's not checked), greying towards the marked centre; the point shows the colour it applies. | LightBench |
| Dragging | Press anywhere on the wheel: the point jumps there and follows. **Shift**: saturation only (along the current spoke); **Ctrl/⌘**: hue only; **Alt**: fine, a quarter of the pointer's movement from where it started. One history step per drag ("Colour grading: Shadows"). Adobe's soft spoke constraint isn't copied: Shift is a hard constraint. | Adobe's gestures |
| Double-click | Resets that wheel's hue and saturation (one step, "Colour grading: reset Shadows colour"). Double-clicking a slider resets it, as everywhere. | Adobe; LightBench's sliders |
| Keyboard and screen readers | The point is a focusable `role="slider"` named "Shadows colour", with `aria-valuetext` "hue 200°, saturation 40". **← →** turn the hue by 1° (wrapping through 0/360), **↑ ↓** change the saturation by 1, **Shift** ×10, **Home** resets the colour. Each wheel block is a group named "Shadows wheel"; changes are announced in a live region; keys merge into one step like a slider's. | Owner's decision 5; Part 12's rules |
| Number fields | Hue (0–359) and Saturation (0–100) under every wheel ("H"/"S" in 3-Way). | Owner's decision 5 |
| Blending's Alt-drag | Holding Alt while dragging Blending shows Shadows', Midtones' and Highlights' tints at saturation 100 (Global left alone) until release; nothing is saved. | Adobe (via jkost.com) |
| Advanced | Hue shift, Vibrance, Contrast; Linear chroma, Perceptual saturation and Perceptual brilliance for Global, Shadows, Mid-tones and Highlights; Shadows and Highlights fall-off (following Blending until set), White fulcrum, Contrast fulcrum; Reset Advanced. Each with darktable's name in its help. darktable's vibrance and contrast here are its own, not Basic's. | darktable's module parameters (`colorbalancergb.c` 60-106) |
| Reset, eye, history | **Reset** (wheels, Blending, Balance; Advanced stays), **Reset Shadows** etc. in single views; the group's eye hides the grade ("Hidden: Colour Grading"). | Parts 1, 12, 15 |
| Guidance | Under the title: "Colour by tone: cool shadows, warm highlights. Basic first; grade last." Its tooltip says what grading does, to set white balance, Basic and the mixer first, and the pitfalls (small saturations go a long way; greys take the tint; Blending and Balance shape only Shadows and Highlights). `help.ts`: `gradeWheel`, `gradeLuminance`, `gradeBlending`, `gradeBalance`. | Owner's rule (USABILITY.md) |

### Copy, paste, presets and the stored edit

| Behaviour | Detail |
|---|---|
| Stored | `colourGrading` in the develop settings and `lightbench:Develop`: `shadows`, `midtones`, `highlights`, `global` (`hue` 0–359, `sat` 0–100, `lum` −100…100, integers), `blending`, `balance`, and `advanced` only when darktable's settings differ from their defaults. **Absent when untouched or reset**: `normalizeSettings` and `withColourGrading` drop a grade at its defaults, so such an edit's JSON is exactly an old edit's. A hue picked with saturation 0, or Blending or Balance alone, are kept (they render nothing). |
| Copy, Sync, Paste, New preset | One item, **Colour Grading**, in the Colour group after Point Color, ticked by default (as the mixer's). A photo without a grade copies "none". Auto Sync sends it only when it changed. A Paste choice remembered from before this part doesn't include it (as for the mixer's items). |
| Presets and Amount | Each wheel's hue and saturation move as a colour vector (saturation × the hue's direction, LightCraft's way) from the photo's towards the preset's, so hue 350 and hue 10 blend through 0; luminance, Blending, Balance and darktable's sliders linearly; the fall-off overrides follow the preset above 0 % (`blendColourGrading`). |
| Process version | None: a new tool, off by default, skipped when neutral; no existing rendering changes (byte-identical, below). A later change to its maths, mapping or scales needs a new version. |

### Lightroom's Color Grading in sidecars (`sidecar/lightroom.ts`)

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Fields | `crs:SplitToningShadowHue/Saturation`, `ColorGradeShadowLum`, `ColorGradeMidtoneHue/Sat/Lum`, `SplitToningHighlightHue/Saturation`, `ColorGradeHighlightLum`, `ColorGradeGlobalHue/Sat/Lum`, `ColorGradeBlending`, `SplitToningBalance` (`LR_GRADE_FIELDS`). | ExifTool 3.79 |
| Mapping | Number for number: the wheels have Lightroom's names and ranges. The look is only approximately Lightroom's (closest match; Lightroom's maths isn't public). Hue as degrees and Balance as ±100 are **assumptions**. Absent fields read 0; Blending absent reads **100 when any Split Toning field is present** (an edit from before Color Grading; Adobe documents the behaviour, the field rule is **inferred**), otherwise 50. Reading keeps the photo's Advanced settings. | Owner's decision 8; Cowork's check |
| Nothing to grade | A sidecar's grade with every saturation and luminance at 0 (many sidecars carry Split Toning fields at 0) counts as no grade: no "Lightroom settings" step, no key. A hue only counts with its saturation; Blending and Balance only when a wheel grades. | LightBench |
| Reading and writing | Part 7's rules, as the Detail and HSL fields: read when a photo is added and by **Read changes**; written only into a sidecar that already has `crs:ProcessVersion` and isn't already applied, only the fields LightBench changed since the file was last written (a changed saturation writes its hue too), each in place; integers, Luminance and Balance with a sign ("+5"), as the HSL fields. A sidecar with Split Toning and no `ColorGradeBlending` gets `ColorGradeBlending` written with the first change, so it still reads as LightBench read it. The numbers are part of the `lightbench:LightroomGeometry` marker; markers and change records from before this part have none and never count as a difference. | Part 7; **not checked against a sidecar Lightroom wrote** |
| Advanced | darktable's settings aren't exchanged with Lightroom. | — |

### Engine (`engine/gpu/develop-engine.ts`, `colour-grading.wgsl`)

| Step | Detail |
|---|---|
| Per pixel | `gradeApply` in the display shader: `gradeSteps` twice (the grade and the neutral run), no texture reads besides the photo. The colour-mixer.wgsl helpers that don't read the Mixer uniform are shared. |
| Binding | One uniform at **binding 11**, `Grade` (2480 bytes): the matrices, the resolved parameters, a flag and Rec.2020's 512-entry gamut table (darktable's `dt_UCS_22_build_gamut_LUT`, the mixer's table) as 128 × vec4. `View` (512 bytes) and bindings 0-10 are unchanged. |
| CPU twin | `gradeSteps` / `gradePixel` / `gradeImage` repeat the shader line for line in double precision; `engine.gradingImages` reads the grade's input and output (linear Rec.2020 before the sigmoid; a new display-shader mode 5, "after the mixer and the grade") for the comparison. |
| GPU robustness (Part 14) | The uniform buffer is the engine's (like the View uniform), made with the engine, so a new engine after a lost device has its own; it is written only when the grade changes (`gradeKey`), and **`unload()` zeroes it** so the next photo starts with the stage off. No texture, no pipeline and no per-drawing resource is added: the stage is part of the display pipeline, which Export, the background developer and the 100 % view already run inside `guarded()` and after the foreground gate. |
| Benchmark (Part 16) | One row after the Colour Mixer rows: **Colour Grading on: display pass, fit** (all four wheels tinted; GPU = the `display` pass). |

### Verified

Headless Chromium with software WebGPU (SwiftShader), the app loaded with `?gpu=readback`, one test at a time. Scripts in `scratchpad/cg/` (copies of Part 15's `cm/` scripts, adapted; the originals unchanged). Test files: Nikon D700 `philadelphia.nef`, Nikon D80 `glarus.nef`, Sony ILCE-6300 and ILCE-7M2 ARWs, `test-srgb.jpg`.

- **Old edits are byte-identical to main** (`7b8b0f0`, 0.5.7; `identity.mjs`, main served from its own copy of the tree, each run alone). Fifteen edits rendered on main and on this branch, hashed (SHA-256) in six outputs each: the full-size 8-bit export, a 1024 px export, the 512 px thumbnail, the histogram and clipping statistics, and the Develop canvas (890 × 662, read back) at fit and at 100 %. **All 90 hashes were identical.** The edits: Part 15's twelve (D700 process 1 with crop, turn and angle; process 2 with angle; D700 defaults with the RAW sharpening; D80 custom white balance; the JPEG unedited, process 1 and process 2; ILCE-6300 with angle; D700 with three Remove/Heal/Clone spots, crop, angle and custom white balance; D700 with spots, a tone curve and sharpening; the JPEG with spots, a tone curve and Detail; the JPEG process 2 edit with a `colourMixer` at its defaults), their full-size hashes the same as in Part 15's table, plus three new ones: the JPEG with a **Colour Mixer** (bands, guided filter: `d585b523c61b9337…`), the D700 with **a mixer and Point Color, spots, a tone curve and Detail** (`ab06f5dde373ab47…`), and the D80 with a `colourGrading` at its defaults (`f34d827d0e1ed7bc…`: dropped on the branch, ignored on main). The branch run took 2494 s against main's 1418 s because another session's identity run shared the machine for part of it; the hashes don't depend on that.
- **GPU = CPU** (`e2e-gpucpu-grading.mjs`). The engine's own grading input and output (`engine.gradingImages`: linear Rec.2020 before the sigmoid, the new mode 5) against the CPU twin (`gradeImage`) run on the GPU's input, for six grades on the JPEG and the D80: teal shadows and orange highlights; all four wheels with luminance, Blending 80, Balance −30; Global alone (tint and luminance); luminance only; darktable's Advanced settings (hue shift, vibrance, contrast, chroma, saturation, brilliance, white fulcrum) with wheels; and a grade after a Colour Mixer with its guided filter.
  - At 300 px: largest relative difference 1.3·10⁻⁴, 99.9th percentile ≤ 1.2·10⁻⁴, mean 0.9–4.7·10⁻⁵, no pixel over 1 %.
  - At 1600 px (the JPEG at full size; the mixer case drawn in 2 × 2 tiles): largest 1.9·10⁻⁴, 99.9th percentile ≤ 1.3·10⁻⁴, mean 0.9–4.6·10⁻⁵, no pixel over 1 %. **24 of 24** checks at each size.
- **In Develop** (`e2e-grading.mjs`, the JPEG; **34 of 34** on the final code; a first run passed 30 of 33, the 3 failures the test's own: its Alt-drag on Blending also moves the slider, so the fixed Blending and undo expectations after it were wrong; the script now reads the values and undoes before the Alt-drag):

  - Shift-drag changed only the saturation (200/60 → 200/23); Ctrl-drag only the hue (→ 260/23); Alt-drag of 40 px moved the point 20.1 of the 80 units a plain drag would (a quarter).

The device was destroyed twice; each time the notice showed and the photo was drawn again on a new engine with exactly the same mean and colourfulness (129.56 and 22.74; without the grade 131.02 and 27.36), no error, one canvas, one live device. Afterwards a grading change showed (colourfulness 18.17) and the eye gave the photo without the grade exactly (131.02, 27.36).

SwiftShader at 0.3 MP (**says nothing about a real GPU**): grading 1964 ms median (GPU 1956 ms, peak 16 MB), "Display pass, fit, without Tone Curve" 2472 ms in the same run (noise larger than the difference). After each image's `unload()` 36,936 bytes stayed allocated (Part 15: 34 kB): the 2480-byte Grade uniform, which belongs to the engine.

- **Unit tests** (`npx vitest run`: **817 tests in 85 files**; main `7b8b0f0` 787 in 82, so 30 new):

  - `engine/gpu/colour-grading-gpu.test.ts` (2): `unload` zeroes the 2480-byte uniform once; the uniform travels in `displayUniforms` with a key that changes with the grade.

- From a clean `npm ci`: `npx tsc -b`, `npx oxlint` (the two existing warnings), `npx vitest run` and `npm run build` passed. The main chunk is 1,091.62 kB (348.59 kB gzipped).

### Limitations and unverified

- **Lightroom.** Nothing was checked in Lightroom. Its wheel, Global and Luminance maths aren't public: the mapping and the scales are LightBench's closest match, tuned by eye on three photos, so the same numbers look only roughly like Lightroom's. Unverified: the hue scale of the XMP hue fields (degrees assumed), Balance's numeric range (±100 assumed), the rule that an absent `ColorGradeBlending` means 100 (Adobe documents the behaviour, not the field), the sign formatting of written values, and Lightroom's wheel orientation (LightBench puts red at the right). The sidecars were written for the tests; no sidecar written by Lightroom was available.
- **darktable differences (all LightBench's choices, above):** the wheel → slope/exponent mapping (T / white; mid-grey-calibrated exponents), Global's offset in proportion to luminance, Lightroom-style hues, the neutral run subtracted, LightBench's D65 matrix and the Yrg white computed from it, exact matrix inverses. Not compared with darktable's own output.
- **Masks:** darktable's; the three don't sum to 1, and the Midtones power is unmasked, so Blending and Balance shape only Shadows and Highlights (and the Advanced per-range sliders).
- **Speed:** the steps run twice per pixel when a grade is on; SwiftShader's timings are noisy and say nothing about a real GPU.
- Not offered: darktable's JzAzBz formula, mask previews and colour picker; a hover readout of the range masks (the brief's idea).

## Part 18: Culling helpers

**What it does.** LightBench measures every photo's camera preview (a JPEG's own pixels) and helps cull with what it finds, without AI and without downloading anything:
- **Badges** in the grid and the culling view, apart from the flag, stars and label: **Soft** (possibly soft), **Shake**, **Clipped**, **Look-alike** (“duplicate of…”), **☆ Best** (the sharpest of its stack or of a group of look-alikes) and a dashed **Reject?** (a suggested reject). Each badge's tooltip says why, in a sentence. Which badges show is a choice (Culling menu, Settings › Culling).
- **Sort** by sharpness (Sharpest first, Least sharp first) and a **Sharpness filter** (Possibly soft or shake, Not soft, Suggested rejects, Look-alikes of a sharper photo, Not analysed yet).
- **Auto-stack** can require the photos to look alike, with a **strictness** slider, and put the sharpest on top; frames of a fast burst are ordered by the camera's sub-second time and frame counter.
- **Select the best of each stack** (selects, doesn't flag; P then picks them).
- **Suggest rejects…** lists the photos it would reject and why; only **Apply** sets their reject flags, in one step that **Undo** (or Ctrl+Z in the Library) takes back. Files are never deleted.
- In culling, **Z** zooms to 100 % at the **measured focus point**: the camera's AF point when the file records it, else the sharpest area. A click still zooms where it is clicked.

Photos are measured in the background **after** Open folder or Import has added them (so adding photos is exactly as fast as before), and photos already in the library once, with a quiet note and Pause; until a photo is measured it shows a small “…” (“Not analysed yet”). This part is numbered 18: Part 17 is Colour Grading. Nothing is written to sidecars, exports or the photos, and no face data exists: faces and blinks wait for the Phase 4 runtime (owner).

Owner's decisions (2026-10-13, after [research/CULLING_HELPERS.md](research/CULLING_HELPERS.md)):
1. **v1 is non-AI**: tiled Laplacian/gradient sharpness on the camera preview, weighted by the AF point where the file gives it, else the sharpest tiles; relative within one camera + ISO and within a stack or burst, and only “possibly soft” across a library. Motion blur/shake. Exposure problems with the existing histogram code. Near-duplicates and bursts from 64-bit dHash/pHash plus capture time, sub-second time and frame counters, in the Auto-stack dialog with a strictness slider. Best of each stack by sharpness and exposure.
2. **No faces or blinks** until the Phase 4 runtime (then BlazeFace only).
3. **Suggested rejects always need the owner's Apply**: shown apart from real flags, applied as reject flags (catalog and `lightbench:Flag`) in one undoable step, never automatically; never a deletion.
4. **Background analysis**: a pausable, resumable pass like `refresh.ts`, skipping folders without permission; results in a new `photo_analysis` table (schema 14), deleted with the photo, never in sidecars. New photos were first to be measured in the probe worker while Open folder reads them; **changed by the owner on 2026-10-14**: they are measured by the background pass right after Open folder or Import, so Open folder runs at its old speed (it added about two thirds to its time on small JPEGs, below).
5. **UX**: badges on by default for possibly soft, shake, clipped and duplicate (switchable); sort and filter by sharpness; select best of each stack; Z to the measured focus point; tooltips, help text, keyboard access; a 1,000+ photo folder stays responsive.

### What is measured (`engine/analysis/`)

All on the CPU in the background pass's own workers (`library/analysis.worker.ts`, which decodes the preview as the probe worker does for thumbnails; Open folder's probe worker, `import/probe.worker.ts`, is unchanged), on the upright preview scaled to **1,536 px** on its long side (smaller previews as they are; the size measured is stored). No GPU, so the foreground gate (Part 14) isn't involved.

| Measure | How | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Sharpness | Luma split into 64 px tiles (24 × 16 at 3:2). Per tile: Laplacian energy (variance of the 4-neighbour Laplacian) at full and half size, standard deviation, and the Sobel structure tensor. **Noise floor** = median Laplacian energy of the flattest fifth of the tiles, taken off every tile. `sharpPeak` = 90th percentile of √(energy − floor) over textured tiles (σ ≥ 4 levels); `sharpSubject` = its mean over the subject's tiles; **acuity** = √(fine − floor) / √(coarse − floor) on the subject's tiles (median): lower is softer, nearly independent of the scene's contrast. | Pech-Pacheco et al.'s variance of the Laplacian and Crete et al.'s re-blur idea (research §2.1, not re-read); digiKam's imgqsort approach (GPL-2.0+) for flat tiles and the AF region; LightBench's code |
| Subject | Tiles under the AF box expanded 2× about its centre (digiKam's choice), else the sharpest tenth (at least 4). | research §2.1 |
| Where Z goes | The AF box's centre, else the centre of the tile whose 3 × 3 neighbourhood is sharpest. Normalised and upright. | LightBench |
| Shake | Each textured tile's gradient direction (doubled angle) weighted by its coherence, averaged over the frame: 0 when directions are random, near 1 when the whole frame is smeared one way. Also the smear's direction. Horizontal and vertical edges cancel (doubled angles 0° and 180°), so buildings don't read as shake. | Structure tensor, standard; LightBench's use |
| Exposure | `statsFromPixels` / `metricsFromStats` from Auto (`engine/develop/auto.ts`), as they are, on every other pixel of every other row: clipped highlights (a channel at 255), clipped shadows (all at 0), median, 0.5th and 99.5th percentiles. | Owner's decision 1 |
| Look | dHash (9 × 8) and pHash (32 × 32 DCT, 8 × 8 low frequencies against their median), 64 bits each, from a quarter-size grey image; written here (`hash.ts`), as the `imagehash` package defines them (BSD-2-Clause, not copied). | research §2.3 |
| Burst order | SubSecTimeOriginal (else SubSecTime); the camera's frame counter; LibRaw's `shot_order` when set. | Below |
| AF point | EXIF SubjectArea (point, circle or rectangle); Sony FocusLocation; Fujifilm FocusPixel; Olympus AF area through LibRaw. | Below |

`ANALYSIS_ALGO` (1) is stored with each result; raising it makes the background pass measure everything again.

### Focus points and frame counters: what the files and LibRaw give

LibRaw's binding exposes maker-note fields, but on the test files they were **not usable** [measured]: `shot_order` was 0 on all seven test RAWs; Sony's `Sony0x9400_SequenceImageNumber` read 0xFFFFFFFF and `FocusLocation` zeros on both Sony files; the Fujifilm block read implausible values (sequence 1207959576), which looks like a structure-layout mismatch in this build. So LightBench reads the tags itself with its TIFF reader (`engine/meta/capture.ts`) and keeps LibRaw's `shot_order` (when non-zero) and Olympus `AFPointSelected` (when it is four plausible 0–1 values) as fallbacks.

| Source | Tag | Test files [measured] |
|---|---|---|
| EXIF | SubSecTimeOriginal 0x9291 / SubSecTime 0x9290; SubjectArea 0x9214 in PixelX/YDimension | D700 “060” (60 ms), D80 “00”; no test file has SubjectArea (unit-tested only) |
| Nikon maker note (“Nikon\0”, TIFF at +10) | ShutterCount 0x00a7 | D700 49277, D80 33318 |
| Sony maker note (with or without the “SONY DSC ” header; offsets from the file's TIFF header) | FocusLocation 0x2027 = width, height, x, y; SequenceNumber 0xb04a | ILCE-6300: 6000 × 4000, (3525, 1241), on the tower's roof (checked by eye at 100 %); sequence 1. ILCE-7M2: no FocusLocation, sequence 0 |
| Fujifilm maker note (RAF's embedded JPEG) | FocusPixel 0x1023; SequenceNumber 0x1101 | X100V: the frame's centre; sequence 1 (its preview is black, so nothing else is measured) |
| Olympus | LibRaw `AFPointSelected` | No test file (unverified) |
| Pentax, Canon, Nikon AF | AF point indices need per-camera layouts | Not read (the sharpest tiles are used) |

The box is turned upright with the photo's orientation, as the previews are.

### What the Library shows (`catalog/analysis.ts`, used by the catalog worker and the page)

| Behaviour | Detail |
|---|---|
| Possibly soft | Acuity below **0.6 ×** the median of photos from the same camera at a similar ISO (one-stop bands), when there are at least **8** of them; or below **0.22** on its own (very soft on any scale; acuity depends on how much the preview is scaled down: a sharp Sony 1,616 px preview measured 0.42, a sharp D700 one 0.92). Tooltip: “Possibly soft: 47 % of the usual fine detail for … at ISO 141–283 (compared with 10 photos). Measured on the camera preview; check at 100 %.” |
| Shake | Direction agreement ≥ **0.3** over textured tiles (sharp test files measured up to 0.23; synthetic shake 0.44–0.60). “Possible camera shake or motion blur: detail is smeared in one direction (about 0° from horizontal) across 60 % of the textured areas.” |
| Clipped | Highlights in ≥ 2 % of the frame or shadows in ≥ 5 %; for RAWs “The RAW may hold more.” |
| Very dark / very bright (off by default) | Median < 12 % with the 99.5th percentile < 60 %, or median > 85 % (often intended). |
| Groups | **Stacks** (their hidden members count, also when a filter hides them), and **look-alikes** among unstacked originals: photos taken at most 30 s apart whose dHash and pHash differ by at most 10 of 64 bits on average (Medium). RAW+JPEG twins count once (the RAW); virtual copies show their original's measures but aren't ranked. |
| Ranking | Subject sharpness × an exposure factor (1, less up to 0.5 for clipped highlights above 1 %, up to 0.3 for clipped shadows above 5 %, 0.2 when the median is below 8 % or above 92 %). The first is **☆ Best**; others below **0.6 ×** the best's score are “clearly softer” (“93 % less sharp than BURST_0001.JPG, the sharpest of the 6 in this stack.”); unstacked ones get **Look-alike** (“Looks like BURST_0001.JPG, taken 0.2 s apart (1 of 64 bits differ); that one is sharper.”). |
| Suggested rejects | By default: clearly softer than the best of its group, and shake. Optional: possibly soft against the library, badly exposed (≥ 15 % clipped, or nearly black or white). Never a picked or rejected photo, never the best of a group. The badge is dashed red “Reject?”, kept apart from the ✕ flag. |
| Sort | Sharpest / least sharp first, by acuity relative to the photo's peers (or to the library's median when there are too few); not yet analysed photos last; stable. Capture-time sorts now order frames within one second by sub-second time and frame counter. |
| Filter | The Sharpness menu (above), applied in the catalog worker after the stacks' members are ranked. |
| Auto-stack | “…and only photos that look alike” (on when ≥ 90 % of the photos shown are analysed), **Strictness** Loose, Fairly loose, Medium, Fairly strict, Strict = at most 18, 14, 10, 6, 3 bits; linked when taken at most the gap's seconds apart (union of pairs, so a burst that drifts stays one stack). “Put the sharpest photo on top”. Unticked, it is exactly the old Auto-stack. Photos not yet analysed are left out (said in the dialog). |
| Select the best of each stack | Culling menu: selects the best of every stack shown, expanding stacks whose best is hidden, and says “P picks them”. |
| Suggest rejects… | Culling menu (or command search): the list with reasons and a count (“Apply rejects 6 of 17 photos … Files are never deleted”), reason check boxes (remembered), **Apply: reject n**. A banner then offers **Undo**; Ctrl+Z in the Library does the same. Undo restores each photo's flag from before, except on photos whose flag was changed since. |
| Not analysed yet | A muted “…” badge (switchable) whose tooltip says “Not analysed yet: … left out of suggestions and sorts last by sharpness”; the culling info line says “Not analysed yet” (or “Couldn't be analysed (no usable preview)”). Such photos get no verdicts, are never suggested, sort last by sharpness, and are what the “Not analysed yet” filter shows; Auto-stack's look-alike mode and the Suggest rejects dialog say how many are analysed. |
| Culling view | Badges and a line of measures in the info line (I), badges in Compare's panes; “Z 100 % at the focus point · click: 100 % there” in the hints. |
| Settings › Culling | What is measured and how far to trust it, the badge switches, the analysis's progress, Pause/Resume, and “Measure all photos again…”. |
| Keys | `library.suggestRejects` and `library.selectBest` (no key; in command search and the menu), `library.undoSuggested` Ctrl+Z (Library only; Develop's Ctrl+Z is unchanged), `cull.zoom` Z now “Zoom to 100 % at the measured focus point, and back”. No conflicts in either shortcut set (unit test). |

All thresholds are **starting points [estimate]**, set on synthetic blur and the test files, not tuned on a labelled set of the owner's photos (research §8 asks for one).

### Catalog: schema 14

| Behaviour | Detail |
|---|---|
| Table | `photo_analysis (photo_id PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES photos ON DELETE CASCADE, algo, analyzed_at, failed, src_w, src_h, meas_w, meas_h, sharp_peak, sharp_subject, acuity, noise_floor, motion, motion_subject, motion_angle, textured, subject_src, subject_box, focus_u, focus_v, clip_hi, clip_lo, median, p005, p995, dhash, phash, sub_sec, seq, seq_src)`. One row per original; virtual copies use their original's. `failed` = the preview couldn't be measured (not retried until `algo` changes). No face columns. |
| Upgrade | Adds the table only: every other table and row is unchanged (unit test compares the whole catalog). Dropping the table and setting `user_version` 13 gives the version-13 catalog exactly; an older LightBench refuses the version-14 catalog without changing it (its existing check). |
| Deletion | Removing a photo deletes its row (ON DELETE CASCADE; also for copies removed with their original). |
| Sidecars, exports | Nothing is written: derived data only. Backups include the table; a library restored from an older backup has no rows and is measured again. |

### Background pass (`library/analysis-pass.ts`, `AnalysisProgress.tsx`)

Like Part 6's refresh: originals with no row or an older `algo` (so photos just added, and older ones); started when the library opens, when Open folder finishes (and when it is opened again), after Import and when a folder is reconnected; two analysis workers; a yield between files; waits while Open folder or the refresh reads, and while Develop draws (the foreground gate, Part 14: the pass uses no GPU but leaves the CPU to Develop, at most 2 s at a time so it can't be starved); results are stored every 8 photos and the grid refreshes about every 3 s, so badges appear as photos are done; folders without read permission are skipped without asking and wait; Pause stops after the files being read (“Culling analysis is paused: 15 left.” with Resume); the rest is done on Resume or the next visit. “Analysing photos for culling: 4 of 19” with Pause, then “Culling analysis done: 19 photos measured for sharpness, exposure and look-alikes.” with OK. It writes only `photo_analysis` rows.

### Verified

Headless Chromium 141 with SwiftShader WebGPU and `?gpu=readback`, Vite dev servers of this branch and of main (`7b8b0f0`, then `6301e86` after merging it) (and their production builds for the timings), 4 CPU cores. Scratch scripts in the scratchpad's `ch/` folder. Test files: copies of the D700 and D80 NEFs, the Sony ILCE-6300 and ILCE-7M2 ARWs, the Fujifilm X100V RAF, the Ricoh GR III DNG and two JPEGs; and synthetic folders made by `make_folders.py` from copies of the D700, D80, ILCE-6300 and ILCE-7M2 camera previews (Gaussian “defocus”, line “shake”, shifted and zoomed frames, with EXIF capture time, SubSecTimeOriginal, make, model and ISO).

- **Unit tests** (`npx vitest run`: 91 files, 872 tests; main `6301e86` has 85 and 817, so 55 new; the schema-11/12/13 upgrade tests' fixtures now drop the new table first):
  - `engine/analysis/analyze.test.ts` (8): sharpness, peak and acuity fall strictly with Gaussian σ 0.7, 1, 1.5, 2, 3 on two synthetic scenes; defocus never reads as shake; line blurs of 9, 15 and 12 px at 0°, 90° and 30° read as shake with the smear's direction within 10°; a burst of sharp, σ 0.8, 1.6, 3 frames with noise ranks in that order; noise raises the floor more than 3× without being taken for detail; the AF point picks a defocused half when it is there, else the sharpest tiles, and Z's point is the box's centre; clipping and median; the analysis size.
  - `engine/analysis/hash.test.ts` (6): Hamming distance against bit-by-bit counting (200 random pairs), malformed hashes; hashes stable under blur, a 4 px shift, noise, +10 % brightness (within Medium's 10 bits) and a half-size copy (within Fairly strict's 6); six different scenes all further apart than Loose's 18 bits.
  - `engine/meta/capture.test.ts` (8): SubSecTimeOriginal / SubSecTime, SubjectArea as point, circle and rectangle, Sony's header-less maker note (FocusLocation, SequenceNumber), Nikon ShutterCount, Fujifilm FocusPixel and SequenceNumber, junk, boxes turned upright for each orientation.
  - `catalog/analysis.test.ts` (16): burst order by sub-second time over file names, then frame counter (over a rolled-over file counter), file number, name; look-alike groups at each strictness and time window, chains, different scenes and missing hashes; ranking with the exposure factor; stacks (best, clearly softer), unstacked look-alikes (“taken 0.5 s apart”), RAW+JPEG twins and virtual copies; “possibly soft” only with 8 peers of one camera and ISO band; shake, clipping and darkness reasons; suggestions never for picked, rejected, unanalysed photos or the best of a group, and only for the reasons chosen.
  - `catalog/analysis-catalog.test.ts` (9): the 13 → 14 upgrade leaves every other table and row identical and passes the integrity and foreign-key checks; dropping the table gives the version-13 catalog exactly; an older version refuses 14 without changing it; rows go with their photo; storing changes no photo row or sidecar record, copies read their original's, failed and older-`algo` rows, a photo removed meanwhile; sorting and filtering by sharpness, capture order within a second, the stack's hidden members in the ranking; Auto-stack by time (unchanged) and by look-alikes with the sharpest on top; **Apply and Undo** of suggested rejects (one call, sidecars scheduled, ratings untouched, a flag changed since is kept).
  - `library/cull-badges.test.ts` (7): defaults and stored choices, which badges a photo gets and their reasons, Z's point, the pass skipping folders without permission, the “…” badge for photos not analysed yet (none for one that couldn't be measured), and the keys (Ctrl+Z in the Library, Z in culling, no conflicts in either set). `analysis-catalog.test.ts` also checks that photos not analysed yet are marked pending (or failed), get no verdicts and are never suggested.
- **A fresh folder** (`e2e-cull.mjs`, 17 synthetic photos, **44 of 44**): nothing analysed while Open folder read the photos; then the pass's note, the “…” badges meanwhile, and all 17 measured (“Culling analysis done: 17 photos measured …”); the burst ordered by SubSecTimeOriginal (`BURST_0003, 0001, 0006, 0002, 0004, 0005`, unlike their names); Shake, Soft + Reject?, ☆ Best, Clipped and Look-alike badges where expected and not on the dark frame (off by default), tooltips with reasons (“93 % less sharp than BURST_0001.JPG …”, “Looks like BURST_0001.JPG, taken 0.2 s apart (1 of 64 bits differ)”); switching Shake off and Very dark on in the Culling menu; Esc closes it; Sharpest first / Least sharp first; the filters Possibly soft or shake (exactly the four soft or shaken burst frames and two of the second burst) and Not soft; Auto-stack by time with a 1 h gap made one stack of everything, with look-alikes 4 stacks (the two bursts apart from the singles); **strictness**: the three zoomed frames of one scene were one stack only at Loose (Strict 3 stacks, Medium 4, Loose 4; at Medium two of them are linked); with a 2 s gap and the sharpest on top, each burst became one stack with its sharpest frame on top; Select the best of each stack selected exactly one photo per stack with “P picks them”; Suggest rejects listed the four soft or shaken burst frames and two of the second burst, the library-relative reason added more, nothing was flagged before Apply; **Apply** rejected exactly those 6 (ratings and labels unchanged), the banner offered Undo, the rejected photo's sidecar had `lightbench:Flag="-1"`, no file was deleted; **Ctrl+Z** put every flag back and the sidecar to `Flag="0"`; in culling the info line showed the badges and “Z: sharpest area”, **Z** zoomed to the stored focus point and the hint line says so.
- **Real files** (`e2e-real.mjs`, **9 of 9**): all 8 readable files analysed (the Canon EOS 6D DNG isn't one LibRaw opens, as on main); the ILCE-6300's FocusLocation and the X100V's FocusPixel used as the subject; the D700's shutter count 49277 and 60 ms; no shake on any real file (highest 0.23, the GR III); the D80's clipped sky flagged; **Z on the ILCE-6300 zoomed to (0.5875, 0.3102)** and the 100 % RAW render showed the tower's roof there (screenshot checked by eye).
- **An existing library** (`build-v13.mjs` then `existing.mjs`, **12 of 12**): a library made with main `7b8b0f0` (schema 13: 21 photos in Photos and Other, ratings, a pick, a label, keywords, an edit, a stack, 21 sidecars; reopening it with main changed nothing), opened with this branch on the same origin with Other's permission refused: upgraded to 14; “Analysing photos for culling: 0 of 19” with Pause; paused at 15 left, nothing measured in 4 s; Resume finished “Culling analysis done: 19 photos measured …”; Other waited; **every catalog row, edit, keyword and thumbnail, and every sidecar's bytes and date unchanged**, no sidecar added; badges on the 19; reloaded with permission, Other was measured, still nothing else changed; a further visit showed no note.
- **Settings and keys** (`e2e-settings.mjs`, **7 of 7**): Settings › Culling's help text and “17 of 17 photos analysed”; a badge switched there is switched everywhere; “Measure all photos again…” measured all 17 again in the background with the same verdicts and nothing else changed; command search finds and runs the new actions.
- **Old edits render the same** (`identity.mjs`, 12 cases on the NEFs, the ARW and the JPEG, with crops, angles, spots, Tone Curve, Detail and the Colour Mixer at defaults): full-size and 1024 px export SHA-256, 512 px thumbnail, histogram statistics and the Develop canvas at fit and 100 %, **12 of 12 identical** to main `6301e86` (0.5.9, after merging it; also 12 of 12 against `7b8b0f0` before). **Open folder** (`thumbs.mjs`, the 8 real files and 17 synthetic ones opened afresh on both, against `6301e86`): **25 of 25 thumbnails byte-identical** and every catalog field identical except the file date of the test copies; Open folder's code (`import/probe.worker.ts`, `library/indexer.ts`) is main's.
- **Speed and responsiveness** (`e2e-big.mjs`, 1,000 synthetic 1,024 × 683 JPEGs in bursts of 3–8; production builds of main `6301e86` and this branch, run alternately; 4 cores; other work on the machine, so differences of a few seconds aren't meaningful):

  | | Main 6301e86 | This branch |
  |---|---|---|
  | Open folder, 1,000 photos (4 runs each) | 24.6, 25.1, 29.2, 29.5 s | 25.8, 28.8, 23.9, 26.1 s |
  | First photos in the grid | 1.1 s | 1.1 s |
  | Long tasks (> 50 ms) on the page while indexing | 0 | 0 |
  | Event-loop lag while indexing (a 50 ms timer): 95th / 99th / worst | 6 / 15 / 27 ms | 5 / 13 / 25 ms |
  | **The analysis pass afterwards** (4 runs) | — | 25.2, 20.1, 24.2, 24.7 s (20–25 ms a photo with two workers) |
  | Long tasks during the pass | — | 0 |
  | Event-loop lag during the pass: 95th / 99th / worst | — | 1–2 / 3–6 / 4–50 ms |
  | Sort Sharpest first, to the grid redrawn | — | 105 ms |
  | Filter Suggested rejects (290 of 1,000) | — | 157 ms |
  | Suggest rejects… listed | — | 755 ms |
  | Long tasks after the pass (sort, filter, dialog) | — | 0 |

  So Open folder takes main's time within the noise, and the analysis then takes about as long again for these small JPEGs, in the background. (With analysis inside Open folder, as first built, Open folder took 82.3 s against main's 49.2 s in an earlier, busier run of the same benchmark.) The last step of the change keeps the Culling menu's count from being queried while Open folder reads. The analysis per photo, timed in the page on the D700 preview: drawing at 1,536 px 4.9 ms, reading the pixels 28.8 ms, measuring 73.1 ms; at 1,024 px 2.1, 11.4 and 23.8 ms (medians of 10); a RAW file also has to be read and its preview decoded again (a RAW folder's pass wasn't timed). On the dev build (unoptimised React) the catalog worker's query with the culling assessment took 61–87 ms for the 1,000 photos, and Auto-stack with look-alikes of all 1,000 made 185 stacks in 6.0–7.4 s (in the worker; mostly creating stacks one by one, as Auto-stack by time does).
- `npm ci`, `npx tsc -b`, `npx oxlint` (the existing warnings only), `npx vitest run` and `npm run build` from clean all passed (oxlint: the two existing warnings, `syscheck/report.tsx` and `LibraryView.tsx`'s focused-photo effect).

### Limitations and unverified

- **Not tuned on real culls.** The thresholds come from synthetic Gaussian and line blurs of real previews and seven real RAWs; no labelled set of the owner's photos (sharp / soft / motion, bursts with the owner's pick) was available, so precision and recall are **unknown**. Real bursts from a camera (rather than synthetic ones) weren't available either.
- Sharpness is judged on the **camera preview**: in-camera sharpening and the preview's size matter, which is why verdicts are relative. Small previews (some cameras embed 640 px or less) measure differently; the size is stored but not yet used to adjust.
- AF points: only Sony FocusLocation (ILCE-6300) and Fujifilm FocusPixel (X100V) were seen in real files; EXIF SubjectArea is unit-tested only; Olympus through LibRaw is untested; Canon, Nikon and Pentax AF points aren't read. Sony models whose maker note is enciphered (0x9400 and others) give no sequence number here.
- Intentional shallow depth of field or panning can read as “possibly soft” or “shake” when no AF point is known.
- Look-alikes are hashes of the whole frame: re-framed or zoomed shots of one scene only group at Loose strictness, rotated ones not at all (embeddings are for Phase 4).
- With a crop in the edit, Z's point (on the uncropped preview) is applied to the 100 % render of the cropped photo, as clicking already was.
- Undo of an Apply lasts until the next Apply or a reload (the Library has no other undo).
- A smart-collection rule for these measures (research §5) wasn't added.

## Part 19: Snapshots

**What it does.** A **snapshot** is a named copy of a photo's full develop settings, process version included, with the time it was made. Snapshots are listed in a **Snapshots** panel in Develop's left side, between Presets and History (Lightroom's order). Clicking one applies it as one history step; hovering previews it on the photo; its menu updates, renames, deletes or **sets it as Before**, so `\` compares the photo with it. A History step's menu makes a snapshot of that step. They are kept in the catalog and in the photo's `.xmp` sidecar, so a library rebuilt from the folders gets them back. Nothing renders differently, so there is no new process version. This part is numbered 19; soft proofing is Part 20.

Owner's decisions (2026-10-14, after [research/SNAPSHOTS_PROOFING_SEARCH.md](research/SNAPSHOTS_PROOFING_SEARCH.md) Part A and Cowork's check of it, [research/cowork-2026-10-13b/E_SNAPSHOTS_PROOFING_SEARCH.md](research/cowork-2026-10-13b/E_SNAPSHOTS_PROOFING_SEARCH.md)):
1. **A snapshot** = a named copy of the full develop settings (process version included) plus its creation time. The default name is a date/time stamp, as Lightroom's is (secondary source); it can be renamed. Listed in Develop's left side near History. Click applies it as **one** undoable history step "Snapshot: name"; hover previews it; **Update with current settings**, **Rename**, **Delete** (undoable) and **Set as Before**. A History step's menu has "Create snapshot from this step".
2. **Keys:** **Ctrl+Alt+N** everywhere; **Ctrl+N** as well when LightBench runs as an installed app window (`display-mode: standalone`), where Chromium reserves no keys. Both in the shortcut registry, without conflicts.
3. **Storage:** a new catalog table (schema 15, safe for existing catalogs, rows deleted with the photo) **and** the photo's sidecar in LightBench's own namespace (`lightbench:Snapshots`), written by the existing sidecar rules, read back on Open folder, Import and Read changes with change detection. Lightroom's own snapshot field is undocumented: no `crs:` snapshot field is read or written.
4. **Virtual copies** have their own snapshots. Copy/Paste and Sync don't carry snapshots; presets don't include them.
5. **Old edits:** rendering never changes. An older snapshot applied keeps its process version (the photo as it was).

### What Lightroom and others do (facts used, and what isn't known)

| Fact | Source | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| Lightroom Classic's "Create a new snapshot" is Ctrl+N / Cmd+N | Adobe's LrC keyboard shortcut table (updated 2025-10-29), quoted by Cowork | Ctrl+N in the installed app |
| Snapshots are named with a date/time stamp by default and can be renamed when made; they travel with the file when metadata is saved; a snapshot is made available in all virtual copies | Julieanne Kost's blog (Adobe evangelist; secondary), quoted by Cowork | Default name; storing them in the sidecar. LightBench gives each virtual copy its own (owner's decision 4), unlike Lightroom |
| In a Chromium tab Ctrl+N (`IDC_NEW_WINDOW`) is reserved; "In Apps mode, no keys are reserved" | Chromium `browser_command_controller.cc` `IsReservedCommandOrKey`, quoted by Cowork. The Ctrl+N → IDC_NEW_WINDOW accelerator mapping and Edge were not checked | Ctrl+N only with `display-mode: standalone` |
| ExifTool 3.79 has no snapshot tag in its `crs` table | Research and Cowork | No `crs:` snapshot field |
| darktable's snapshots are session-only (at most 10), a rendered view plus a history position | darktable `src/libs/snapshots.c` (research) | Not followed: LightBench's are kept |
| ART keeps snapshots in its `.arp` sidecar (author's forum statement); RawTherapee doesn't keep them in `.pp3` | Cowork | Precedent for sidecar storage |
| **Not known:** Lightroom's XMP structure for snapshots; whether clicking a snapshot adds a history step in Lightroom; the exact default name format; whether Lightroom's Ctrl+N dialog asks for the name | Not found by research or Cowork | LightBench's own choices, below |

### The panel

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Place | Develop's left panel: Histogram, Presets, **Snapshots**, History. The heading's tooltip says what snapshots are and where they are kept. | Owner's decision 1; Lightroom's order (research) |
| List | Newest first, like History. Each row shows the name; its tooltip gives when it was made and its process version. The one set as Before has a "Before" mark. An empty list says "None yet. “+ New” or Ctrl+Alt+N saves this photo's settings by name." | LightBench |
| New | **+ New**, **Ctrl+Alt+N**, or **Ctrl+N** in the installed app window. The snapshot is made at once with the date and time as its name ("2026-10-14 13:05:22", local time; "(2)" added if that name is taken), and its name opens for editing with the text selected: type a name and press Enter, or Enter / Esc / click elsewhere to keep the date and time. Names are unique per photo (case-insensitive), at most 120 characters. | Owner's decision 1 (date/time default, renamable); naming while making, as Lightroom allows (secondary); format LightBench's |
| Apply | Click: the photo takes the snapshot's settings as **one** history step "Snapshot: name" (a step of its own even right after the same one), so Ctrl+Z goes back. The settings are applied whole, exactly as saved: an older process version stays, and the "Update to current process" offer appears for it (Part 1). A crop being edited is dropped first. | Owner's decisions 1 and 5 |
| Hover | Moving over a row (or focusing it) shows the photo with the snapshot on the main view and a "Preview: snapshot “name”" badge, as presets already do (Phase 2, Part 8); no history step. | Owner's decision 1 (the main view rather than a navigator, which LightBench doesn't have) |
| Menu | Right-click a row (or the Menu key / Shift+F10, or its ⋯ button): **Update with current settings**, **Rename…**, **Set as Before** / **Clear Before**, **Delete**. ↑ ↓ Home End move, Enter chooses, Esc or Tab closes. | Owner's decision 1 |
| Update | Replaces the snapshot's settings with the photo's current ones; its name, time and id stay. | Owner's decision 1 |
| Undo | Creating, renaming, updating and deleting show a line under the list ("Deleted “Warm”." with **Undo**). Undo (or **Ctrl+Z** while that line shows) puts the list back as it was. The line goes when the photo's history next changes (any edit), and then Ctrl+Z is the history's again. Snapshot changes are not history steps: they don't change the photo. | Owner's decision 1 (delete undoable); LightBench's design |
| Set as Before | Before / after (`\`, or the toolbar button) then shows the snapshot instead of the defaults, with the badge "Before: snapshot “name”" and the button's tooltip saying so, until **Clear Before**, deleting that snapshot, or another photo (or a reload). | Owner's decision 1; Lightroom's "Copy Snapshot Settings to Before" (research, search summary) |
| History step | Right-click a History step: **Create snapshot from this step** makes a snapshot of that step's settings (the photo doesn't change); its name opens for editing as for New. Each step's tooltip mentions it. | Owner's decision 1 ("if simple"); Lightroom's History "Create Snapshot" is from research [unconfirmed] |
| Keyboard and screen readers | Rows and menu items are buttons; the menu is `role="menu"` with `menuitem`s and returns the focus when it closes; the name field has an accessible name; the undo line is `role="status"`. | LightBench |

### Keys

| Action | Where | Both shortcut sets |
|---|---|---|
| New snapshot | Develop, and with the Tone Curve's, Colour Mixer's or Remove brush's on-photo tool open | **Ctrl+Alt+N** |
| New snapshot (installed app window only) | the same | **Ctrl+N**, only while `matchMedia('(display-mode: standalone)')` matches (the manifest's display mode). In a tab LightBench doesn't take it, so the browser's own Ctrl+N (a new window) is untouched. |

- The registry has a new flag for actions that work only in the installed app (`standalone` in `settings/actions.ts`). `matchAction` skips them unless the page runs standalone (`settings/standalone.ts`, asked at each key press); command search lists them only there. Settings → Keyboard lists both, so either can be changed.
- Not while cropping or drawing Guided Upright lines (the crop isn't applied yet). Ctrl+Alt+Shift+N (end the colour mixer tool) is a different chord and still works.
- The keymap test checks both keys in all four contexts, that Ctrl+N does nothing in a tab and nothing outside Develop, and that both sets have no conflicts.
- Whether `preventDefault()` on Ctrl+N stops an installed Chrome or Edge window from also opening a new window was **not tested** (no installed app in the test browser; Cowork's unresolved item 3). The source says the page gets the key first.

### What is stored

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| A snapshot | `{ id, name, created, settings }`: a random id (kept by rename and update, so lists can be merged), the name, when it was made (ms), and the full `DevelopSettings` (process version, geometry, spots, curves… everything the edit holds). | Owner's decision 1 |
| Catalog: schema 15 | `snapshots (photo_id REFERENCES photos ON DELETE CASCADE, uid, name, settings, created_at, PRIMARY KEY (photo_id, uid))`. A new table only: every other table and row is unchanged by the upgrade (unit test). Dropping it and setting `user_version` 14 gives the version-14 catalog exactly; 0.6.0 refuses the version-15 catalog without changing it (its existing check; e2e below). Rows go with their photo, and a copy's with the copy. Backups include the table. | Owner's decision 3 |
| Sidecar | `lightbench:Snapshots` on the photo's `rdf:Description`: a JSON list (oldest first) of `{ "id", "name", "created" (ISO 8601 UTC), "settings" }`, in LightBench's namespace as `lightbench:Develop` and `lightbench:Versions` are. Removed when the last snapshot is deleted. Written by the existing rules: whenever the photo's sidecar is written (after a change, with folder permission, held when another app changed the file). Other apps' fields are kept. **No `crs:` snapshot field** is read or written. | Owner's decision 3 |
| Virtual copies | Each copy has its own list (a new copy starts with none). In the original's sidecar each copy's list is `snapshots` inside its entry in `lightbench:Versions`, present only when it has some, so copies without snapshots are written as before. | Owner's decision 4 |
| Not carried | Snapshots are not part of `DevelopSettings`, so Copy/Paste, Sync, Auto Sync, presets, exports' embedded XMP and new virtual copies never carry them. | Owner's decision 4 |

### Reading them back

| Behaviour | Detail |
|---|---|
| Open folder / Import | A new photo's sidecar brings its snapshots with it (`applySidecar`), and its virtual copies come back with theirs (`restoreVersions`). A RAW twin's borrowed sidecar gives a JPEG none. |
| Damaged data | A list that isn't valid JSON or isn't a list is ignored (nothing read; the rest of the sidecar reads as before) and left in the file until the photo has snapshots of its own. Entries without a name or settings are left out; a missing id is made from the time and name (the same file always gives the same id); a bad time reads as 1970; a repeated id keeps the first. A copy's damaged list is dropped. |
| Changed by another app | Snapshots join the fields compared to notice another app's change (Phase 2, "Sidecars changed by other apps"). Another LightBench adding, renaming, updating or deleting one raises the usual notice; **Read changes** merges by snapshot: what the other app added, changed or deleted is taken, LightBench's own new or changed ones that it didn't touch stay (both changed one: the other app's wins, as for every field). The Develop panel shows the result at once. |
| Apps that drop LightBench's namespace | A file without `lightbench:Snapshots` and without `lightbench:Develop` says nothing about snapshots, so they are kept (as edits are). A file with LightBench's settings but no list means "none". |
| Records from before this part | A record made before schema 15 doesn't know snapshots, so the catalog's own list is the base: snapshots in a file that the catalog doesn't have (written by another, newer LightBench) count as a change to read. And before a sidecar whose record doesn't know snapshots is rewritten, any snapshots in it that the catalog lacks are taken into the library first, so writing never drops them. |

### Old edits

Nothing in the engine changed: rendering, exports, thumbnails and histograms of every existing edit are byte-identical (below). An existing library opens unchanged: no catalog row, edit, history, thumbnail or sidecar changes until a snapshot is made. A snapshot of an older process version applied later keeps that version, so the photo looks as it did when the snapshot was taken.

### Also changed

| Behaviour | Detail |
|---|---|
| History steps | Each step's tooltip now reads "Click to go back to this step · right-click to make a snapshot of it". |
| Develop's left panel | History keeps at least about three steps visible; when the panel is too short for the histogram, presets, snapshots and History together, the panel scrolls instead of squeezing History to nothing (seen on a 900 px window while testing). |
| "Changed by another app" notice | Now says Read changes also takes "another LightBench's edits and snapshots". |

### Verified

Headless Chromium with SwiftShader WebGPU and `?gpu=readback`, Vite dev servers of this branch and of main `5307029` (0.6.0, served from its own copy of the tree), one test at a time; another session's tests shared the machine for part of the time. Scratch scripts in the scratchpad's `sn/` folder (copied from `cg/` and `ch/`). Test photos: copies of `test-srgb.jpg` (1600 × 1072) and `test-adobe.jpg`; for the identity check the D700, D80 and ILCE-6300 RAWs and the JPEG.

- **Unit tests** (`npx vitest run`: **905 tests in 95 files**; main `5307029` has 872 in 91, so 33 new; the schema-11 to 14 upgrade tests' fixtures now also drop the new table, and their schema-version expectations say 15):
  - `catalog/snapshots-catalog.test.ts` (7): the 14 → 15 upgrade adds an empty `snapshots` table and leaves every other table and row identical, with the integrity and foreign-key checks passing, and opening again changes nothing; **taking it back** (dropping the table, `user_version` 14) gives the version-14 catalog exactly, and it upgrades again; a version that doesn't know 15 refuses it without changing it; rows go with their photo and a copy's with the copy; saving and listing (newest first; a row that can't be read is left out); **virtual copies**: a new copy starts with none, the original's sidecar source holds the original's list and each copy's inside its version entry (none when a copy has no snapshots), a fresh library's restored copy gets its own back, a copy's damaged list is ignored; removing a photo removes its and its copies' snapshots.
  - `develop/snapshots.test.ts` (13): the date/time name; unique names (case-insensitive, "(2)", a rename to its own name is not a clash), trimming and the 120-character limit; a new snapshot holds a **copy** of the settings; newest first; **round trip** sidecar form → JSON → read → snapshots (Unicode, quotes, `<` and `&` in names; milliseconds kept), the change-detection key independent of order; **damaged data ignored**: not JSON, not a list, an empty string, entries without a name or settings, a blank name, settings that aren't an object, a repeated id, a missing id (made from time and name, the same every time) and a bad time (1970); the Read changes merge (their additions, changes and deletions; LightBench's own new and changed ones kept; one LightBench deleted stays deleted; both changed: theirs); **applying** a process-1 snapshot on a process-2 photo gives one step (twice: two steps), keeps process 1 and its rendering parameters, and two undos give back the current version; **Set as Before** shows the snapshot's settings (crop and process version included), not the defaults; snapshots aren't a paste item or a settings key.
  - `sidecar/snapshots-sidecar.test.ts` (11): `lightbench:Snapshots` written and read back exactly, no `crs:` snapshot field; an empty list removes the field, undefined leaves it, an unchanged list rewrites nothing; Lightroom's fields kept when adding it; a damaged list read as nothing and flagged, the rest of the file read; mixed good and bad entries; a copy's list in `lightbench:Versions` and a copy's damaged list dropped; **change detection**: a written file matches its catalog; another LightBench's rename, addition and deletion is a change, and the merge keeps LightBench's new one; an app that drops the namespace deletes nothing; a record from before schema 15 treats unknown snapshots in the file as a change and an ordinary change as only that; no record and an older file: taken silently without claiming its snapshots; a damaged list is no change.
  - `settings/keymap.test.ts` (+1): Ctrl+Alt+N makes a snapshot in Develop and the Tone Curve, Colour Mixer and Remove contexts, in a tab and in the app; **Ctrl+N only with standalone**; neither while cropping, drawing Guided Upright lines, in the Library, culling, Compare or Survey; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+N still ends the mixer tool; **no conflicts in either shortcut set**. `settings/standalone.test.ts` (1): `display-mode: standalone` recognised, anything else (another query, an exception, no `matchMedia`) is not.
- **In Develop** (`e2e-snapshots.mjs`, **42 of 42** on the final code; earlier runs found the test's own problems, a stale locator after relaunching the browser and checks run before D was ready, and one real one, fixed: after choosing from a snapshot's menu the focus went back to its row and started a preview, so `\` then showed the snapshot instead of the photo; now only keyboard focus previews):
  - The panel sits between Presets and History; empty, it says how to make one. **+ New** opened the name field with "2026-10-14 15:39:…" selected; typing "Warm" + Enter kept it, and the catalog held the full settings (Exposure +1.50, process 2).
  - **Ctrl+Alt+N** made one named with the date and time. **Ctrl+N** in the tab made none; with `display-mode: standalone` emulated (a `matchMedia` override) it made "App". Listed newest first.
  - **Hover** showed "Preview: snapshot “Warm”" and the photo as in the snapshot (canvas mean 172.93 against 172.91 when Warm was the photo's state), with no history step; leaving gave the photo back (87.58 = 87.58).
  - **Click** added exactly one step "Snapshot: Warm" (Exposure +1.50, mean as Warm); Ctrl+Z went back in one step (−1.50), Ctrl+Y forward.
  - **Set as Before** marked the row; `\` showed "Before: snapshot “Warm”" and the snapshot's pixels (172.93) while the photo was at −1.50 EV; `\` again showed the photo (87.58); after **Clear Before**, `\` showed the defaults (130.86).
  - **Rename…** to "Cool"; Esc while renaming kept the name. **Update with current settings** changed Cool from −1.50 to −0.50 EV, said so, and its **Undo** put −1.50 back.
  - **Delete** removed Warm (and its Before); **Ctrl+Z** right after brought it back with the same id and no history change; Delete then the **Undo** button brought Cool back.
  - A History step's menu (the first step, "Import") made "Original" with Exposure 0 and no history step.
  - `C.xmp` held `lightbench:Snapshots` with the four (ids, times, settings with process version), and no `crs:` snapshot field.
  - **Copy/Paste** from C to D pasted the settings ("Paste settings") and gave D no snapshots; `D.xmp` has no Snapshots field.
  - **After a reload** the list was the same; Before wasn't kept.
  - **A virtual copy** (Ctrl+') started with none; "Copy look" made on it stayed the copy's; `C.xmp` held it inside the copy's `lightbench:Versions` entry, the original's four unchanged.
  - **A fresh library** (a new browser profile, the same JPEGs and the two sidecars, Open folder): C's four snapshots came back with their names, settings and ids, the copy came back with "Copy look", D with none; applying Warm there gave "Snapshot: Warm" at +1.50 EV.
  - **Another LightBench's change:** the script renamed Cool, added "From elsewhere" and deleted "Original" in `C.xmp`, while LightBench made "Mine meanwhile"; the notice "1 photo was changed by another app" appeared; **Read changes** gave exactly Cool (renamed elsewhere), From elsewhere, Mine meanwhile, Warm and the date/time one, in the catalog and the panel at once, and the file was then rewritten with that list.
  - Screenshots checked by eye: the panel, the Before badge with the toolbar's "Showing before", the History with three steps visible under the snapshots.
- **An existing library** (`build-v14.mjs` with main `5307029`, then `existing-v15.mjs` and `refuse-v14.mjs` with this branch, one persistent profile on the same origin; 3 JPEGs with ratings, a pick, a label, keywords, an edit with a three-step history, a virtual copy and three sidecars; reopening it with main changed nothing): **8 of 8** then **3 of 3**: it opened without an error and was upgraded (the snapshots table answers, empty); **every catalog row, edit, history, keyword and thumbnail and every sidecar's bytes and date unchanged**, no sidecar added; the edited photo opened in Develop with its history (Contrast, Exposure, Import) and an empty Snapshots panel; Ctrl+Alt+N stored a snapshot (Contrast +20) and `E1.xmp` gained `lightbench:Snapshots` with every other field's name and value as main wrote them (only `xmp:MetadataDate` changed, and the order of the start tag's attributes: the namespace declarations came first after the rewrite. This part doesn't change how files are serialised; whether main reorders them the same way when it rewrites a file it read wasn't checked). Main opened on the same origin then refused the library with its message "This library was saved by a newer version of LightBench…" and changed no sidecar; this branch opened it again with the snapshot there.
- **Old edits render the same** (`identity.mjs` from `cg/`, unchanged: 15 edits on the D700, D80, ILCE-6300 and the JPEG, with crops, angles, spots, Tone Curve, Detail, the Colour Mixer with Point Color, and a Colour Grading at its defaults; for each the full-size and 1024 px export SHA-256, the 512 px thumbnail, histogram statistics and the Develop canvas at fit and 100 %): **all 90 hashes identical to main `5307029`**. In both full runs the fifteenth edit (D80 with a Colour Grading at defaults) failed the same way on both servers (`NotFoundError` reading B.NEF from the test page's own OPFS folder after about 40 minutes; the cause wasn't found); run again alone on each server, its six hashes were identical too (full size `f34d827d0e1ed7bc…`, as in Part 17). Rendering code is untouched by this part (no engine or shader file changed).
- From a clean `npm ci`: `npx tsc -b`, `npx oxlint` (only the two existing warnings, `syscheck/report.tsx` and `LibraryView.tsx`), `npx vitest run` (905 passed) and `npm run build` passed. The main chunk is 1,128.74 kB (360.12 kB gzipped).

### Limitations and unverified

- **Lightroom's snapshot XMP field is unknown** (not in ExifTool 3.79; Cowork found none), so Lightroom's snapshots aren't read and LightBench's aren't visible in Lightroom. Lightroom shares snapshots between a photo and its virtual copies (secondary source); LightBench keeps them per copy, by the owner's decision.
