Masks are drawn and changed on the photo (Shift+W, or K, M, Shift+M, Shift+J, Shift+Q to start one), shown with an overlay in Lightroom's six modes, kept in the edit, the history, the catalog and the `lightbench:` sidecar, and rendered by the GPU in Develop, thumbnails, the histogram, Auto and Export. With no masks nothing renders differently (below). This is the first of two builds: the AI Depth range mask (Depth Anything V2 Small, its download, the depth map inside the .xmp) came next ([Part 24](#part-24-ai-depth-range-mask-and-model-delivery)), in the place the data model and the engine left for it.

The brief is [research/MASKING.md](research/MASKING.md); the owner's decisions 1–14 (2026-10-13/14) recorded at its end are followed throughout; Adobe's facts come from Cowork's check, [research/cowork-2026-10-13b/B_MASKING.md](research/cowork-2026-10-13b/B_MASKING.md).

### Owner's decisions and how they are built

| # | Decision | Built as |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Control points in sensor coordinates; shapes drawn in the straightened frame | Every point (gradient ends, ellipse centre, brush dabs) is stored as x / sensor width, y / sensor height, as the Remove spots; sizes as fractions of the sensor's shorter side. Each drawing maps them forward through its own geometry (crop, angle, Upright, flips, lens correction) and evaluates the shape in its target pixels, so a linear gradient stays straight and a radial gradient an ellipse on screen, and both stay on their content. |
| 2 | Brush, Linear, Radial, Luminance range, Colour range; the ten local sliders; Feather and Refine on every mask; Add/Subtract/Intersect and Invert | All of them. Spatial sliders (Texture, Clarity, …) are left for when their global tools exist (decision 11): the planes the shader reads have room. |
| 3 | darktable's maths, called LightBench's own | Add = max, Intersect = min, Subtract = a·(1 − c) (darktable `group.c`); darktable's quadratic circle falloff, its sigmoidal (erf) gradient, its brush (solid core, linear falloff). No text calls it Lightroom-compatible. |
| 4 | Range masks measure after the global white balance and exposure, before tone and colour | They read the scene × the white-balance fix × the exposure gain, exactly what the display shader has just before the tone equalizer (`mask-math.ts` `measured`; GPU: `mask.wgsl` `measured`). Basic's tone sliders, the curve, the mixer, grading and the masks' own sliders never move them. Colour range in **OkLab** (below). |
| 5 | Local Contrast scene-referred, as color balance rgb's | Y' = 0.1845·(Y / 0.1845)^γ before the sigmoid, γ = 2^(contrast × the process version's contrast spread), the spread the global Contrast slider's sigmoid uses; the colour's ratios kept. |
| 6 | Docked group, group eye and per-mask eyes; red overlay with a colour-blind-safe alternative; Lightroom's six modes; Adobe-confirmed keys | The Masks group with its group eye, an eye per mask (both views only, as Part 21's eyes), overlay red by default with five Okabe–Ito colours, the six modes by Adobe's names, and the keys below. |
| 7 | Copy/Paste: Masks unticked by default, pasted masks added; never in Auto Sync; presets hold gradients and range masks, not brush strokes | `paste.ts` item **Masks** (group Masks), unticked; ticked, `appendMasks` adds them after the photo's own with new ids (up to 64); `NOT_AUTO_SYNCED`; New preset leaves Masks unticked and drops any mask with a brush part when it saves (`presetMasks`); a preset's Amount scales the masks it adds (their Amount). |
| 8 | Read Lightroom's `crs:` masks best-effort (experimental); warn about `.acr`; never write `crs:` masks | `sidecar/lightroomMasks.ts` reads `crs:MaskGroupBasedCorrections` when a photo is imported without a LightBench edit; the Masks group warns when a `.acr` file sits next to the photo; nothing writes `crs:` masks (unit test). |
| 9 | Caps: 64 masks, 32 parts, 10,000 dabs per mask | Enforced on every load and before every change, with a message saying which cap and what to do. |
| 11 | Spatial sliders later | Not in this build. |
| 12 | Design around: range masks apply to the whole photo; no automatic colour-aware brush | Below. |
| 13 | Pen pressure → size or flow, off by default | The brush's **Pen pressure** menu (Off, Changes the size, Changes the flow), from Pointer Events' `pressure`, stored per dab. |
| 14 | darktable export of masks later | Not in this build. |

### The design-around (owner's decision 12, binding)

**Facts** (from Cowork's patent check, B_MASKING.md item 5; Google Patents' status is "an assumption and is not a legal conclusion"; this is not legal advice): **US 11,223,744 B2** (Adobe, "Local color range selection", filed 2017-10-17, active, adjusted expiry 2037-12-02); its claim 1 weights an adjustment "by a combination of the value of the spatial mask and the value of the range mask". **US 8,837,849 B2** (Nik/Google, "Method for noise-robust color changes in digital images", priority 2007-06-26, active, expiry 2031-10-04); its claim 1 covers colour-distance-driven local enhancement after an edge-preserving pre-filter. The 2001 U Point family has expired.

**How LightBench stays clear of them:**
- **A mask is either shapes or ranges.** Its first part decides: a shape mask (Brush, Linear, Radial) takes only shapes, a range mask (Luminance, Colour) only ranges. A range is never intersected with or subtracted from a shape, and a shape never joins a range mask. Ranges combine with ranges (a bright **and** blue mask: no spatial mask is involved).
- **Enforced in three places:** the model (`masks.ts` `canAdd`, `refuseReason`), the panel (the **Part to combine** menu lists only the mask's family), and every load (`normalizeMask` drops a part of the other family, so an edit from elsewhere, a damaged one or a Lightroom sidecar can't bring one in; Lightroom's own "range mask on a correction" is left out and the reader says so).
- **Separate masks still stack:** a gradient mask and a luminance mask each add their own sliders where they are.
- **No automatic colour-aware brush:** the brush paints what it is told; nothing follows colours or edges while painting. **Refine** (decision 2) is a guided filter on the photo's **luminance**, applied to the finished mask; it uses no reference colour and no colour distance. It is flagged here for the owner's advice anyway, as an edge-aware step applied to drawn masks.
- **The interface** says only "Range masks apply to the whole photo … make it a separate mask; masks stack" (`help.ts` `RANGE_NOTE`). No patent talk.
- **Depth ∩ shape** is held back too (decision 12) for the Depth build: the model treats Depth as a range kind when it lands.

### Using it

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Open | **Shift+W**, the toolbar's **Masks** button, or **Edit on photo…** in the group: the masking tool on the photo (Develop listens to the masking keys). Again, Enter or **Done** finishes. | Adobe's shortcut table: "Open / close Masking: Shift + W" |
| New mask | **K** Brush, **M** Linear gradient, **Shift+M** Radial gradient, **Shift+J** Colour range, **Shift+Q** Luminance range, or the five buttons (44 px targets). Then: drag on the photo from where the effect is full to where it ends (Linear); drag from the centre outwards (Radial; a click makes a circle); paint (Brush); click a colour (Colour range; **Shift-click** adds samples, up to five); a luminance range starts on the brighter half and a click on the photo centres it on that tone. Each is one history step, "New mask: Radial 1". | Adobe's shortcut table (K, M, Shift+M, Shift+J, Shift+Q; "up to five color samples by holding Shift") |
| Names | By type: Brush 1, Linear 1, Radial 2, Colour range 1… (Lightroom users asked for names by type, MASKING.md). Double-click a name to rename (one step). | MASKING.md |
| The list | Each mask: its eye, its name and parts. The selected mask shows its parts, each with how it combines (+ Add, − Subtract, ∩ Intersect: a menu), and **Invert**. Under the list: a **Part to combine** menu and **+ Add**, **− Subtract**, **∩ Intersect** buttons; then the mask's **Invert**, **Duplicate**, **Delete**, **Amount**, **Feather**, **Refine** and its sliders in Basic's groups (White balance, Tone, Presence) with Basic's hints, and **Reset mask sliders**. | MASKING.md "UX" |
| Brush | **Paint** / **Erase** (Alt-drag erases while Alt is held, Adobe's "Alt-drag"), **Size** (2–1500 px of the full-size photo, [ and ]), **Feather** ({ and }), **Flow**, **Density**, **Pen pressure** (off by default). Each stroke is one step ("Brush 1: brush stroke", "… erase"); the mask is shown while painting. | Adobe's table ([ ], Shift+[ ], Alt-drag) |
| Handles | The selected gradient's handles: Linear's Full and Zero points and its middle (moves both); Radial's centre and the ends of its two radii (an end of the long radius also turns it). Each drag is one step ("Radial 1: move Radial gradient"), the mask shown while dragging. Pins (one per mask with a shape) select a mask; **H** shows the pins of all masks or only the selected one's. Hits within 22 px (44 px targets: Apple's **default** control size, not its minimum, B_MASKING.md item 9). | LightBench; Adobe's H |
| Overlay | **O** shows or hides the selected mask over the photo; **Shift+O** the next colour; **Alt+O** the next mode: **Color Overlay**, **Color Overlay on B&W**, **Image on B&W**, **Image on Black**, **Image on White**, **White on Black** (Adobe's names, B_MASKING.md item 1d); colour Red (default) or one of five colour-blind-safe Okabe–Ito colours; opacity 10–100 %. Develop's canvas only: never in the histogram, thumbnails or exports. | Adobe's table (O, Shift+O, Alt+O); Okabe & Ito 2002 |
| Eyes | The group's eye shows the photo without its masks; each mask's eye without that mask. Views only, like Part 21's: nothing saved, no history step; the "Hidden: …" badge says "Masks" or "mask Radial 1". | Part 21 |
| Amount | 0–200 %, default 100 %: scales all the mask's sliders. **The 0–200 % range is an assumption** (LightCraft's clamp); Adobe documents no range (B_MASKING.md item 1c). The slider's tooltip says so. | LightCraft |
| Touch and pen | No modifier keys on touch: Erase and Add/Subtract/Intersect are buttons; two fingers move a zoomed photo; pen pressure as above. | MASKING.md "Touch and pen" |
| Help | Every mask slider has a tooltip (`help.ts` `MASK_HELP`; the local sliders reuse Basic's, saying they act only in the mask and add to Basic's); local Temp/Tint say they are relative (−100…100, not kelvin). | Owner's rule (guidance) |

### Keys (`settings/actions.ts`, context **Develop: masking**, the same in both sets)

| Action | Key | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Masking on the photo / finish | Shift+W (also Enter to finish) | Adobe's table |
| New Brush / Linear / Radial / Colour range / Luminance range mask | K / M / Shift+M / Shift+J / Shift+Q (in Develop and while masking) | Adobe's table |
| Overlay on/off, next colour, next mode | O, Shift+O, Alt+O | Adobe's table |
| Pins | H | Adobe's table |
| Brush size / feather | [ ] / Shift+[ Shift+] | Adobe's table |
| Invert the selected mask | ' | **secondary source** (Julieanne Kost's blog; not in Adobe's table) |
| New mask of the selected kind | N | **secondary source** (Kost) |
| Delete the selected part (or the mask) | Delete, Backspace | LightBench |
| Stop drawing, deselect, or finish | Esc | LightBench |
| Brush erase on/off | none (Alt-drag erases while held) | Adobe's table (Alt-drag) |

The masking context replaces Develop's while masking (as cropping does), so O and Shift+O keep Develop's clipping keys outside it. The keymap test checks every key above, both sets, and that the secondary-source actions say so in their names.

### Data model (`engine/develop/masks.ts`)

```ts
masks?: LocalMask[]          // in DevelopSettings; absent when there are none
LocalMask { id, name, invert, amount /* 0–200 */, feather /* 0–100 */, refine /* 0–100 */, components, adjust }
MaskComponent { id, op: 'add' | 'subtract' | 'intersect', invert, shape }
shape: { kind: 'linear', full: Pt, zero: Pt }
     | { kind: 'radial', center: Pt, rx, ry /* × short side */, angle /* °, sensor frame */, feather }
     | { kind: 'brush', strokes: { size, feather, flow, density, erase, dabs: number[] /* x0,y0,… */, pressure?, pressureTo? }[] }
     | { kind: 'luminance', range: [a, b, c, d] /* L* trapezoid */ }
     | { kind: 'colour', samples: [L, a, b][] /* OkLab, ≤ 5 */, refine }
     | { kind: string, … }    // a kind from a newer LightBench, or the AI raster (`RasterShape`): kept, not rendered
adjust: { temperature?, tint?, exposure?, contrast?, highlights?, shadows?, whites?, blacks?, saturation?, vibrance? }
```

- **Brush strokes store their dabs** (one every quarter radius, LightCraft's spacing), so the 10,000-dab cap counts what is stored and a stroke renders the same everywhere. Coordinates are rounded to 1e-5 (0.06 px on a 6000 px sensor).
- **Unknown kinds** (a newer LightBench, or the next build's Depth raster) are kept as they are; a mask holding one isn't rendered and the panel says "made with a newer LightBench: kept, not shown".
- **How AI masks plug in** (MASKING.md): a component kind whose shape carries `{ model, modelVersion, raster: { width, height, encoding, data } }` (`RasterShape`), stored inside the edit and so inside the .xmp (decision 10). The engine's component pass has a slot for it: a raster component is one more kind in `mask.wgsl` `componentValue`, sampling an uploaded texture through the same sensor → target mapping the shapes use. The Depth build adds the texture and that case.
- **Stored in** the catalog's edit and history (every step), `lightbench:Develop` in the sidecar (it is part of the settings), snapshots (full settings), virtual copies (their own edits). Paste, presets and the background developer go through the same settings.

### Engine (`engine/gpu/mask-stage.ts`, `mask.wgsl`, `mask-brush.wgsl`; `engine/develop/mask-render.ts`, `mask-math.ts`)

| Step | Detail |
|---|---|
| Pipeline placement | Masks act in the display pass, in scene-linear Rec.2020 **before the sigmoid**, as MASKING.md recommends: white-balance fix → exposure → (range masks measure here) → local Temp/Tint → local Exposure → the tone equalizer with the global **and local** bands → local Contrast → Saturation and Vibrance (global + local) → Colour Mixer → Colour Grading → sigmoid → Tone Curve. The Remove spots are untouched: they work on the scene before the geometry, masks afterwards on each drawing. |
| Preparing a drawing | `prepareMasks` maps each mask's points into the drawing's target pixels through its `ShownMap` (the drawing's geometry and lens map, the one the Remove pins use), weights the mask's sliders by its Amount, sizes Feather and Refine on the drawn photo's long edge, and leaves out masks with no effect (unless the overlay shows one) and masks with unknown parts. Brush dabs mapped for a drawing are kept per stroke, so slider drags don't map them again. |
| Rasterising | Per 1024 px tile of the drawing (Develop's canvas, the histogram, a thumbnail, an export at full size), over the tile plus a margin wide enough for the filters: each part is evaluated per target pixel on the GPU (`mask.wgsl` `component`: the gradients analytically, the ranges from the photo read through the drawing's texture map, the brush from its dabs drawn as blended quads into an r16float texture, `mask-brush.wgsl`) and combined; then the mask's Invert, Feather (a Gaussian on a grid reduced by a power of two, bilinear back) and Refine (He and Sun's fast guided filter on a reduced grid, with the full-resolution log-luminance guide), then its weighted sliders are added into three vec4 per pixel ("planes"). The display shader reads the planes (binding 14) at its pixel. |
| Local tone | Highlights, Shadows, Whites and Blacks change the tone equalizer's nine bands per pixel (global + Σ alpha × local, with the process version's band weights and range); the bands are fitted to its eight factors with the least-squares fit as one 8 × 9 matrix (`toneEqualizerPinv`), only where a mask sets a tone slider. The EIGF mask's statistics are scaled by the pixel's local exposure too. |
| Old edits | With no masks the shader's uniform is all zero and `developPreSigmoid` takes its old path; nothing else changes (identity, below). An edit with masks but none in effect draws as before too. |
| Robustness (Part 14) | The mask passes are recorded in the same command buffers as the drawing, so `guarded` covers them in Export, the background developer and the 100 % view; the foreground gate covers Develop's. `unload()` frees the mask buffers and textures (and zeroes the masks' uniform); a new engine after a lost device makes its own. |
| Process version | **None needed**: masks are a new tool, absent from old edits. Their constants are in `ProcessParams.masks` (`MASKS_V1`, shared by versions 1 and 2), so a later change that alters a saved mask's pixels gets a new version. |

**Colour range in OkLab, not JzCzhz.** The measured colours are scene-referred with no absolute luminance, which JzAzBz's PQ curve needs (darktable assumes one); OkLab's hue is close to uniform where CIELAB bends in the blues (Ottosson 2020), it is two 3 × 3 matrices and a cube root, and LightCraft uses it for the same job. The distance weighs lightness half as much as the colour axes, so a sky's brighter and darker parts are picked together; Refine 0–100 sets the tolerance (ΔE 0.03–0.25: full within half of it, nothing beyond it).

**Luminance range on CIE L\*** of the measured luminance (0–100, capped at 100), through darktable's trapezoid: Range dark end, bright end and Smoothness in the panel.

### Lightroom (`sidecar/lightroomMasks.ts`, `sidecar/acr.ts`)

- **Read, experimental, at import:** a photo imported with a sidecar holding `crs:MaskGroupBasedCorrections` and no LightBench edit gets those masks (one background-developer step "Lightroom masks (experimental)", which also renders its thumbnail), and the Masks group says they came from Lightroom and are approximate. Masks are not read again when the sidecar changes later (Read changes reads the other fields as before).
- **What is known and what is guessed:** the field names are ExifTool's (3.79); their meanings are LightCraft's observations and are **guesses**, marked so in the code: `What` values Mask/Gradient (FullX/Y, ZeroX/Y), Mask/CircularGradient (Top/Left/Bottom/Right, Angle, Feather, Flipped = inverted), Mask/Paint (Dabs "d x y", Radius, Flow, CenterWeight, MaskValue ≤ 0 = erase), Mask/RangeMask with CorrectionRangeMask Type 2 and LumRange (a luminance range); MaskBlendMode 0/1/2 = add/subtract/intersect (Cowork: "Don't hard-code 0/1/2 … as fact"); Local* as fractions (×100; LocalExposure2012 × 4 EV); CorrectionAmount 1 = 100 %; coordinates as fractions of the photo as stored (the frame of Lightroom's crop edges, Part 7), which is LightBench's sensor frame.
- **Left out, and said so** (the console names each): AI masks (Mask/Image: the pixels aren't in the XMP), colour and depth ranges (encodings unknown), a correction's own range mask and a range part combined with shapes (the design-around), sliders LightBench lacks in masks (Texture, Clarity, Dehaze, …), inactive corrections.
- **Never written:** LightBench's masks go only in `lightbench:Develop`; a sidecar's `crs:` masks are kept as they are when LightBench rewrites it (unit test).
- **`.acr`:** Adobe: Lightroom "writes the heavy edits to the ACR sidecar while keeping the XMP sidecar lightweight" when there are "heavy edits, masks, or AI settings" (B_MASKING.md item 4i). When Develop opens a photo whose folder holds `NAME.acr` (any case), the Masks group says so: its masks can't be read.
- No Lightroom-written sidecar with masks was available: everything here was tested on a **synthetic** sidecar written from ExifTool's names.

### Files

New: `engine/develop/masks.ts` (model, checks, caps, design-around, paste and preset helpers), `mask-math.ts` (the maths, CPU twin), `mask-render.ts` (a drawing's masks), `engine/gpu/mask-stage.ts`, `mask.wgsl`, `mask-brush.wgsl`, `develop/MasksPanel.tsx`, `MaskOverlay.tsx`, `useMasking.ts`, `maskUi.ts`, `sidecar/lightroomMasks.ts`, `sidecar/acr.ts`; tests `mask-math.test.ts`, `masks.test.ts`, `mask-render.test.ts`, `sidecar/masks-sidecar.test.ts`, `develop/masks-paste.test.ts`. Changed: `develop-display.wgsl` (`developMasked`, the overlay, bindings 14–16), `develop-engine.ts` (`writeMasks`, the tiled drawing with masks, `sampleMaskInput`, unload/destroy), `settings.ts` (`masks`, `MasksGpu`), `process.ts` (`MaskParams`), `tone-equalizer.ts` (`toneEqualizerPinv`), `indicators.ts` (`maskOverlay`), `DevelopView.tsx`, `ToolStack.tsx`, `tools.tsx`, `sectionEyes.ts` (the Masks group's eye), `paste.ts`, `presets.ts`, `help.ts`, `actions.ts`, `xmp.ts`, `import/run.ts`, `bench/run.ts`, `index.css`. Not changed: `export/run.ts`, `engine/retouch/*`, `import/probe.ts`, `library/analysis-pass.ts` (the performance build's files).

### Verified

Headless Chromium with software WebGPU (SwiftShader), `?gpu=readback`, on a machine shared with other test runs; each script run alone. Files: `test-srgb.jpg` (1600 × 1072) as C.JPG and D.JPG. Scratch scripts in `scratchpad/mk/`: `engine-masks.mjs`, `e2e-ui1.mjs`, `e2e-masks.mjs`, `identity.mjs`, `mem45.mjs`, `engine-perf.mjs`.

- **Engine** (`engine-masks.mjs`, the engine driven directly; **10 of 10**): a radial brightened the centre and left the corner; a linear gradient darkened its side and left the other; a brush stroke changed the photo under it and nowhere else; a luminance range changed the bright pixels and left the dark; an inverted radial did the opposite; and after drawing with masks, the same engine drew the edit without them **identically** to before (every byte).
- **The masking tool end to end** (`e2e-masks.mjs`, **45 of 45**: 44 in the full run, and the last, whose first form sampled fixed spots the Angle had moved away from, checked by centroid and run again on its own; the first run found two bugs, fixed: Shift-click didn't add a colour sample after the first, and the brush-cap message read badly; the rest of the first run's failures were the script's):
  - **Each part type**, made with its key and drawn on the photo, shown with **White on Black** (the mask itself): Shift+M and a drag, a radial (centre 255, corner 0); M, a linear gradient (255 at its start, 126 half-way, 0 beyond its end); K, a brush stroke (255 on the stroke, 0 off it), **Alt-drag erases** (its own step, "Brush 1: erase"; 0 where erased); Shift+Q and a click on the sky, a luminance range (sky 255, dark forest 0); Shift+J and a click on the forest, a colour range (forest 255, sky 0), then **Shift-click** on the sky added it (255; "Colour range 1: colour sample"). Each creation was one history step ("New mask: Radial 1").
  - **Each of the ten local sliders** at a strong value on the radial: the photo changed inside the mask (mean change 1.3 for Blacks on this bright photo to 144 for Highlights) and **not at all outside** (0.000 for every slider); each move one step named after the mask ("Radial 1: Vibrance").
  - **Combining:** a shape mask's **Part to combine** offered only Brush, Linear gradient, Radial gradient; a range mask's only Luminance range, Colour range. Subtract a linear gradient: the ellipse's left part removed (6), its right kept (221); Intersect: the left the ellipse's own feathered value (133.2 = 133.2), the right and the outside 0; Add: both (255 / 221); inverting the part under Intersect = Subtract again (12 / 221); **'** inverted the whole mask (243 / 34 / corner 255), one step "Radial 1: invert".
  - **Undo/redo:** Ctrl+Z undid that invert (the mask inverted again, the edit changed), Ctrl+Y restored the edit exactly (JSON identical).
  - **Design-around on load:** an edit stored with a luminance range intersected with a radial came back with the radial only.
  - **Geometry:** with an off-centre radial (Radial 2) and **Angle +8°**, the mask's centroid on the canvas was 0.7 px from where the drawing maps its stored centre (200.0, 151.7 vs 200.6, 152.1).
  - **Snapshots:** a snapshot of 6 masks, one deleted (5), the snapshot applied: 6 again.
  - **Copy/Paste:** Copy's Masks box unticked by default; ticked and pasted twice onto D: 12 masks (added, with new ids).
  - **Persistence:** after a reload the same six masks in the same order (catalog); **Export** (PNG, 1334 × 894) brighter at the radial's centre with the masks than without (190 vs 146), the corner the same (175 / 175); **C.xmp** held all six in `lightbench:Develop` and no `crs:` mask fields; a **virtual copy** started with the six; a **fresh library** importing C.JPG with that C.xmp read the six back and drew Radial 2 on its content with the Angle (centroid 200.0, 151.7 again).
- **By eye** (`e2e-ui1.mjs`, 3 of 3: a radial made, its Exposure acting inside only; and the screenshots `e2e-masks.mjs` saves): the red Color Overlay over the radial with its handles, the inner feather ring and the centre pin; White on Black; the Masks group with its five New buttons, the range note, the mask row with its part, Part to combine, the overlay row, Feather and the mask's settings.
- **Old edits unchanged** (`identity.mjs`, Part 17's fifteen edits on four photos — two NEFs, an ARW, the JPEG — with Remove spots, a tone curve, Detail, grading and the mixer, each server run alone): the branch against `lightbench/section-eyes` `3b787de` (what `main` `27ad60e` renders: it adds only release notes and the version) gave **90 of 90 identical** (each server run alone, no transient errors this time) for the full-size 8-bit export SHA-256, the 1024 px export, the 512 px thumbnail, the histogram statistics and the Develop canvas at fit and at 100 %.
- **GPU memory at 45 MP** (`mem45.mjs`, the engine's own allocations counted by `bench/profiler.ts`; a synthetic Bayer image at 8188 × 5458 = 44.7 MP, because SwiftShader's 8192 px texture limit refuses the benchmark's 8256 px width): the RAW stage peaked at 1947 MB; a full-size export render with no masks at 1281 MB (566 MB live before it), **with the benchmark's three masks** (a brush stroke, a radial with Feather and Refine, a luminance range) **at 1361 MB: +80 MB, of which the mask buffers 79 MB**; after `unload()` 0 MB live. The mask stage works per 1024 px tile, so its memory grows with the number of masks and the tile, not the photo (at 2 MP the same three masks took 67 MB). SwiftShader times (not a GPU's): that 45 MP render 445 s without masks, 746 s with them.
- **Fit-view drawing time** (SwiftShader, `engine-perf.mjs`, the same 2 MP photo): no masks 3878 ms, a radial 4726 ms, a luminance range 5463 ms, a radial with Feather and Refine 5869 ms. A real GPU's times are for the benchmark's new rows (below).
- **Benchmark:** two rows, **masksFit** and **masksExport**, run the three masks above at fit and at full size (`bench/run.ts`, `MASKS`); `bench/plan.test.ts` checks them. Not run on a real GPU here.
- **Unit tests** (`npx vitest run`: **1048 tests in 106 files** after merging `main` `27ad60e`, which has 991 in 101, so 57 new: the five files below, the keymap test's masking case and the benchmark plan's masks rows):
  - `engine/develop/mask-math.test.ts` (17): erf; the linear gradient (0 at Zero, 1 at Full, ½ half-way, monotone); darktable's quadratic radial falloff; the brush (solid core, linear falloff, dabs building to the density or erasing); darktable's operators (max, min, a·(1 − c)) and Subtract + Invert; the measured value (WB fix and exposure only); L\* through the trapezoid; OkLab distance with full weight within half the tolerance; local Temp/Tint keeping luminance; local Contrast about the 18.45 % fulcrum keeping ratios; the Feather/Refine plans and margins; Feather keeping the inside, the far outside and the total; Refine taking the guide's edge; **a tile computed with its margin equals the whole**; the guide on 0…1.
  - `engine/develop/masks.test.ts` (13): edits without masks untouched; a valid mask passed on with the process version's numbers; damaged parts ignored; newer kinds (an AI raster) kept, not rendered; ids and names; effect only with a slider and Amount > 0; shapes with shapes, ranges with ranges; a range with a shape refused **without patent talk**; a stored range ∩ shape dropped; the caps (64, 32, 10,000) on load and before a change; pasted masks added with new ids up to the cap; presets without brush masks.
  - `engine/develop/mask-render.test.ts` (12): a sensor point lands where each drawing shows it (as shot; cropped; straightened 7.5°; turned and flipped; Upright Vertical 40; lens-corrected, cropped and straightened); masks move with the content when the angle changes; axes turn with a quarter turn; radii and dabs sized with the drawing; Amount weighting and the overlay mark; masks without effect and from a newer LightBench left out; ranges passed as they are, Feather and Refine sized on the drawn photo.
  - `sidecar/masks-sidecar.test.ts` (8): masks round-trip through `lightbench:Develop`; damaged masks ignored, the rest of the edit kept; a **synthetic** Lightroom sidecar (ExifTool's names) in all three XMP forms; gradients, brush dabs and luminance ranges read with their sliders; a range on a gradient, AI masks, missing sliders and inactive masks left out and reported; `crs:` masks never written and kept as they were; sidecars without or with damaged masks; the `.acr` name.
  - `develop/masks-paste.test.ts` (5): Masks unticked by default and added when ticked; never in Auto Sync; the group eye a view only; a preset's Amount scaling only the masks it adds.
  - The keymap test checks every masking key in both sets and the secondary-source labels.
- **Full checks from a clean install** (`npm ci && npx tsc -b && npx oxlint && npx vitest run && npm run build`): all pass; oxlint shows only the two warnings `main` already has (`syscheck/report.tsx`, `LibraryView.tsx`).

### Limitations and unverified

- **Real GPU:** everything was measured on SwiftShader. The tiled mask stage, the brush's blended quads and the guided filter have not been timed on a real GPU; the benchmark's two new rows are there for that.
- **Lightroom masks:** read from a **synthetic** sidecar only; the meanings of `What`, `MaskBlendMode`, the Local* scales and the coordinate frame are LightCraft's observations, not Adobe's documentation (Cowork: don't hard-code the blend values as fact). A real Lightroom sidecar with masks should be tried before the reader is trusted. Masks are read only at import, not again when **Read changes** rereads a sidecar.
- **`.acr` naming:** the notice looks for `NAME.acr` beside the photo (any case); Adobe's documentation names the file type but not its naming, so this is an assumption.
- **Amount 0–200 %** is LightCraft's range; Adobe documents none.
- **Refine** (an edge-aware guided filter on luminance, applied to finished masks) is flagged for the owner's patent counsel in the design-around above, though it uses no colour distance.
- **Spatial local sliders** (Texture, Clarity, Dehaze, Sharpness, Noise, Moiré, Defringe) and darktable export of masks are not in this build (decisions 11 and 14). The Depth range mask is built in Part 24.
- **Pen pressure** was not tested end to end (no pen; headless mouse events carry no pressure); the code path stores the pressure per dab and the unit tests cover the dab maths.
- **Touch:** the 44 px targets and two-finger pan were checked by layout and code, not on a touch device.

## Part 24: AI Depth range mask and model delivery

**What it does.** A **Depth range** part for masks: it selects the photo by how near or far each part is, from a depth map that **Depth Anything V2 Small** (Apache-2.0) estimates on the user's own computer. It is LightBench's first downloaded AI model, so this build also lays the Phase 4 groundwork for every later model: a pinned manifest, the deploy fetching the files by exact URL and SHA-256 into LightBench's own site, an opt-in download that says the size, the licence and, in plain words, the training data, an on-device cache checked by SHA-256 that works offline, Settings → AI models (info and removal), and one runtime (ONNX Runtime Web) in a worker. Each photo's depth map is computed once and **stored inside its edit and its .xmp**, so another computer (or a fresh library) draws the same mask without the model. With no Depth range nothing renders differently, and old edits render byte-identically (below).

The brief is [research/MASKING.md](research/MASKING.md) (owner's decisions 2, 10 and 12 at its end) with Part 23 (the masks this builds on); model facts from [research/PHASE4_MODELS.md](research/PHASE4_MODELS.md), [research/DEPTH_OF_FIELD.md](research/DEPTH_OF_FIELD.md) and Cowork's checks ([F_MODEL_DELIVERY.md](research/cowork-2026-10-13b/F_MODEL_DELIVERY.md), [B_SKY_DEPTH.md](research/cowork-2026-10-14-batch3/B_SKY_DEPTH.md)).

### Owner's decisions and how they are built

| # | Decision | Built as |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Depth Anything V2 **Small** only | `web/models/manifest.json`: the Hugging Face `onnx-community/depth-anything-v2-small` export, its `onnx/model_fp16.onnx` (49,642,442 bytes) and `onnx/model.onnx` (99,060,839 bytes): the sizes Cowork read from the Git LFS pointers on `main` (F3). Base/Large/Giant (CC-BY-NC-4.0) are not offered. |
| 2 | Served from LightBench's own Pages site; downloaded once on explicit opt-in (size, licence, plain-words training data); cached on the device with SHA-256; offline afterwards; removable; never fetched without consent; not precached | The deploy workflow fetches each pinned file by exact URL and checks its size and SHA-256 (`web/scripts/models.mjs fetch`, a step after the build; a mismatch fails the build), into `dist/models/<id>/<revision 7>/<variant>.onnx`. **Not in git.** The app knows each file's size and hash from the manifest compiled into it (`web/models-plugin.ts`), so nothing is asked of the network before the user clicks Download. Cache Storage `ai-models` (`src/ai/store.ts`); the service worker leaves `models/` and `ort/` out of its precache. |
| 3 | onnxruntime-web pinned, WebGPU, fp16 only with `shader-f16`, WASM fallback only if fast enough, a worker, Part 14's gate and device loss | ONNX Runtime Web **1.30.0** exactly (`package.json`), its native WebGPU provider (`onnxruntime-web/webgpu`), in `src/ai/runtime.worker.ts` on **its own GPU device** (not the DevelopEngine's). fp16 file only when the adapter has `shader-f16`, else fp32 (`chooseFile`). Without WebGPU, the **WebAssembly fallback** (ONNX Runtime's CPU provider, 4 threads, the same runtime file) is used, because a model of DA-V2 Small's shape ran in about 3–5 s per photo on it (`WASM_FALLBACK`; "Timing"); the panel says it runs on the CPU. Model runs that hang are stopped after 3 minutes. |
| 4 | A range-family part (no Depth ∩ shape); a near/far picker with feather; a depth-map view | `DepthShape` (`kind: 'depth'`) is in `RANGE_KINDS`: `canAdd`, the panel's Part to combine menu and `normalizeMask` treat it as Luminance and Colour ranges are treated. Near end, Far end and Depth feather sliders (a trapezoid on 0 near … 100 far), **Pick a distance…** (a click centres the range there), **Show depth map**. |
| 5 | The depth map inside the .xmp, compressed, in the `RasterShape` slot; sensor/straightened frame documented; size measured | 8 bits in the **sensor frame**, MED prediction + raw DEFLATE + base64 (`depth-map.ts`), stored in the Depth range part (`raster`). Sizes: "Verified". |
| 6 | Old edits never change; no process version | Nothing changes for an edit without a Depth range; the mask stage's new texture and grid are bound only for one. Identity: "Verified". |
| 7 | Credit and notices; an in-app model info panel | THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md (DA-V2 Small Apache-2.0; ONNX Runtime MIT and its third-party notices); the model's licence served next to it, the runtime's licence and notices next to the runtime; **About this model** in the download dialog and in Settings → AI models. |

### Using it

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| New Depth range | The Masks group's **Depth (AI)** button (or the action "New mask: Depth range (AI)" in the command search; no default key). The first time on a device it asks to download the model (below). On a photo without a depth map it computes one ("Computing the depth map (once for this photo)…"), then makes **Depth range 1**, starting on the nearest part (0–30, fading out by 50), with the picker on. A second Depth range on the same photo reuses its map. One history step. | LightBench |
| Range | **Depth: near end** and **Depth: far end** (0 is the nearest part of this photo, 100 the farthest: relative depth, not metres), **Depth feather** (how far beyond the ends it fades), each one history step; **Pick a distance…**: a click on the photo centres the range there (±10, feather 15). Lightroom's Depth Range has a similar range control per secondary sources (**unverified**; Adobe's page was not read), and Lightroom offers it only for photos with embedded depth (search snippet, **unverified**, MASKING.md); LightBench estimates the depth instead. | MASKING.md |
| Combining | A Depth range joins range masks only: added to, subtracted from or intersected with Luminance and Colour ranges and other Depth ranges, never with a brush or gradient (decision 12; the panel's range note says so without patent talk). Separate masks still stack. | Part 23 |
| Depth-map view | **Show depth map** (with the masking tool on) draws the selected mask's depth map instead of the photo: white near, black far (the overlay's White on Black). A view only. | LightBench |
| Download dialog | "Download Depth Anything V2 Small?": what it is for, that it is downloaded **once**, from LightBench's own site, only if the user agrees, checked against its SHA-256, kept on the device (works offline), removable in Settings → AI models; the size (on this machine "26.8 MB (the model, fp32 …; the runtime it needs, ONNX Runtime Web 1.30.0: 26.8 MB)" with the stand-in; with the real files 76.4 MB with fp16 or 125.8 MB with fp32, runtime included); the training data in plain words; **About this model** (licence, credit, runtime, files and hashes); progress and Cancel. A file that doesn't match its SHA-256 or its size is not kept and nothing is installed. | Owner's decision 2 |
| Settings → AI models | Each model: Not downloaded / On this device (variant, size) / Not available in this build; Download… (the same dialog); **Remove from this device** (the runtime goes with the last model); About this model. | Owner's decision 2 |
| Paste, presets | A depth map belongs to its photo: pasted Depth ranges take the target's own map when it has one, else they wait for one (the panel says so and offers **Compute the depth map**, one history step); presets keep Depth ranges without a map. AI masks recomputing on paste (MASKING.md) is thus one click, not automatic. | MASKING.md |
| Messages | No WebGPU: the CPU fallback, with "Computing the depth map on the CPU (once for this photo; no WebGPU here, so it takes longer)…" (with the fallback switched off, `WASM_FALLBACK`, it would say "The AI Depth mask needs WebGPU …"). A run that takes over 3 minutes: stopped, "The model didn't finish within 3 minutes …". Not pinned in this build: "… isn't available in this build of LightBench: its files haven't been published with it yet." A lost GPU during a run: "… Develop is not affected; try again." fp16 giving non-finite numbers (ORT issue #26732): says so and asks for the fp32 file next time. Each with a link to Settings → AI models. | Part 14 |
| Help | Every new control has a tooltip (`help.ts` `MASK_HELP.depthNew`, `depthNear`, `depthFar`, `depthFeather`, `depthView`), the range note names depth ranges. | Owner's rule |

### Model delivery (the Phase 4 foundation)

| Piece | Detail |
|---|---|
| Manifest | `web/models/manifest.json`: per model its id, name, licence (id, URL, the README's statement, the licence file), credit, training data in plain words, source (Hugging Face repo and a 40-digit commit), input (short side 518, multiple of 14, ImageNet mean/std), and per file its variant, path, bytes and SHA-256. Checked at build time (`src/ai/manifest.ts` `parseManifest`: malformed hashes, commits, paths or duplicate variants stop the build). **Not pinned yet** (`revision` and `sha256` null): see "What the owner must supply". |
| Deploy | `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` step **Fetch AI models**, after the build: `node scripts/models.mjs fetch --out dist` downloads `https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/depth-anything-v2-small/resolve/<commit>/onnx/<file>`, checks bytes and SHA-256, writes the files and the model's `LICENSE.txt`, and checks the site stays under Pages' 1 GB. A mismatch fails the build. An unpinned model is skipped with a `::warning::` (the deploy goes on; the app then says the model isn't available). `verify --dir` checks files laid out as served (no network); `pin` (the owner) reads the commit and each file's Git LFS pointer, downloads, checks the download against the pointer and writes the manifest. |
| Sizes and limits | DA-V2 Small: fp16 49.6 MB, fp32 99.1 MB (each under GitHub's 100 MiB file limit, though they aren't in git); runtime 26.8 MB; the site grows by about 176 MB of the 1 GB limit. Bandwidth: Pages' 100 GB/month soft limit is about 1,300 first installs with fp16 or 800 with fp32 (arithmetic). |
| On the device | `src/ai/store.ts`: `installed` and `installSize` read the cache only; `install` (after the user's click) streams each file with progress, checks its size and SHA-256 before keeping it, records what is installed last (so an interrupted install leaves nothing recorded), removes another variant's file and asks `navigator.storage.persist()`; `load` checks the SHA-256 again before handing the bytes to the runtime (a damaged cache entry is reported, never run); `remove` deletes the model, and the runtime with the last model. Cache `ai-models`: not a `lightbench-…` cache (the service worker deletes those on updates) and never served by the service worker. |
| Runtime | `src/ai/runtime.worker.ts` + `runtime.ts`: ONNX Runtime Web 1.30.0's `onnxruntime-web/webgpu` bundle (its JavaScript is in the worker's chunk, 118 kB); its WebAssembly (`ort-wasm-simd-threaded.asyncify.wasm`, 26.8 MB, also runs the CPU provider) is copied by the build to `ort/1.30.0/` with its SHA-256 worked out at build time, downloaded with the first model and passed to ONNX Runtime as bytes (`env.wasm.wasmBinary`), so the worker fetches nothing. The bundle's own `new URL(…wasm)` is replaced at build time (`ortWasmUnbundled`) so Vite doesn't copy the 27 MB file into `assets/` (it would have been precached). The worker is started on the first run and ended after 2 minutes unused, or after any failure (the next run starts a fresh worker and GPU device). One runtime for every model: later models only add manifest entries. |
| fp16 / fp32 | The main thread asks the default adapter for `shader-f16` (the worker's ONNX Runtime uses the same default adapter) and the dialog offers the fp16 file only then; elsewhere fp32, because ONNX Runtime's WebGPU provider **fails** on fp16 graphs without `shader-f16` ("requires f16 but the device does not support it", F9). Non-finite output from fp16 (ORT issue #26732) is caught (`quantizeDisparity`) and the user is told to use fp32. |
| GPU robustness (Part 14) | The model runs on the runtime worker's own device, so a lost device or an out-of-memory there can't touch Develop's engine; the worker reports a loss (`device.lost`) and is replaced. Rendering the photo as shot and the model run are held as foreground work (`gpuGate.foreground`), so Export and the background developer wait for them (up to the gate's limit) as they wait for Develop's drawings. |

### The depth map

| Step | Detail |
|---|---|
| Input | The photo **as shot**: the edit's tones and colours, without crop, angle, perspective, flips, lens correction, masks or Detail, in the camera's orientation (upright, as the model expects), rendered by Develop's engine at 1.5 × the model's long side, then area-resized to the model's input: DA-V2's own preprocessing (`depth_anything_v2/dpt.py` `image2tensor` at `a561b84`: "lower_bound" resize so the short side is 518, sides multiples of 14 — 784 × 518 for a 6000 × 4000 photo, 770 × 518 for the 1600 × 1072 test photo — RGB 0…1, ImageNet mean and std). A file with fixed input sizes is fed those. |
| Output | Relative inverse depth (larger = nearer) at the input size, scaled per photo to 0…255 between its 0.1st and 99.9th percentiles (255 nearest). |
| Frame | **Sensor frame**, like every mask point (Part 23, decision 1): the map is resampled from the as-shot rendering into the sensor's orientation through the rendering's own uv → sensor map (quarter turns and flips only; no lens correction in it, so no warp is involved), keeping its texel count (the test photo: 772 × 517). Each drawing then maps its pixels to sensor uv through **its** geometry — crop, angle, perspective, flips and lens correction — so the mask stays on its content when those change later: `mask-render.ts` `sensorGrid` evaluates `displayToSensorAt` at 65 × 65 points over the drawn image, and `mask.wgsl` (case 6) interpolates it bilinearly and samples the map (an r8unorm texture, bilinear). The depth was estimated on the uncorrected photo; lens correction then moves it with the pixels, as it moves everything. |
| Encoding | `u8-med-deflate-b64`: each byte's difference from JPEG-LS's median-edge predictor (left, up, upper-left), raw DEFLATE (the browser's `CompressionStream`), base64. Decoded synchronously while a drawing is prepared (`lib/inflate.ts`, written after zlib's public-domain "puff"), cached by its data. Caps: sides ≤ 2048, ≤ 3.1 M texels, ≤ 8 MB of text; a damaged map is dropped on load (the part stays, waiting for a new map) or, if it passes the checks but doesn't decode, the mask is left out of drawings. |
| Stored in | The part's `raster` with `model`, `modelVersion` (`<commit 7>/<variant>`) and `runtime` ("onnxruntime-web 1.30.0 webgpu"): so the catalog's edit, the `lightbench:Develop` sidecar field, snapshots and virtual copies carry it. The **saved history** keeps each map once (`history.ts` `savedForm`: "@r<n>" references), not once per step. Several Depth ranges on one photo each carry a copy in the edit and the sidecar (not de-duplicated there). |
| Rendering | With the masks of Part 23: per 1024 px tile, the Depth range evaluated per target pixel (trapezoid on 100 × (1 − depth)), combined, inverted, feathered and refined as any part; in Develop, the histogram, thumbnails, Auto and Export. Nothing is computed when drawing: the model is needed only to make a map. |

### Files

New: `web/models/manifest.json`, `web/models/notices/` (DA-V2's LICENSE at `a561b84`; ONNX Runtime's LICENSE and ThirdPartyNotices.txt at `v1.30.0`), `web/models-plugin.ts`, `web/scripts/models.mjs` (+ `models.test.mjs`), `web/scripts/models-check.mjs` (+ `models-check.test.mjs`), `.github/workflows/ai-models.yml` (also on `main` through `lightbench/ai-models-workflow`), `src/ai/manifest.ts`, `store.ts`, `runtime.ts`, `runtime.worker.ts`, `models.ts`, `depth.ts`, `ModelDialog.tsx`, `AiModelsSettings.tsx`, `src/develop/useDepth.ts`, `src/engine/develop/depth-map.ts`, `src/lib/inflate.ts`; tests `src/ai/store.test.ts`, `src/engine/develop/depth-map.test.ts`, `depth-mask.test.ts`. Changed: `masks.ts` (`DepthShape`, range family, paste/preset helpers), `mask-render.ts` (`sensorGrid`, depth parts, the depth-map view), `mask-math.ts` (`depthAlpha`), `engine/gpu/mask-stage.ts` and `mask.wgsl` (bindings 10–11, case 6), `develop-engine.ts` (passes the view flag), `indicators.ts`, `MasksPanel.tsx`, `useMasking.ts`, `MaskOverlay.tsx`, `DevelopView.tsx`, `help.ts`, `history.ts`, `actions.ts`, `settings/SettingsDialog.tsx`, `App.tsx`, `bench/run.ts`, `vite.config.ts`, `sw-plugin.ts`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, `package.json` (onnxruntime-web 1.30.0), THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md. Not changed: the Crop section and Develop's placeholder thumbnail handling (another build's), release notes and the version.

### The model files: where they come from, and what the owner must supply

huggingface.co (and its mirrors, jsDelivr and unpkg) answered **403** to this build machine's proxy, so **neither the real ONNX files nor their hashes could be read here**. Nothing in the repository invents them:

| File | Source URL (pinned when the commit is known) | Bytes | SHA-256 |
|---|---|---|---|
| fp16 | `https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/depth-anything-v2-small/resolve/<commit>/onnx/model_fp16.onnx` | 49,642,442 (Cowork's LFS-pointer reading of `main`, 2026-10-13; **to confirm**) | **unknown** |
| fp32 | `https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/depth-anything-v2-small/resolve/<commit>/onnx/model.onnx` | 99,060,839 (same; **to confirm**) | **unknown** |
| runtime | `ort/1.30.0/ort-wasm-simd-threaded.asyncify.wasm` (from npm `onnxruntime-web@1.30.0`, copied by the build) | 26,781,914 | worked out by the build from the npm file (npm's lockfile integrity checks the package) |

**The owner's step** (owner's choice, 2026-10-19: done by the **"AI models" GitHub Action** on GitHub's servers, which reach huggingface.co; see "Pinning and checking the models" below). By hand it is, once, on a machine that reaches huggingface.co: `cd web && node scripts/models.mjs pin` (or `pin --revision <40-digit commit>` to pin a commit read elsewhere; `--save models/local` also keeps the files for the dev server). It reads the commit (the Hub API's `sha`, not checked from here), each file's Git LFS pointer at that commit (`/raw/<commit>/<path>`: `oid sha256:` and `size`), downloads each file, checks it against its pointer, and rewrites `web/models/manifest.json`; commit that file. From the next deploy on, the workflow fetches and checks the files and the site offers the model. Until then the deploy warns ("not pinned … not deployed") and the app says "isn't available in this build". If the owner prefers to commit the files instead (each is under 100 MiB), they go in the same layout and the workflow step can use `--from`.

Two things to confirm when pinning, not checked here (the workflow's check now reports both): the export's input and output (expected `pixel_values` [1, 3, H, W] with free H and W, and `predicted_depth` [1, H, W], as Hugging Face's DPT exports name them; the code takes the session's first input and output whatever their names, and uses fixed sizes if the file has them), and that the fp16 file keeps float32 inputs and outputs (the runtime feeds float32).

A third-party ONNX export of the same weights exists as a GitHub release asset (`fabio-sim/Depth-Anything-ONNX` v2.0.0, `depth_anything_v2_vits.onnx`, 99,373,606 bytes, SHA-256 `d2b11a11c1d4a12b47608fa65a17ee9a4c605b55ee1730c8e3b526304f2562be`, fixed 518 × 518 input), which this machine could download; it was **not run** here (the session's safety policy refused running a downloaded model) and is **not** proposed as the source: it is not one of the official exports named by the owner.

### Pinning and checking the models

Owner's choice (2026-10-19): the model is pinned and tested by a GitHub Action that runs on GitHub's servers (they reach huggingface.co; this build machine doesn't), started by hand from the Actions tab. The coordinating session reads its job log.

**Owner's steps:**

1. Once: merge `lightbench/ai-models-workflow` into `main`. It adds only `.github/workflows/ai-models.yml`. GitHub lists a manually run (`workflow_dispatch`) workflow only when the file is on the default branch. On `main`, which has no AI models code yet, the workflow prints "run this workflow on a branch that contains the AI models code" and ends successfully without doing anything.
2. **Actions** tab → **AI models** (left column) → **Run workflow** → **Use workflow from: `lightbench/depth-mask`** → task **`pin-and-check`** (leave *revision* empty to pin the model repository's current `main`, or give a 40-digit Hugging Face commit) → **Run workflow**. The branch's own copy of the workflow runs.
3. When it finishes (a few minutes), the run's page shows a summary: the pinned commit, each file's size and SHA-256, and the check's table. If everything passed, the workflow has committed `web/models/manifest.json` (and nothing else) to `lightbench/depth-mask` as `github-actions[bot]`. Pull it before working on the branch again. GitHub doesn't start other workflows for a commit pushed with the workflow's own token, so the pull request's checks for that commit don't run by themselves: re-run them from the pull request, or push any later commit.
4. Later, task **`check`** checks the files the branch's manifest pins again, downloaded from their pinned Hugging Face URLs, without committing anything. Use it, for example, before merging or after an ONNX Runtime upgrade.

**The workflow** (`.github/workflows/ai-models.yml`; inputs `task` = `pin-and-check` (default) or `check`, and an optional `revision`; `permissions: contents: write`; one run per branch at a time):

| Step | What it does |
|---|---|
| Look for the AI models code | If `web/scripts/models.mjs`, `models-check.mjs` or `web/models/manifest.json` is missing, it prints "run this workflow on a branch that contains the AI models code" and the rest is skipped (the run succeeds). |
| Check the inputs | Requires a branch (not a tag) and a known task. `revision` must be 40 lower-case hex digits; with `check` it is ignored, with a warning. |
| Node 22 and `npm ci` in `web/` | The same setup as the deploy workflow. |
| Pin (`pin-and-check`) | Runs `node scripts/models.mjs pin [--revision …] --save $RUNNER_TEMP/models`. It reads the commit, each file's Git LFS pointer at that commit, downloads each file, checks it against the pointer and rewrites the manifest. The manifest's layout is kept, so only the revision and the files' sizes and hashes change. The step prints the manifest before and after (`models.mjs show`). |
| Check the real model | Runs `node scripts/models-check.mjs`: on the files `pin` saved (`pin-and-check`), or on the files downloaded from the pinned URLs (`check`). See below. |
| Deploy step and tests (`pin-and-check`) | Runs the deploy workflow's `models.mjs fetch` on the saved files (`--from`, no second download), then `npx tsc -b` and `npx vitest run src/ai scripts`, the tests that read the manifest. `store.test.ts` accepts a manifest pinned all or nothing. |
| Commit (`pin-and-check`) | Commits only `web/models/manifest.json`, and only if it changed and everything above passed. The author is `github-actions[bot]`, with the session trailer. It pushes to the same branch; if the branch moved while the run was going, it rebases onto it first. |

**`web/scripts/models-check.mjs`** (Node, no browser, no new dependency). It uses the pinned `onnxruntime-web` 1.30.0's own Node build (its WebAssembly CPU backend, up to 4 threads) and the **app's own code**: `src/ai/depth.ts` (`modelSize`, `toTensor`, `toSensorFrame`) and `src/engine/develop/depth-map.ts` (`quantizeDisparity`, `encodeDepth`, `decodeDepth`). Vite bundles these for Node in an SSR build without the app's config, into `node_modules/.cache/models-check/`. `depth.ts`'s import of the browser runtime (a worker) is replaced by a stub that throws if called. The test photo is synthetic and deterministic (the repository has no fixture photo): 1176 × 784, the size at which Develop renders a 6000 × 4000 photo for the model. It has a sky, a far building, a near ball and a receding ground. For each file of the manifest, fp16 then fp32, it prints plain lines:

- `ok size … bytes, sha256 …`, or a mismatch;
- each input and output: name, type and shape (expected `pixel_values: float32 [1, 3, "height", "width"]` and `predicted_depth: float32 [1, "height", "width"]`). Other names or fixed sizes are noted, because the app takes the first input and output and uses fixed sizes. For fp16, it says whether the inputs and outputs are float32, as the app needs;
- the model's input size (784 × 518 with free sizes), the session creation time and two run times (the first includes warm-up), and the output's type and shape;
- the output's min, max and mean, and whether all values are finite;
- the depth map in the sensor frame, whether it decodes back to itself, and its mean over the sky, building, ball and bottom ground. A depth model should give sky < building < ball and bottom; this is reported, not checked;
- the size of the depth map as stored in the edit and the .xmp, in base64 characters: full, at half size, at 6 bits, and at half size and 6 bits (the options left to the owner under "Sidecar size");
- for the two files together, the mean and largest difference between the fp16 and fp32 maps, in 8-bit levels;
- `RESULT: all checks passed`, or the list of problems.

It also appends a Markdown table to the run's summary. It exits 1 on any of these: a size or SHA-256 mismatch; an unpinned or missing file; a file that doesn't load or run; no output, or an output that isn't one map; inputs or outputs that aren't float32 [1, 3, H, W] → [1, H, W]; a map that doesn't decode to itself; non-finite fp32 output. Non-finite fp16 output is a warning only, because the app then tells the user to use fp32. Locally: `node scripts/models-check.mjs [--manifest FILE] [--files DIR] [--variant fp16|fp32] [--threads N] [--summary FILE]`. `--files` takes the layout under `dist/models/`, which is also what `pin --save` writes. Without `--files`, it downloads the files.

**What the workflow can't show:** the WebGPU provider (no GPU on the runner; the check uses ONNX Runtime's CPU kernels, the same version, not the app's WebGPU bundle), speed on a GPU, and the map of a real photo, including its real sidecar size (the synthetic photo is simpler). Those still need the app on a real GPU: the benchmark row and the dialog. The headless-browser e2e of Part 24's "Verified" section isn't in the workflow. Its scripts are outside the repository, and SwiftShader's WebGPU never finished a model of this size.

### Test stand-ins (never shipped)

Built and tested with **locally generated ONNX stand-ins** of the same signature (`scratchpad/dm/make_standin.py`; Python `onnx` 1.23): `standin-fp32.onnx` (1,054 bytes: "depth" = 3 × row/height + 0.3 × blurred luminance + 1, so the bottom of a photo is "near"), `standin-fp16.onnx` (the same in float16 inside) and `costtwin-fp32.onnx` (87 MB: **random weights** in the shape of DA-V2 Small — ViT-S/14, 12 blocks of width 384 with 6 heads and MLP 1536, then a convolutional head to the input size; 21.9 M parameters — for timing only). They are served by the dev server through a test-only manifest (`LB_MODELS_MANIFEST`, `LB_MODELS_DIR`, with an obviously fake commit `feedface…`), never committed or deployed.

### Verified

Headless Chromium with software WebGPU (SwiftShader, which has **no `shader-f16`**: the fp32 path), `?gpu=readback`, on a machine shared with other sessions' test runs (each of these scripts run alone); the TEST stand-in model above. Photos: `test-srgb.jpg` (1600 × 1072) as C.JPG and D.JPG. Scripts in `scratchpad/dm/`: `e2e-depth.mjs` (parts main, fresh, nogpu, integrity, unavailable), `e2e-prod.mjs`, `identity.mjs`, `timing.mjs`, `dryrun.sh`, `make_standin.py`, `mk_test_manifest.py`.

- **End to end, dev server** (`e2e-depth.mjs`, **31 of 31**):
  - **No fetch before consent** (main, 6 checks): opening Develop, Settings → AI models ("Not downloaded", the info panel with the licence, SA-1B and Virtual KITTI 2 named, ONNX Runtime Web 1.30.0) and the download dialog itself make **no request** under `models/` or `ort/`; the dialog says the size (26.8 MB here: the runtime plus the 1 kB stand-in), "once", "LightBench's own site", "remove it any time", the training data in plain words; it offers **fp32** (the adapter has no `shader-f16`); **Not now** leaves no request, no mask and no history step.
  - **Download and integrity** (main, 3; integrity, 2): Download fetched exactly the runtime and the fp32 file, from the site's own origin, and kept them (and the record) in `ai-models`; one history step "New mask: Depth range 1". With the server's copy altered by one byte, the download was refused ("doesn't match its SHA-256 … it was not kept"), no model file and no record were kept (only the runtime, which had matched); a good copy then installed without downloading the runtime again. (The integrity part's first run timed out waiting for the second install, cause not found; run again with diagnostics it passed.)
  - **The mask** (main, 9): the edit holds the map (772 × 517, the sensor's shape), `feedfac/fp32`, "onnxruntime-web 1.30.0 webgpu"; a new range is 0–30 (fading by 50) with the picker on. White on Black: the near part (bottom of the stand-in's map) 255, the far part 0. **Show depth map**: 42 at the top < 119 in the middle < 219 at the bottom, no history step. Exposure +1.5: the near part 70.4 → 127.3, **the far part not changed at all** (largest change 0). **Near/far** moved to 70–100 with Depth feather 5 (range [65, 70, 100, 100]): the far part now changes (up to 57) and **the near part is exactly as without the mask** (0); each slider one history step ("Depth range 1: depth range"). **Pick a distance**: a click at mid-height gave [26, 41, 61, 76].
  - **Persistence** (main 1, fresh 2): C.xmp's `lightbench:Develop` holds the same map as the catalog; a **fresh library** importing C.JPG with that C.xmp got the Depth range and its map back and drew it (top 237, middle 194, bottom 73 in White on Black for [26, 41, 61, 76]) with **no request, no computation and no runtime worker**.
  - **Offline reuse** (main, 2): a new session (the page loaded again) with every `models/` and `ort/` request blocked made a Depth range on D from the device's copy (no request, no dialog); a second Depth range on D reused D's map (no new computation).
  - **Removal** (main, 2): Remove emptied `ai-models` and the panel said "Not downloaded"; the photos' Depth ranges still drew (their maps are in the edits).
  - **No WebGPU, and not available** (nogpu 2, unavailable 2): with the AI probe's adapter request answered with null, the same dialog first (fp32, nothing fetched), then the **CPU fallback** made the Depth range (runtime "onnxruntime-web 1.30.0 wasm") and the panel said "on the CPU …"; with the real, unpinned manifest, "… isn't available in this build of LightBench …", no dialog, no request, and Settings → AI models says "Not available in this build" with Download disabled.
- **Production build with the deploy step** (`dryrun.sh` and a `vite build` with the test manifest): `models.mjs fetch --from` wrote `models/depth-anything-v2-small/feedfac/fp16.onnx`, `fp32.onnx` and `LICENSE.txt` and reported the site size (34.8 MB); a file swapped for another failed it ("1054 bytes, expected 1095 … 1 model file(s) did not match the manifest", exit 1); `verify` passed on the result; the real (unpinned) manifest with `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true` printed two `::warning::` lines and exited 0. The build copies `ort/1.30.0/ort-wasm-simd-threaded.asyncify.wasm` with its `LICENSE.txt` and `ThirdPartyNotices.txt`, and **nothing under `models/` or `ort/` is in the service worker's precache** (32 files; the 27 MB WebAssembly is not copied into `assets/`: the first build did copy it there, which `ortWasmUnbundled` fixed).
- **End to end, production build** (`e2e-prod.mjs` against `vite preview` of that build, its service worker in control, **13 of 13**): the page cross-origin isolated and controlled by the service worker; its precache (32 files) holds nothing under `models/` or `ort/`; nothing fetched before consent; a Depth range made on WebGPU (the runtime and the fp32 file fetched from the site) and with the **CPU fallback** (the panel said "on the CPU"); White on Black right in both; and **offline for real** (network off, the page reloaded from the service worker) a Depth range on D from the device's copy with no request. The first CPU run **hung**: ONNX Runtime's WebAssembly threads are workers started from the same chunk, and the runtime worker's message handler replaced theirs; fixed (the handler isn't installed in `em-pthread` workers), and the 3-minute watchdog stopped the hung run with its message, as designed.
- **Old edits unchanged** (`identity.mjs`, copied from Part 17's and extended with two edits holding Part 23's masks: 17 edits on four photos — two NEFs, an ARW, the JPEG — with crop/angle, Remove spots, a tone curve, Detail, the mixer, grading at defaults and masks): this branch against `main` `dec3037` (0.6.5), each server run alone, **102 of 102 identical** — full-size 8-bit export SHA-256, 1024 px export, 512 px thumbnail, histogram statistics, Develop canvas at fit and at 100 %. (Three of the branch's cases failed the first time with "A requested file … could not be found" — the photo files in the page's storage, while the machine's disk was full — and were run again: identical.)
- **Timing** (SwiftShader and this shared machine's CPU: relative only; not a GPU's):
  - The stand-in model (trivial maths; the time is SwiftShader's overhead for a 770 × 518 tensor): model run 3.7–5.3 s on the dev server, 2.4–3.8 s in the production build; the whole depth map (rendering as shot, resizing, the run, the sensor-frame move and encoding) 3.8–8.2 s, and 14 s the very first time (session creation).
  - The **cost twin** (random weights in DA-V2 Small's shape, 784 × 518, `timing.mjs`): **WebAssembly, 4 threads: 4.6 s (first run, with warm-up) and 3.1 s**. WebGPU on SwiftShader: 65–79 s at 392 × 266 and 18–28 s at 196 × 140; at 784 × 518 it **never finished** (SwiftShader's GPU process went idle after about 20 minutes and ONNX Runtime waited forever), which is why model runs now have a 3-minute watchdog. On a real GPU these numbers mean nothing; the benchmark row is for that.
  - The **CPU fallback** is therefore on: about 3–5 s per photo on 4 threads for a model of this shape (the real model's DPT head is heavier than the twin's: expect more, unmeasured).
  - **Benchmark:** a new row **"AI depth: DA-V2 Small inference"** (`bench/run.ts` `aiDepth`, on the sRGB image in Quick and Full): the wall time of one depth map as Develop computes it (rendering as shot, resizing, one model run on the runtime's own device, encoding), the model run's own time in its note; never downloads ("skipped" until the model is on the device). `bench/plan.test.ts` checks it.
- **Unit tests** (`npx vitest run`: **1123 tests in 113 files**; `main` `dec3037` has 1090 in 108, so 33 new):
  - `src/ai/store.test.ts` (10): the shipped manifest is well formed, names the licence and the training sets (SA-1B, Virtual KITTI 2, "non-commercial or research-only"), has the Cowork sizes and **no revision or hashes** (so nothing is offered or deployed), each file under 100 MiB; malformed manifests refused; **fp16 only with `shader-f16`**, fp32 otherwise, a forced variant only if the GPU can run it; served path and pinned source URL; the download plan (runtime only once); **no network request before opt-in** (`installed`, `installSize`, `usage` only read); download, check, keep and **load offline** (fetch failing) with the hashes checked again; a file not matching its SHA-256 or size refused and nothing recorded, then a good copy fetching only the model; a second variant replacing the first and reusing the runtime; a damaged cache entry reported; **removal** (the runtime with the last model); an unpinned model never fetched.
  - `src/engine/develop/depth-map.test.ts` (7): the inflater against the platform's DEFLATE (stored, fixed and dynamic blocks, a hand-made stored block), damaged data and the output cap; MED prediction round trip; **encode/decode round trip** and a model-sized smooth map under 20,000 characters; damaged maps decode to null and bad fields are refused; bilinear sampling and the near → far scale; the percentile scaling and non-finite values refused (ORT #26732).
  - `src/engine/develop/depth-mask.test.ts` (9): **the range-family rule** (joins Luminance, Colour and Depth ranges, never a brush or gradient, either way round, no patent talk); a stored Depth ∩ shape loses the other family on load; **the sidecar round trip** (`lightbench:Develop` → identical settings and map, only the map's size added); **corrupt input** (bad base64, sizes, encoding, oversized data, a bad range; data that passes the checks but doesn't decode leaves the mask out of drawings); a drawing gets the map, the range and the grid, and the depth-map view; the grid through a quarter turn; paste (the target's own map, else waiting), presets (ranges without maps); the history saving each map once and a missing reference leaving the part waiting.
  - `src/ai/depth.test.ts` (3): DA-V2's "lower_bound" input sizes (770 × 518 for 1600 × 1072, 784 × 518 for 6000 × 4000, fixed sizes when the file has them), normalisation, the box resize; the sensor-frame move (identity, and a camera quarter turn: a permutation, nothing lost).
  - `web/scripts/models.test.mjs` (5): pinned files at their exact URLs, size and hash checks, LFS pointers, a dry run writing the deployable layout, a mismatch failing, an unpinned model skipped with a warning; `show`'s lines and table; `pin` keeps the manifest's layout (the shipped file reformats byte for byte; pinned, only the revision and the two file lines change).
  - `web/scripts/models-check.test.mjs` (5): output statistics with non-finite values, the input/output report (the expected export passes; float16 I/O, a channels-last input or no output are problems; other names and fixed sizes are notes), the deterministic test photo and its regions, half size, 6 bits, map differences, the summary table.
  - `masks.test.ts` (an AI raster from a newer build now uses kind `ai`), `bench/plan.test.ts` (the new row).
- **Full checks from a clean install** (`rm -rf node_modules dist && npm ci && npx tsc -b && npx oxlint && npx vitest run && npm run build`): all pass; oxlint shows only the two warnings `main` has (`syscheck/report.tsx`, `LibraryView.tsx`). Then the workflow's new step, `node scripts/models.mjs fetch --out dist`, on the real manifest: two "not pinned" warnings, exit 0, site 34.8 MB (`dryrun.sh` above covers a pinned manifest and a mismatch).

### Limitations and unverified

- **The real model was not run anywhere here.** Everything was built and tested with stand-ins (huggingface.co was blocked; running a downloaded third-party export was refused by the session's policy). Unverified until the owner pins and runs it: the export's input/output names and dynamic sizes, its fp16 file on a GPU with `shader-f16`, its speed, its output's look, and the real **sidecar size** (below).
- **Sidecar size**: the stand-in's map (blurred luminance on a ramp: far busier than a depth map) took **79,384 characters (77.5 KiB)** in C.xmp per Depth range; the same map at half size would take 27,388, at 6 bits 45,044. A smooth synthetic depth-like map of the model's size (a ground plane, a near subject, a far sky) takes under 20,000 (unit test). A real DA-V2 map is smooth with sharp object edges, so its size should fall between; **measure it** after pinning (the e2e prints it) and, if it is too large, store at half size (the encoding allows any size; the decision is the owner's). Several Depth ranges on one photo each carry their copy in the sidecar.
- **CPU fallback**: on, from a random-weight twin's 3–5 s; the real model's CPU time is unmeasured. If it proves too slow, `WASM_FALLBACK = false` makes the panel say "needs WebGPU" instead.
- **Lightroom's Depth Range**: its controls and behaviour come from secondary sources (unverified); LightBench's range control is its own (the luminance range's trapezoid on a 0 near … 100 far scale). Lightroom reads depth only from photos that carry it (search snippet, unverified); LightBench estimates it.
- **The relative scale** is per photo (0.1st–99.9th percentile of the model's inverse depth): the same range values select different distances on different photos, so pasted ranges and presets give similar, not identical, selections.
- **Export, thumbnails and the histogram** draw Depth ranges through the same mask stage (one code path), checked in Develop and from a fresh library; not separately in Export end to end here.
- **Real GPU**: the WebGPU path ran only on SwiftShader. The benchmark's new row "AI depth: DA-V2 Small inference" is for the owner's GPU (it never downloads; it says "skipped" until the model is on the device).
- `Hub API` `sha` for `pin` and the LFS pointer path `/raw/<commit>/<path>` follow Hugging Face's documented conventions as Cowork read them (F3), not tested from here. The "AI models" workflow is the first real test of both.
- **The "AI models" workflow** was checked here with `actionlint` 1.7.7 (with shellcheck) and run locally step by step: its `run:` steps in order, under bash `-eo pipefail` with the runner's variables, against a local copy of the branch and a bare repository as `origin`. Hugging Face was answered from the stand-in files (a `fetch` preload, test only). The runs covered: `pin-and-check` (pinned, checked, deploy step and tests, committed only the manifest with a 3-line diff, pushed); the same again (manifest unchanged, nothing committed); `check` (downloaded by the pinned URLs and checked); a bad revision (refused); and the `main` stub (said so and succeeded). `models-check.mjs` on the stand-ins passed. It failed, as it should, on a wrong SHA-256, a missing file, an unpinned manifest and NaN fp32 output, and it warned without failing on NaN fp16 output. The real files and GitHub's runner itself were not run from here.

### Merged with Effects, the memory fix and Diagnostics (main 0.6.8)

- **Masks:** a drawing's prepared masks carry both Part 25's fourth plane (`spatial`) and the depth grid; the depth-map view writes the four planes' values. Glow's smaller source drawing re-prepares the masks from the drawing's inputs, so Depth ranges (with their grid) are in it too.
- **Memory:** the mask stage's depth textures (up to four kept) are freed through Part 14's retirement (`MaskStage` gets `DevelopEngine.retireTex`): one dropped for a fifth map waits for the drawings open at that moment, and `unload()` hands them over the same way. `memoryStats()` counts them with every engine texture; `maskBytes` includes them.
- **The depth model's input** is rendered without effects, as without masks and Detail (Texture, Clarity, Dehaze, vignette, grain and Glow change the photo, not its depth).
- **Diagnostics:** each AI depth run is logged (`ai-depth`; "Diagnostics" below).
- **Checks** (SwiftShader, test stand-in models): `e2e-depth.mjs` main 23 of 23, fresh 2, integrity 2, no WebGPU 2; engine checks with a synthetic depth map, 8 of 8 (a Depth range beside a mask with local Texture; Glow 80 with a Depth range carrying local Glow −100: the near rows exactly as without Glow, the far rows unchanged; seven maps; `unload` leaving no mask memory and nothing waiting); Part 25's local Texture, Clarity, Dehaze and Glow in a mask, 5 of 5; Part 23's mask parts and combine modes, 20 of 21 (the one failure is that script's expectation from before Part 24: a range mask now also offers "Depth range (AI)"). **Old edits:** Part 25's identity script, all 18 edits **identical** to the recorded main hashes (full-size and 1024 px exports, thumbnail, histogram, canvas at fit and 100 %); main 0.6.8 itself re-run on 5 of them (JPEG defaults, mixer + spots + curve + Detail, both mask edits, effects at defaults), also identical.

## Part 25: Effects

**What it does.** Six tools: **Texture**, **Clarity** and **Dehaze** in Basic → Presence (before Vibrance and Saturation, where Lightroom has them), and a new **Effects** group after Detail (jump bar "Effects") with **Post-Crop Vignetting**, **Grain** and **Glow**. Each section has its eye (Presence; Vignette, Grain, Glow), the Effects group its group eye, and every slider its help text. Masks get local **Texture**, **Clarity** and **Dehaze** (a fourth mask plane) and a local **Glow** amount. Every tool is off by default and an edit without effects is exactly one made before they existed: **old edits render byte-identically and there is no new process version**. Exports gain one option, **Dither JPEGs**, off by default.

The brief is [research/EFFECTS_DESIGN.md](research/EFFECTS_DESIGN.md) with the owner's answers (2026-10-17) and the decisions after Cowork's patent check ([research/cowork-2026-10-17-effects/EFFECTS_VERIFY.md](research/cowork-2026-10-17-effects/EFFECTS_VERIFY.md)); Lightroom's field names come from Cowork's earlier check ([research/COLOUR_MIXER_EFFECTS.md](research/COLOUR_MIXER_EFFECTS.md) Part B). Engine code is darktable's (commit `e5306a7`, GPL-3.0-or-later) ported to WGSL; Clarity's mid-tone weight is LightCraft's (MIT); both are credited in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`.

### Owner's decisions and how they are built

| # | Decision | Built as |
|---|---|---|
| E2 | Local Texture, Clarity, Dehaze in this build (a fourth mask plane) | `masks.ts` adds `texture`, `clarity`, `dehaze` and `glow` to the local sliders (−100…100); `mask-render.ts` packs them as plane 3; the mask stage writes four planes only when one of them is used (`planeStride`), so a mask with only the old sliders allocates and draws exactly as before. Local values add to the global ones per pixel (`s(x) = global + Σ mask·local`, the design's exact form). Glow: only its Amount per mask (Range, Spread, Warmth and Style stay global, as Camera Raw). |
| E3 | Lightroom fields read at import and written back under Part 7's rules | `sidecar/lightroomEffects.ts`, below. Glow read only (owner, after Cowork). |
| V1 | Highlight Priority = a scene-linear gain before the sigmoid, plus highlight protection | `vignetteScene` (WGSL) / `vignetteSceneGain` (CPU): a gain of up to ±3 EV × the vignette weight, applied before the sigmoid; Highlights fades the darkening where the scene is 0.5–3 EV above grey. |
| — | Color Priority and Paint Overlay on the display image | `vignetteDisplay`: after the tone curve, Color Priority scales the display RGB (hue kept), Paint Overlay blends towards black or white; Color Priority's highlight protection works on display lightness. |
| V2 | Export dither: C, an Export option off by default | `export/run.ts` `ditherJpeg` (default `false`) and `exportDither`; the dialog's "Dither JPEGs" box. Untouched, JPEG exports are the same bytes as before (checked below); PNG/TIFF keep their own option. |
| V3 | Vignette centre offset and shape under Advanced | Horizontal / Vertical centre (−100…100 % of half the frame) and darktable's Shape (0.1…5) in the section's Advanced. |
| G1 | Grain seed stored per edit, new for a virtual copy | `effects.grain.seed` (1…2³¹−2), set when grain first turns on (`withGrain`); `catalog.worker.ts` `createVirtualCopy` reseeds the copy's edit and history (`reseedGrain`); Paste keeps the target's seed (or makes one); a seed alone is no change for Auto Sync. The seed moves darktable's simplex lattice (`hash32`), replacing its file-name hash. |
| G2 | Roughness: weight between darktable's octaves plus a fourth fine octave | `grainAmps`: at 50 darktable's three octaves exactly; below, weight tilts to the coarse octave; above, to the fine ones while a fourth octave (1.8× the third's frequency) fades in. LightBench's design. |
| G3 | `crs:GrainFrequency` read as Roughness | As decided; unverified (darktable's importer reads it as coarseness). |
