| G4 | Monochrome by default; darktable's colour grain under Advanced | Monochrome grain on CIE L\* (D65 Lab from Rec.2020), as darktable; **Colour grain** adds darktable's per-channel noise (z offsets 4, 8, 12) to the encoded RGB. |
| D1 | Dehaze: darktable's port with the binding constraints | Below. |
| D2 | Before Exposure | Right after the white-balance fix, before Exposure, in the drawing's scene-linear Rec.2020. |
| D3 | Default distance 0.2, under Advanced | Basic → Advanced → "haze removal" Distance (0…1, 0.2). |
| D4 | Negative Dehaze allowed | −100…100; negative is darktable's negative strength (adds haze). |
| D5 | Statistics within the crop | The ambient light A0 and the maximum depth come from a reference render of the crop (≤ 1024 px long side), as darktable measures what its pipe sees; re-measured when the crop changes. |
| D6 | Full-resolution exact guided filter | The guided filter runs at each drawing's own resolution (fit, 100 %, export at full size) with darktable's window sizes scaled to it (`dehazeWindows`: w₁ = 2 + ⌈4·s⌉, w₂ = 3 + ⌈6·s⌉); no subsampled "fast" filter. |
| T1–T3 | darktable's contrast & texture (EIGF), at darktable's position, rebuilt per redraw; Clarity → coarse + medium, Texture → fine | `tcPlan`, `extractDetails`, `eigfBlend` (CPU) and `tc*` passes (GPU): darktable's cascaded `fast_eigf_surface_blur` pyramid of log luminance, its band weights from the full-size detail levels, the result applied as an exposure gain 2^Σ after Exposure and the tone equalizer (and local contrast, Saturation, Vibrance). Clarity's gain multiplies the coarse and medium bands, Texture's the fine band. darktable's three Advanced settings (Base detail level, Halo control, Noise bias) are under Basic → Advanced → "contrast & texture". |
| T4 | LightCraft's mid-tone weight for Clarity | `midtoneWeight`: 0.35 + 0.65·exp(−(log₂(Y/0.18)/3.2)²) on the base layer, multiplying Clarity's bands only. |
| T5 | Radius within the crop | Radii are fractions of the crop's long side at full size; Texture's and Clarity's help say the effect re-measures when the crop changes. |
| T6 | Placement | Presence: Texture, Clarity, Dehaze, Vibrance, Saturation; Effects group: Vignette, Grain, Glow. |
| — | No UI that shows or edits individual frequency bands (US 8,666,194) | Only the two global sliders and darktable's three Advanced settings; nothing displays a band. |
| — | Glow: LightBench's scene-linear glow; ranges LightBench's own | Below; the help says the ranges are LightBench's. |

### Dehaze's binding constraints and how they are enforced

The owner's decisions after Cowork's patent check (2026-10-17; facts from EFFECTS_DESIGN.md, not legal advice). Each is a property of the code, stated in the comments of `engine/develop/effects.ts` (the "Dehaze" section), `engine/gpu/effects.wgsl` and `develop-display.wgsl`:

- **Strength and distance are always the user's values.** `dehazePixel` and the shader read the strength only from the Dehaze slider (and the mask's local Dehaze) and the distance only from Advanced; `dehazeWindows` and `ambientLight` return window sizes, A0 and the maximum depth, never a strength. Nothing scales the strength by anything measured. (darktable's **maximum depth**, used for the floor t_min = exp(−distance × depth_max), is measured from the photo as in darktable; it limits how far the removal reaches, it doesn't set the strength. One change from darktable, found by the engine test: darktable takes −1.125·ln(the 95 % dark-channel level), which is ≤ 0 once 5 % of the photo is brighter than 1 in every channel, and then removes nothing ("could not calculate ambient light due to image content"). That happened for the test JPEG (LightBench's inverse tone mapping puts a JPEG's white far above 1) and for a RAW with a bright sky. LightBench measures the level against max(1, the ambient light's smallest channel), which is never below it; while that channel is ≤ 1 it is darktable's depth exactly.)
- **darktable's standard colour guided filter with a constant eps** (`EffectsParams.dehazeEps` = 0.025, as `hazeremoval.c`): the `dhGf*`/`dhCoef*` passes compute the per-window colour covariance and its 3×3 inverse with that constant; there is no weighted guided filter and no variance-based per-pixel weight. The transmission is 1 − s·min_c(I_c/A0_c), the **RGB minimum**, never a luminance channel.
- **No output-side clean-up:** the output is darktable's closed form (I − A0)/max(t, t_min) + A0 per pixel, and nothing after it looks at the output's edges, gradients or an artefact layer.
- **No trained model, no multi-frame airlight:** A0 is darktable's per-photo estimate (the brightest of the darkest-channel pixels), from one photo.

### Using it

- **Basic → Presence:** Texture, Clarity and Dehaze (−100…100), each one undoable step named after it. Advanced has "haze removal" Distance and "contrast & texture" Base detail level, Halo control and Noise bias (darktable's names and defaults).
- **Effects → Post-Crop Vignetting:** Style (Highlight Priority, Color Priority, Paint Overlay), Amount, Midpoint, Roundness, Feather, Highlights (Highlight and Color Priority with a darkening vignette); Advanced: Horizontal and Vertical centre, Shape. "Post-crop": it follows the crop.
- **Effects → Grain:** Amount, Size, Roughness; Advanced: Colour grain, Mid-tones bias (darktable's).
- **Effects → Glow:** Style (Bloom, Diffusion, Halation), Glow (the amount), Range, Spread, Warmth.
- **Masks:** the local list gains Texture, Clarity and Dehaze (in Presence) and Glow (its own "Effects" heading).
- **Copy/Paste and Sync:** Texture, Clarity, Dehaze (Presence) and Vignette, Grain, Glow (an "Effects" group), ticked by default; each copies its own part with its Advanced settings.
- **Export:** "Dither JPEGs" (off).

### Where each runs (the display shader's order)

white-balance fix → **Dehaze** → Exposure → tone equalizer (+ local) → local contrast → Saturation/Vibrance → **Texture/Clarity gain** → Colour Mixer → Colour Grading → **Glow** → **Vignette (Highlight Priority)** → sigmoid → Tone Curve → **Vignette (Color Priority, Paint Overlay)** → **Grain** → output.

With no effects every branch takes the old path: the shader tests the effects' flags (`FX.flags.x`) and, when they are 0, runs exactly the old instructions (`developBeforeCurve`, `developRec2020`), so old edits and edits with every effect at 0 render byte-identically.

### Engine (`engine/gpu/effects.wgsl`, `effects-display.wgsl`, `effects-stage.ts`; `develop-engine.ts`)

- **Per-pixel effects** (Vignette, Grain, the Dehaze and Texture/Clarity recovery, Glow's composite) are in the display shader; their settings in one 256-byte uniform (`effectsUniform`, binding 17).
- **Neighbourhood effects run per 1024 px tile**, like the mixer and the masks, with a margin: before a tile is drawn, Dehaze renders its input (mode 8: the white-balanced scene) over the tile plus 2w₁ + 2w₂ px and runs the box min/max, box max/min and guided-filter passes in 512 px chunks, storing the two filtered maps M₊, M₋ (positive and negative strength) for the tile; Texture/Clarity renders its luminance (mode 9) over the tile plus darktable's blur margins and runs the EIGF pyramid, storing the three band planes and the base. Both are rebuilt per redraw (T2) and bounded by the tile, not the photo.
- **Dehaze's A0 and depth** come from one ≤ 1024 px render of the crop read back to the CPU (`ambientLight`, darktable's estimator), cached per photo, size, geometry and white balance, so dragging the slider costs a redraw only.
- **Glow** renders its source (the scene before the sigmoid, mode 7) once per drawing at half the drawing's size or less, at most 1024 px long, with its own masks, mixer and effect passes at that scale, thresholds it, blurs it at five scales (0.25–4 % of the long side, as a pyramid), composites them and samples the result per pixel. Bloom adds it; Diffusion swaps a share of the pixel for it (energy kept); Halation adds the glow's brightness, tinted yellow to red, only where the pixel itself isn't glowing (a halo around bright edges). Warmth tints Bloom and Diffusion cool to warm, and Halation yellow to red. All before the sigmoid, in scene light.
- **Masks' fourth plane:** `MaskTile` gained a stride (3 or 4); `mask.wgsl` accumulates plane 3 when a mask uses a local effect; with Texture/Clarity on, the mask planes cover the T/C region so local gains apply to the bands. A mask's local Glow scales Glow's amount where it is.
- **Benchmark:** ten new rows, `vignetteFit/Export`, `grainFit/Export`, `tcFit/Export`, `dehazeFit/Export`, `glowFit/Export` (`bench/run.ts`, `FX_ROWS`), each effect at a strong value at fit and at full size; `bench/plan.test.ts` checks them. Not run on a real GPU here.

### Lightroom (`sidecar/lightroomEffects.ts`, `xmp.ts`, `sync.ts`, `import/run.ts`, `develop/batch.ts`, `changes.ts`)

- **Read at import and written back** (E3 = A, Part 7's rules: only into a sidecar that already has Camera Raw settings and isn't marked as already applied, only the groups LightBench changed, in place, and a `lightbench:LightroomEffects` marker so Lightroom's own later changes are offered by **Read changes**): `crs:Texture`, `crs:Clarity2012`, `crs:Dehaze`; `crs:PostCropVignetteAmount`, `…Midpoint`, `…Roundness`, `…Feather`, `…HighlightContrast` (Highlights), `…Style` (1 Highlight Priority, 2 Color Priority, 3 Paint Overlay); `crs:GrainAmount`, `crs:GrainSize`, `crs:GrainFrequency` (as Roughness). Sliders have Lightroom's names and ranges, so numbers carry over one for one; the look is darktable's, only close to Lightroom's.
- **Glow:** `crs:GlowStyle`, `crs:GlowRange`, `crs:GlowSpread`, `crs:GlowWarmth` are read at import only when the photo has no LightBench edit, and **never written**; Glow's amount field is unknown, so a read Glow keeps its style and settings but stays off until the user sets an amount.
- **Lightroom's masks:** `crs:LocalTexture`, `crs:LocalClarity2012` (an assumed name) and `crs:LocalDehaze` are now read with Part 23's masks.
- What Lightroom has no field for (the vignette's centre and shape, colour grain, a grain seed, darktable's Advanced settings) is kept when Lightroom's values are read.

### Files

New: `engine/develop/effects.ts` (settings, checks, slider mappings and a CPU twin of every formula), `engine/gpu/effects.wgsl` (Dehaze, Texture/Clarity and Glow passes), `effects-display.wgsl` (the per-pixel effects, part of the display module), `effects-stage.ts`, `develop/EffectsPanel.tsx`, `sidecar/lightroomEffects.ts`; tests `engine/develop/effects.test.ts`, `sidecar/lightroomEffects.test.ts`, `develop/effects-paste.test.ts`. Changed: `develop-display.wgsl`, `develop-engine.ts`, `mask-stage.ts`, `mask.wgsl`, `process.ts` (`EffectsParams`, `EFFECTS_V1`), `settings.ts`, `masks.ts`, `mask-render.ts`, `BasicPanel.tsx`, `DevelopView.tsx`, `MasksPanel.tsx`, `tools.tsx`, `sectionEyes.ts`, `help.ts`, `paste.ts`, `catalog.worker.ts`, `export/run.ts`, `ExportDialog.tsx`, `xmp.ts`, `sync.ts`, `changes.ts`, `watch.ts`, `import/run.ts`, `batch.ts`, `lightroomMasks.ts`, `bench/run.ts`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`.

### Verified

Headless Chromium with software WebGPU (SwiftShader), `?gpu=readback`, on a 4-core machine shared with other agents' test runs; each script run alone. Photos: `test-srgb.jpg` (1600 × 1072) and, to keep SwiftShader's times bearable, the same photo at 800 × 536 (`test-small.jpg`). Scratch scripts in `scratchpad/fx/`: `engine-fx.mjs`, `engine-seam.mjs`, `dbg-dehaze.mjs`, `e2e-fx.mjs`, `e2e-export.mjs`, `identity.mjs`.

- **Engine** (`engine-fx.mjs`, the engine driven directly, 400 px renders of the 800 px photo; **19 of 19** over two runs, the second after the Dehaze fix below): each of Highlight Priority, Color Priority and Paint Overlay vignettes, monochrome and colour grain, Texture, Clarity, Dehaze +60 and −60, and Glow's three styles changed the photo (mean change 1.3 for Texture 80 to 39 for the Paint Overlay vignette); the Highlight Priority vignette darkened the corner and left the centre; **effects at 0 rendered every byte as without them**, and the same engine drew the plain edit identically after drawing effects; grain with the same seed twice was identical and another seed differed. A full-size render with Texture, Clarity, Dehaze and Glow came out at full size.
- **Dehaze found a bug, fixed:** the first engine run's Dehaze +60 changed nothing (after the fix: mean change 22.6, the centre darker and clearer, 142 → 100). The cause was darktable's depth rule (above): the JPEG's 95 % dark-channel level was 1.63 in LightBench's scene values (A0 3.24, 2.96, 2.73), so the depth was −0.55 and t_min 1; the D80 NEF with its bright sky gave the same (A0 2.05, 1.75, 1.65; depth −0.05). With the depth measured against max(1, A0's smallest channel): depth 0.58, t_min 0.89 at Distance 0.2, mean change 16.8 at +60 and 28.5 at +100 (`dbg-dehaze.mjs`); unit tests cover both cases.
- **Tile seams** (`engine-seam.mjs`, 3 of 3): the 1600 × 1072 photo at full size is drawn as four 1024 px tiles; with Texture 60, Clarity 60, Dehaze 50 and Glow 60 (mean change 17.2), the column-to-column step of the effects' difference across the seam at x = 1024 was 2.12 against 1.73 on the columns beside it, and 4.65 against 4.90 across y = 1024: no visible seam.
- **End to end in the app** (`e2e-fx.mjs`, **30 of 30** over two runs: the full run, 24 of 25, and the Masks part again after the script's reference was fixed; the failed check was the script's, below):
  - **Each slider** (Texture 100, Clarity 100, Dehaze 70, the vignette's Amount −80, Glow 90) changed the photo on the Develop canvas (mean 1.6, 4.7, 29.0, 37.2, 5.4) and **at 0 was the photo again, every byte**; Grain 80 changed it (2.8) and stored a seed.
  - **Undo/redo:** Ctrl+Z undid the grain step (no grain), Ctrl+Y redid it with the same seed; each change was one history step named after its tool.
  - **Eyes:** the Grain section's eye (shown once grain is on) gave the photo without grain exactly, nothing saved, and back; the Presence eye hid Texture and kept grain; the Effects group eye hid grain, kept Texture, and the badge read "Hidden: Effects".
  - **Masks:** on a radial mask, local Texture, Clarity, Dehaze and Glow each changed the photo inside the mask (0.65, 2.6, 31.9, 6.5 under the mask's overlay) and **nothing outside (0.000)**; the mask was stored with them back at 0. (The first run compared against the photo before the mask was drawn, so its overlay counted as a change; the second compares against the drawn mask with its sliders at 0.)
  - **Persistence:** after a reload the effects were in the catalog; **C.xmp** held them in `lightbench:Develop` and no `crs:` effects fields (a sidecar without Camera Raw settings); a **virtual copy** had the same effects with a new grain seed (1217049951 → 1001067736); a **fresh library** importing C.JPG with that C.xmp read them back, and the panels showed Color Priority and Clarity +40.
  - **Export:** the exported JPEG had the effects (the Color Priority vignette's corner 130 against 235 without).
- **Export files** (`e2e-export.mjs`): the same photo exported twice gave the same pixels and the same bytes except three timestamps every export writes (EXIF DateTime, `xmp:MetadataDate`, the embedded ICC profile's creation time; on `main` too): without effects, with effects (Clarity, the vignette, grain), and **the plain JPEG export from this branch was byte-identical to `main`'s** (the same SHA-256 with those timestamps blanked). With **Dither JPEGs** ticked the file differed (pixels by up to 10 levels after JPEG compression). (The full e2e run's dither check compared raw hashes and failed on the timestamps; this script replaces it.)
- **Old edits unchanged** (`identity.mjs`, Part 17's fifteen edits on four photos — two NEFs, an ARW, the JPEG — with Remove spots, a tone curve, Detail, the mixer and grading, plus three new ones: masks with the old local sliders (radial, brush, luminance range with Refine), masks with a tone curve and spots on a NEF, and an edit carrying every effect at its default): the branch against `main` `44c29cd` gave **108 of 108 identical** for the full-size 8-bit export, the 1024 px export, the 512 px thumbnail, the histogram statistics and the Develop canvas at fit and at 100 %. The branch's first run hit the script's known transient OPFS `NotFoundError` from its third edit on (twice); those edits were run again, each in its own browser, and all matched.
- **Times on SwiftShader** (relative only; the machine was shared, and the same render took 17 s in one run and 27 s in another): a 400 px render of the 800 px photo took 10–11 s with a vignette or grain against 17 s for the first, plain render of that run (no measurable cost); in the other run 27 s plain, 68 s with Texture, 54 s with Clarity (the contrast & texture pyramid), 46–75 s with Dehaze (the first includes its statistics), 23–24 s with Glow; on the 1600 px photo 93 s plain and 315 s with Texture. The full-size render of the 1600 px photo took 48 s plain and 326 s with Texture, Clarity, Dehaze and Glow together. A real GPU's times are for the benchmark's ten new rows.
- **Unit tests** (`npx vitest run`: **1159 tests in 112 files**; `main` `22be61b` has 1096 in 109, so 63 new):
  - `engine/develop/effects.test.ts` (40): settings checks (no key without effects, defaults dropped, damaged edits safe, a new seed when grain starts and for a virtual copy); the vignette (Amount through darktable's importer table, weight 0 at the centre and 1 in the corners, Roundness, the three styles and Highlights); grain (darktable's 3D simplex noise, Roughness 50 = its three octaves exactly, the seed choosing the pattern, the same noise at any drawing size, Size and Amount through its importer, the paper response, monochrome grain on L\* only and colour grain per channel); contrast & texture (band weights, radii within the crop, Clarity's and Texture's bands, LightCraft's mid-tone weight, Wiener-weighted details, **a tile computed with its margin equal to the whole drawing**); Dehaze (window sizes and t_min, the ambient light and depth, darktable's depth while A0 ≤ 1 and a positive one above, haze removed and added, the stored maps equal to darktable's transmission for any strength, **the strength never derived from the image**, a constant-eps guided filter, statistics within the crop); Glow (threshold, Spread, Warmth, the three styles); the flags and the uniform.
  - `sidecar/lightroomEffects.test.ts` (11): a synthetic Camera Raw sidecar's fields read; one-for-one mapping (GrainFrequency as Roughness, Style 2 as Color Priority); absent fields as Lightroom's defaults; damaged values; what Lightroom has no field for kept; writing only into Camera Raw sidecars, only changed groups, in place, **never Glow**; every written field one that is read; the `lightbench:` round trip; Read changes.
  - `develop/effects-paste.test.ts` (7): the paste items and their groups, each item copying its own part, pasted grain keeping the target's seed, a seed alone no change, the checklist text, a virtual copy's reseeding of edit and history, and the Export dither option.
  - Updated: `mask-render.test.ts` (16 values a mask, the fourth plane only when used), `sectionEyes.test.ts` (the Effects group and the Presence eye), `bench/plan.test.ts` (the ten rows), `masks-sidecar.test.ts` (Lightroom's local Clarity read).
- **Full checks from a clean install** (`npm ci && npx tsc -b && npx oxlint && npx vitest run && npm run build`): all pass; oxlint shows only the two warnings `main` already has (`syscheck/report.tsx`, `LibraryView.tsx`).

### Limitations and unverified

- **Real GPU:** everything was measured on SwiftShader on a shared 4-core machine; its times are relative only. The benchmark's ten new rows are there for a real GPU.
- **Lightroom fields:** no sidecar written by Lightroom was available; all names and encodings come from Exiv2, the Lightroom SDK, darktable's importer and secondary sources (Cowork). Unverified: Vignette Style 2 = Color Priority and 3 = Paint Overlay; `GrainFrequency` as Roughness; `LocalClarity2012`'s name and the Local* scales; the defaults of absent Midpoint, Roundness, Size and Roughness; Glow's field names (a third-party source), their encodings and ranges, and its amount field (unknown). Every slider mapping (vignette gain table, grain size and strength, Texture/Clarity gains, Dehaze's −100…100 to darktable's strength) was tuned here and not compared with Lightroom.
- **Glow's ranges and maths** are LightBench's own (Adobe publishes none); its source is drawn at half size or less (at most 1024 px long), so at export the glow is smoother than the photo's finest detail (its radii are relative, so it looks the same at fit and export).
- **Texture at fit:** as in darktable, detail finer than a screen pixel can't be shown zoomed out (the levels under 2 px are left out of the drawing); the export uses the full-size detail. Check at 100 %.
- **Measurements without Dehaze:** the tone equalizer's mask and the masks' luminance/colour ranges are measured without Dehaze (they see the photo before it).
- **Memory:** the export's mask planes grow with Texture/Clarity's margin (they cover the tile plus the blur margin when both are on).
- **Patents:** the facts and constraints above come from Cowork's check; nothing here is legal advice. No search was done for Highlight/Color Priority vignetting, LightBench's Roughness design or Glow.

## Diagnostics (flight recorder)

**What it does.** Settings → Diagnostics has a **Record diagnostics** switch, off by default. While it is on, LightBench keeps a log of what happens in Develop and in its GPU engines, writing each entry to the browser's storage as it happens, so the log survives a crash or a hard power-off; **Export diagnostics…** saves it as a `.json` file (the save dialog where the browser has one, as Backup does, else a download) and **Clear** empties it. A small readout in the bottom-right corner shows the GPU memory LightBench's engines have made while it is on (also with `?memdebug` in the address, without recording anything). Added after a report of the whole computer freezing in Develop (Part 14, "Replaced textures freed while drawings overlap").

| What | Detail |
|---|---|
| The computer (once per session) | App version and commit, the user agent and, where the browser gives them, its client hints (platform and version, architecture, model, browser versions), display mode (browser tab or installed app), device pixel ratio, screen size, the GPU adapter (vendor, architecture, device, description, whether it is a software adapter, whether it looks integrated), its `shader-f16` / `timestamp-query` / `float32-filterable` features and key limits, `hardwareConcurrency`, `deviceMemory`, and the CPU budget (Part 22). The adapter is also logged each time an engine is made. The settings page says plainly that the browser cannot see the graphics card's real memory use, its temperature or other programs. |
| Actions | Each history step with the settings that changed and their new values (`"detail.noise.luminance": 35`; lists such as spots and masks by their length), the zoom level, the tools open (white balance picker, crop, Guided Upright, Remove, Masks, Before), the module (Library, Develop, Export, System check), and each photo opened as **a number for this session, the camera model, RAW or JPEG and its pixel size** — never a file name, folder, path or pixels (`diagnostics/anon.ts`). |
| Engine events | Each drawing, histogram and curve measure, loupe, Detail run, RAW-stage rebuild, export render and scene sample, with its duration and, when it ended, the engine's texture and buffer bytes, the replaced textures still waiting (count and bytes) and the drawings open (`DevelopEngine.memoryStats()`); GPU errors, `GpuWorkError`s, a lost device and Develop's recovery; page errors. Each AI depth run (Part 24, `ai-depth`): the model file (fp16/fp32), GPU or CPU, the model's input size, the durations of the as-shot rendering, loading the model, the model run and the whole, the stored map's size, or the error. Each AI Remove run (Part 26, `ai-remove`): the model version, the GPU adapter's name, GPU or CPU, the crop and patch sizes, the stored patch's characters, the durations of loading, encoding, a denoising step (mean) and decoding and the whole, or the error, or that it was cancelled. Object select (Part 27, `ai-mask`): per photo encoded, the model file (fp16/fp32), GPU or CPU, the card, the input size and the durations of the as-shot rendering, loading and encoding; per selection, its kind (new, add, remove, box, compute), the matte's size and characters and the decoder's and total durations; one summary per hover session (pointer moves, previews, previews dropped while one ran, median and 95th-percentile decoder time); errors. Never a prompt's coordinates, a file name or pixels. |
| Storage | IndexedDB (`lightbench-diagnostics`), batches written every 0.5 s (or every 200 entries, and when the page is hidden) with strict durability. A ring of the newest **5,000 entries**; each entry's data is cut at 600 characters of JSON (the session description at 4,000), so the log stays a few MB. |
| Cost | Writes are asynchronous and batched; nothing waits for them in a drawing. When the switch is off, the engine has no trace: its methods are called straight through and nothing is timed, kept or written. Rendering is the same either way (Part 14's identity check for this change ran with it off). |

How to use it: turn on **Settings → Diagnostics → Record diagnostics**, work until the problem happens (also after a forced restart: the log is still there), open the same page and click **Export diagnostics…**. The page also shows the graphics adapter the browser gives LightBench. When that adapter looks like integrated Intel graphics, Develop shows a one-time notice (dismissible, remembered per adapter): "LightBench is running on <name>. If this computer has a faster graphics card, set your browser to 'High performance' in Windows Settings → System → Display → Graphics, then restart the browser." The browser cannot tell whether the computer has another graphics card, so the notice doesn't say it has one. LightBench already asks for `powerPreference: 'high-performance'`; the owner's Chrome on Windows gave it the integrated Intel UHD Graphics anyway (Task Manager, 2026-10-19), Edge the NVIDIA card.

Tests: `diagnostics/diagnostics.test.ts` (the ring, batching and timed writes, a new session continuing the stored log, off records nothing, the switch remembered, export and Clear; photo numbers and summaries, the settings diff, adapter names and the integrated hint) and `e2e-diagnostics.mjs` (on in Settings, the readout, an edit and the picker in Develop, a reload, Export: the file has the session with the GPU adapter, the photo as number/kind/size, the Exposure steps with values, render events with memory, the picker, two sessions, and not the file's name or folder; Clear; off again hides the readout): 16 of 16.

## Develop groups start collapsed (owner, 2026-10-19)

From the owner's first real use: too many tools were open at once. Library and Import changes from the same feedback are in [PHASE2.md](PHASE2.md#owners-feedback-from-first-real-use-2026-10-19-batch-1).

| Behaviour | Detail |
|---|---|
| Groups | Every tool group (Crop & Lens, Remove, Masks, Basic, Tone Curve, Colour Mixer, Colour Grading, Detail, Effects) starts **closed** the first time Develop opens. What the user opens or closes is remembered in this browser (`lightbench.develop.openGroups`, a list of open groups). The old list of closed groups isn't carried over, so existing users also start with everything closed once. |
| Sections | Secondary sections start closed and are remembered too (`lightbench.develop.openSections`): **Advanced** in Basic, Tone Curve, Colour Mixer, Colour Grading and Effects (Vignette, Grain), and the **Lens** and **Transform** sections of Crop & Lens. A closed Lens shows On or Off next to its title; a closed Transform shows "Changed" when it holds a correction. Guided Upright opens Transform while it runs. |
| Opening | The jump bar opens the group it jumps to, as before, and **R** opens Crop & Lens. A tool started from its shortcut opens its group if it is closed, without scrolling an open one: Remove (Q) → Remove, Masks → Masks, the curve's targeted adjustment → Tone Curve, the mixer's targeted adjustment or Point Color's picker → Colour Mixer, the white balance picker (W) → Basic, Guided Upright → Crop & Lens. |
| Storage | `develop/panelState.ts`. Reads and writes are wrapped in try/catch. With storage blocked (a private window, cleared site data) the panel still opens and closes; it just isn't remembered. |

Tests: `develop/panelState.test.ts` (everything closed at first; opened and closed groups remembered by a new store over the same storage, as after a reload; subscribers told only on a change; blocked storage, unreadable JSON and non-string entries). Headless Chromium (`?gpu=readback`): all nine groups closed on first use; the jump bar's Basic opened Basic; Basic's Advanced, Lens and Transform started closed; with Basic and Detail open and Transform opened, a reload kept exactly Basic and Detail open, and Transform open; with Basic closed, **W** opened it; **R** opened Crop & Lens.

## Import speed (owner, 2026-10-20)

From the owner's first real use: about 1,500 photos (Nikon D5200 RAW+JPEG pairs) took several minutes to import from a card. The import copied one file at a time, read each copy back to check it, made the optional backup copy the same way, and added the photo, its sidecar, thumbnail and stacks in the same loop. The owner chose all four changes below. Decisions in [USABILITY.md](USABILITY.md#import-speed-owner-2026-10-20); the earlier stacking fixes of the same session are in [PHASE2.md](PHASE2.md#raw--jpeg-stacking-on-import-owner-2026-10-19).

| Change | Detail |
|---|---|
| 1. Every step timed | Each photo's steps are timed (`import/timing.ts`): **read** (pieces read from the card), **hash** (SHA-256 of what was read), **write** (the copy), **verify** (reading the copy back and hashing it), **backup** (the whole backup copy and its sidecar's), **sidecar** (finding it on the card, writing it next to the copy), **catalog** (adding the photo; a transaction's time is shared by the photos added in it), **thumbnail** (saving the camera preview) and **stacking**. Written to the Diagnostics log (Settings → Diagnostics, when Record diagnostics is on): `import-scan` once per card (files, RAWs, JPEGs, bytes, listing ms, reading wall time, the probe workers' summed time, MB/s), `import-photo` per photo (its number in the import, RAW or JPEG, which pass, the batch it was added in, bytes, outcome and the step times), `import-done` (counts, RAW-only skips, whether it was stopped, workers, bytes, wall time, MB/s, and per step the summed ms and its percentage of the work) and `import-details` (the background pass: photos, ms, time spent waiting for the CPU budget). No file names or folders, as everywhere in the log. The finished Import window shows one muted line: "123 photos, 748 MB in 20 s · copying 51 % · verifying 24 %" (the two largest groups of at least 5 %; groups are copying = read + hash + write, verifying, backup, sidecars, library, thumbnails, stacking). Steps of photos copied at the same time overlap, so shares are of the summed step time, and the wall time is separate. |
| 2. Copy first, details later | The import is finished, and says so ("Every file was copied and checked against the card."), when every file is copied, verified, and in the Library with its camera preview as thumbnail, its sidecar's stack and its RAW+JPEG twin's stack. The rest is done by `import/details.ts` in the background: the sidecar's ratings, flag, label, keywords, metadata, collections, virtual copies, edits, snapshots and Lightroom settings (else a JPEG's own XMP), the camera's IPTC/EXIF fields, pairing with twins imported earlier or in other folders (`stackRawJpegPairs`, which the old import ran at its end), the sidecars of the stacked photos (written only then, so they carry the ratings and keywords), and the develop and metadata presets for new photos (`photosAdded`, which render the full thumbnails). What is left is kept with each photo in the catalog, in the same transaction as the photo: catalog **schema 17** adds `import_pending (photo_id, details)` (JSON: the card sidecar's text, the embedded XMP, the camera fields, the twin to stack with), deleted when done or with the photo. The pass is background work on the CPU budget (it waits while the copying, Export or Remove use the slots), works in batches of 24 in the order the photos were added, shows "Finishing imported photos (ratings, keywords and edits from sidecars, presets): n of m" in the left panel and in the finished Import window, and is started again when the Library opens, so closing the tab loses nothing. A rating, flag, label or edit the person gives a photo before its details arrive wins over the sidecar's (they may cull the RAWs while the JPEGs are still copying); untouched photos get exactly what the old import gave them. |
| 3. RAW first | With **Stack each JPEG under its RAW** on, every photo except the JPEG twins is copied first, in capture order, then the JPEG twins, in capture order (`import/schedule.ts`). The Import window says "Copying the RAW files first: n of m", then "The RAW files are in the library. Copying their JPEGs: n of m". It can be closed meanwhile with **Continue in the background**: the import goes on (`import/session.ts`), the Library shows the photos as they are added (refreshed at most once a second), and the left panel shows the import with **Show** and **Stop**, then its summary. Each JPEG joins its RAW's stack as it is added (feedback-1's per-pair stacking); a JPEG whose stack was cut off by a closed tab is stacked by the background details on the next start. |
| 4. Parallel copies, RAW only | 2–3 photos are copied at once: foreground work on the CPU budget, wanting 3 and getting at least 2 (`?importWorkers=N` sets it, for measurements). Every copy and backup copy is still read back and checked. Photos start in the planned order, and each is added to the library as soon as its copy is checked, with the others checked by then, in one catalog transaction (up to 16; RAW+JPEG twins never in the same one, so the second is stacked with the first). Adding them strictly in order was tried first: small JPEGs then waited, copied but not in the library, behind a slower RAW, and a tab closed at that moment left them out of the library (seen in the interrupt test). At most twice as many photos as workers are started past the oldest one not yet added, which bounds memory; each copy holds one 8 MiB piece. **RAW only (skip JPEGs that have a RAW twin)** replaces "Also import the JPEGs shot alongside RAW files" (the same setting, reversed: off by default, kept in import presets as before, `includePaired: false`). The JPEGs it leaves on the card are counted in the summary ("40 JPEGs with a RAW twin left on the card (RAW only)"). |

What stays as before: the destination files and their names, the numbering of a clash (`-1`), the dated folders, the backup folder, the sidecars written next to the copies, the catalog entries and the stacks; a photo that fails fails alone, with the same message; Stop ("Stop after the photos being copied") lets the copies in progress finish and adds them, and copies nothing more; "Already imported" marks the photos already in the library, as before. Two photos that would write the same file name (the photo, its sidecar, or a backup copy) never run at the same time: the one earlier in capture order goes first, as before, and if RAW first would put it later it is moved up (`planImport`). Rename numbers come from the capture order, as before, not from the copying order.

Resuming an import that was cut off (the tab closed): photos already in the library are marked "Already imported" and unticked, as before. A file of the same name and size at the destination that the library doesn't have (a copy finished just before the tab closed, not yet added) was also marked "Already imported" and unticked, so it stayed out of the library; it is now marked **Copied, not in library** and stays ticked: importing it checks it against the card and adds it without copying it again (a photo removed from the library but left on disk shows the same way). The header counts them ("3 copied before but not in the library").

One change to a copy itself: an **empty** file with the photo's name at the destination is written over instead of numbering the copy. The browser creates the file before writing it, so a copy cut off by closing the tab leaves an empty file, and importing again used to give `DSC_0001-1.NEF` next to it.

Tests: `import/schedule.test.ts` (capture order; RAW only drops JPEGs with a RAW twin, also case-insensitive, and keeps lone JPEGs and a restarted file number; RAW first puts the JPEG twins after everything else, each in capture order; capture order kept without stacking or with RAW first off; a photo wanting an earlier photo's name, sidecar or backup name waits for it, ignoring case; a JPEG twin is moved up when an earlier-phase photo waits for it; the pool: never more than the workers, commits in order or as soon as done, waits for dependencies, Stop finishes and commits the photos running, bounds how far it runs ahead, follows a changing number of workers, batches what is done and keeps twins apart), `import/timing.test.ts` (sums, MB/s and shares; the summary line "1,500 photos, 38 GB in 6 min 12 s · copying 71 % · thumbnails 18 %"; sizes and durations; timing a step that throws), `import/details.test.ts` on the real catalog (an import cut off after the RAW's details and before the JPEG was stacked: a new pass on the next start finishes the rest, stacks the JPEG with the RAW on top, pairs a JPEG with the RAW of an earlier import in another folder and writes the four sidecars; a stack made at import is left alone; a rating given meanwhile wins over the sidecar's; the pass waits while foreground work holds the CPU budget), `catalog/import-pending.test.ts` (schema 17 upgrade adds only the empty table; details kept with photos added with them, listed oldest first, forgotten when done or when the photo is removed, none for a photo already in the library) and `import/copy.test.ts` (an empty file of the same name is written over; reading, hashing, writing and verifying are timed).

Headless Chromium (`?gpu=readback`, a disk-backed profile), a fake Nikon card made from feedback-1's: 60 RAW+JPEG pairs (the two real NEFs and their same-time JPEGs, alternating, each with its own name `DSC_0001…0060` and capture time patched into the EXIF, over two days), a RAW's `DSC_0003.xmp` (4 stars, keyword) and a JPEG's own `DSC_0005.JPG.xmp`, and three lone JPEGs in `101D5200`, one of them `DSC_0001.JPG` on shot 1's day (a name clash): 123 files, 748 MB. Imported with each option combination on this branch and on feedback-1 (the sequential import), comparing every destination file (path, size, SHA-256; sidecars by their text with stack ids and dates blanked), the backup folder, every catalog photo (folder, name, card name, capture time, rating, flag, label, edited), keywords and stacks:

| Options | Destination files | Backup | Photos | Stacks | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Defaults (original names, stacking, RAW first) | 243 | — | 123 | 60 | identical |
| Backup + "Date + number" names | 244 | 125 | 123 | 60 | identical |
| RAW only | 64 | — | 63 | 0 | identical; "60 JPEGs with a RAW twin left on the card (RAW only)" |
| Stacking off | 125 | — | 123 | 0 | identical |

The clash came out the same both ways (`DSC_0001.JPG` for shot 1's JPEG, `DSC_0001-1.JPG` for the lone one); the RAW's card sidecar gave both twins 4 stars and its keyword, the JPEG's own sidecar only the JPEG.

Interrupted imports (defaults): the page reloaded during the JPEG pass ("Copying their JPEGs: 9 of 59") left 76 photos, every copied JPEG stacked with its RAW, no pending details after the Library opened, no empty files; importing the card again marked those 76 "Already imported" and imported the other 47, and the result was identical to the uninterrupted import. Reloaded during the RAW pass ("25 of 64"): two empty files from the copies in progress, written over by the second import (no `-1` copies); identical again. Before photos were added as soon as checked, one such run lost `DSC_0019.JPG` (copied, not added, then marked "Already imported"), which led to that change and to the **Copied, not in library** mark; checked by taking a photo out of the library: the Import window showed "14 already imported · 1 copied before but not in the library", imported it without a new copy, and stacked it with its RAW. **Stop** after 3 photos: the 2 being copied finished, 5 imported, "38 photos not copied (stopped)", no empty or uncatalogued files. The Diagnostics export had `import-scan`, 123 `import-photo` entries with step times, `import-done` and `import-details`, and no file names.

Timings on this machine (4 cores, so a CPU budget of 2 and 2 copy workers; the card and the destination are on one shared, slow disk, and OPFS in a headless profile): no faster. Click to "Import finished", 123 files, 748 MB: sequential (feedback-1) 21.9, 16.5, 16.2 and 21.0 s; this branch 21.7, 18.2, 18.9 and 25.1 s (16.8 and 25.8 s with Diagnostics off); 3 workers 20.5 and 19.7 s; 1 worker 25.8, 23.8, 24.1 and 25.9 s. The runs vary by ±25 %, so the difference between the sequential import and 2–3 workers is within the noise. The step times say why: writing and reading back take half the work (write 25 %, verify 24 %, read 18 %, hash 9 %, catalog 8–12 %, thumbnails 6–8 %), at 35–42 MB/s, which is this disk's speed; with a backup the backup copy is 42 % (36.6 s against 37.7 s sequential). Copying several files at once can only help where the card and the destination are different devices, or with many small files; the owner's Diagnostics log will show which applies there. Reading the card (the Import window's probe, before importing) took 13–15 s here, as before.

Not verified: a real card in a USB reader and a separate destination disk (only OPFS here); Windows and its permission prompts; the interrupt test with a backup or renaming (a second import with other options isn't the same import, so it can't be compared).

## Zoom levels (owner, 2026-10-19)

From the owner's first real use: "Would like to be able to choose my zoom level in develop so I can really zoom in to check sharpness etc." Until now Develop had only Fit and 100 % (Z, or a click on the photo). Zoom is a view only: the edit, its history, sidecars, exports and thumbnails don't change (identity check below).

| What | Detail |
|---|---|
| Levels | **Fit, 25 %, 50 %, 100 %, 200 %, 400 % and 800 %**. 100 % is one photo pixel per CSS pixel, as before (on a 2× screen, two device pixels). Fill isn't offered. |
| Toolbar | A **Zoom** menu in Develop's toolbar (where the Fit / 100 % button was) shows the current level and chooses one. Off while cropping and drawing Guided Upright lines, whose views are always fitted. |
| Keys | **Z**: Fit and back to the last level chosen (100 % until another is chosen; remembered in this browser, `lightbench.develop.zoom`). **=** (also **+** and the keypad's +) zooms in, **−** (also the keypad's −) zooms out, one level at a time; Fit takes its place among the levels by its size (a photo that fits at 60 % steps 50 % → Fit → 100 %), and a level the same size as Fit is passed over. New actions `develop.zoomIn` / `develop.zoomOut` in Settings → Keyboard, in Develop and its on-photo tools (curve and mixer targets, Remove, Masks), like Z. Ctrl+= / Ctrl+− stay the browser's page zoom, and Ctrl+Alt+= / Ctrl+Alt+− stay the interface size. |
| Wheel | **Ctrl or ⌘ + wheel** over the photo steps one level per notch toward the pointer (a trackpad pinch, which browsers send as Ctrl + wheel, too: about 60 px of movement per step). Over the photo it never zooms the page; elsewhere the browser's page zoom is untouched. The plain wheel does nothing on the photo, as before. |
| Click and drag | A click on the fitted photo zooms to the last level chosen; a click on a zoomed photo fits it again. Dragging pans at any level above Fit. Panning stops at the photo's edges (the centre is kept within them, so there is no dead zone to drag back through); a photo smaller than the window at that level is centred. |
| Toward the pointer | Every zoom from the wheel or a click keeps the photo point under the pointer where it is (`develop/zoom.ts` `centerAround`), unless that would show past the photo's edge. The menu and the keys keep the point at the window's centre; Z from Fit returns to where the photo was last looked at. |
| Above 100 % | The visible part of the photo is drawn **at 100 %** (one photo pixel per output pixel, with Detail at full size, exactly as the 100 % view draws it) into a small texture, and each of its pixels is copied to a k × k block of the canvas **without smoothing** (nearest neighbour; `engine/gpu/zoom-blit.ts`). So 200 % and 400 % show the photo's real pixels enlarged, with hard edges, to judge sharpness and noise, and cost no more than 100 %: the drawing is about the window's size divided by k (plus a pixel at each side). The photo's placement is rounded to whole canvas pixels above 100 % so blocks line up with the pixel grid. Clipping, the sensor-clipping overlay, the mask overlay, soft proofing, the mixer's Visualize view and the sharpening mask preview are part of the 100 % drawing and are enlarged with it. |
| Below 100 % | Unchanged: 25 % and 50 % use the scene's mip chain (area-correct averaging), and Detail at the matching level, as Fit always has. |
| Tools | Overlays on the photo map through the placement actually drawn (`engine.layout`), so they line up at every level: Remove pins, outlines and the brush; mask pins, handles, shapes and the brush; the targeted adjustment tools of the Tone Curve and Colour Mixer and Point Color's picker; the white balance picker and its loupe. The histogram, clipping triangles and Auto measure the whole photo and don't depend on the zoom. Before/after (`\`) and the section eyes redraw at the same level. Crop and Guided Upright fit the photo when they start and show the whole straightened photo, as before; the zoom menu is off while they run. |

**Files.** `develop/zoom.ts` (levels, stepping, zoom toward a point, the centre's limits), `develop/useZoom.ts` (the menu, keys, click and Ctrl/⌘ + wheel), `develop/ZoomControl.tsx` (the menu), `engine/gpu/zoom-blit.ts` (the enlargement: plan, shader, pass), and in `DevelopEngine`: `layout` rounds the placement above 100 %, `render` draws the visible part at 100 % and enlarges it (`draw` with a plan). Nothing else in the engine changed; exports, thumbnails, the histogram and the culling 100 % renderer never pass through the enlargement.

**Tests.**
- **Unit** (`develop/zoom.test.ts`, 10; `settings/keymap.test.ts` +1 check): the levels and labels; stepping in and out with Fit among the levels for a large photo (fits at 15 %), a small one (70 %) and one that fits at exactly 100 %; remembered levels; the centre's limits and centring; zooming toward four points through Fit → 100 → 200 → 400 → 800 → 25 → 400 % keeps the point under the pointer exactly wherever the edges allow; panning within the limits; the enlargement plan: every canvas pixel shows the photo pixel its centre falls in, inside the 100 % drawing, in whole k × k blocks, for 200/400/800 % and a photo smaller than the window, and the shader's uniform. The zoom keys conflict with nothing and Ctrl+= / Ctrl+− stay unbound.
- **Headless Chromium** (`e2e-zoom.mjs`, `?gpu=readback` engine on SwiftShader, a 1600 × 1072 JPEG in a 1440 × 900 window, run in parts; the last run of each part): 44 of 44 checks.
  - *Menu:* Fit, 25 % … 800 %; 25 % and 50 % draw the photo 400 × 268 and 800 × 536 px; 100 % has no repeated pixels; 200 %, 400 % and 800 % consist entirely of 2×2, 4×4 and 8×8 blocks of identical pixels (802, 378 and 191 different blocks in the 200 px centre), starting on the photo's own pixel grid; each 400 % and 800 % block is the photo pixel shown at 200 % (0 of 1,600 differ), and the 200 % blocks equal the centred 100 % view's pixels where that view sits on whole pixels (0 of 3,600 differ).
  - *Keys:* = = = = = − − − − − − − Z Z + keypad+ keypad+ keypad− Z Z gave 1 2 4 8 8 4 2 1 Fit 50 % 25 % 25 % Fit 25 % 50 % Fit 100 % Fit 100 % Fit (the photo fits between 50 % and 100 %); the page itself wasn't zoomed.
  - *Ctrl + wheel* at two points, Fit → 100 → 200 → 400 % and back to 200 %: compared with the photo's pixels, the point under the pointer stayed within 1 canvas pixel each time (mean error 0.0 levels above 100 %, 0.4–0.9 at 100 %); the page wasn't zoomed. A click on the fitted photo went to the last level (200 %) with the clicked point under the pointer. A 100 × 60 px drag at 400 % moved the picture by exactly that (100 % of the compared pixels), keeping whole blocks; a click when zoomed fitted the photo.
  - *Tools at 200 %:* Before/after (`\`) stayed at 200 % with whole blocks, Before darker without the edit's Exposure +1; the histogram (element screenshots) was identical at 200 % and at Fit, for after and for Before (controls: Fit again identical, Before different). A Remove spot's pin stayed under the pointer through Ctrl + wheel to 200 % (within 0.3 px), the brush staying on. A radial mask's White on Black overlay stayed centred under the pointer (within 0.5 px) with (2 / fit)² its area at Fit (12.89×, expected 12.93×), its centre pin too, in whole 2×2 blocks. The white balance loupe showed at 200 %. The crop tool fitted the photo with the zoom menu off, and was at Fit with the menu on after Esc.
- **Old edits unchanged** (`identity.mjs`, main against this branch, three edits of the JPEG: defaults, a process-2 tone edit, a Colour Mixer edit): full-size and 1024 px sRGB exports, the 512 px thumbnail, the histogram statistics and the Develop canvas at Fit and at 100 % have identical SHA-256 hashes.
- `npx tsc -b`, `npx oxlint` (the two existing warnings), `npx vitest run` and `npm run build` passed after a clean `npm ci`.
- **Not checked:** a real GPU (WebGPU canvas rather than read-back) and screens with a device pixel ratio other than 1 (there 100 % keeps its smooth upscale and the blocks above 100 % are `zoom × ratio` device pixels, uneven when that isn't whole); trackpad pinch; Safari and Firefox; the curve and mixer targeted adjustment tools, Point Color's picker and Guided Upright above 100 % (they map through the same placement as the checked overlays); a RAW photo at 200 %+ (only JPEGs were zoomed here); exports of RAW edits (a RAW identity case was too slow on the software GPU and was left out).

## Hardening and cleanup (owner, 2026-10-21)

The build the owner chose after the roadmap review ([ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md)): no visible features apart from the graphics messages. Four parts: an error guard for Develop's own GPU engine, a speed check on every release, release tags with one-click rollback, and these docs brought up to date. Nothing renders differently (identity check below); no process version.

### 1. GPU error guard for Develop

**Before.** Part 14 made Develop replace its engine after a lost device, with no limit, and the errors WebGPU reports without losing the device (validation, out of memory, internal) only reached the console (and the Diagnostics log when it was on): the drawing stayed black or unfinished. Develop's engine wasn't guarded because error scopes belong to the whole device, and the histogram, the loupe and Auto use it at the same time (Part 14, "Guarded GPU work").

| What | Detail |
|---|---|
| Errors reported to Develop | `DevelopEngine.onGpuError(fn)`: every error on the engine's device that no error scope caught (`uncapturederror`), as `out-of-memory`, `validation` or `internal` (`engine/gpu/alloc-check.ts` `gpuErrorKind`). Written to the console and, with Diagnostics on, as an `engine` / `gpu-error` entry, as before; engines used inside `guarded` (Export, the background developer, the 100 % view) still catch theirs there. |
| What Develop does | `develop/gpuRecovery.ts`, a small state machine without GPU or React (unit-tested). A lost device, an out-of-memory or internal error, or a full-size allocation that ran out of memory (below): a fresh engine, the photo uploaded again and developed with its current settings (Part 14's restore), the photo drawn again. A validation error: the same work would fail again, so no restart; a notice, at most once in 30 s. Reports about an engine that is already being replaced are ignored, so one failure gives one restart. |
| Limit | At most **3 restarts in 2 minutes**, the 2nd and 3rd after 1 s and 3 s (a driver that was just reset may need a moment); making the new engine failing counts too. A 4th failure in the window stops the preview: no engine is made until the person clicks **Try again**, which starts the count over. |
| Diagnostics | Each failure is logged (`gpu-trouble`: kind, the browser's text, what was done, restarts in the window), as are the new engine (`engine-recovery`) and Try again. |
| Messages | Restart (shown for 8 s at the foot of the photo, as Part 14's notice was): "The graphics card stopped responding. LightBench restarted the preview. Your edit is safe." — "The graphics card ran out of memory. LightBench restarted the preview. Your edit is safe." — "The graphics card reported a problem. LightBench restarted the preview. Your edit is safe." — "The graphics card isn't ready yet. LightBench is trying again. Your edit is safe." Validation: "The graphics card couldn't draw part of this photo. If something looks wrong, open the photo again." Stopped (in the middle of the photo, with **Try again**): "The graphics card keeps stopping, so LightBench stopped the preview. Your edit is safe. Close other tabs and apps that use graphics, then click Try again. If it keeps happening, reload the page or restart the browser." — after out of memory: "The graphics card keeps running out of memory, so LightBench stopped the preview. Your edit is safe. Choose Fit in the zoom menu, close other tabs and apps that use graphics, then click Try again. If it keeps happening, reload the page." Part 14's "The graphics card was reset, so Develop restarted it." is replaced by the first. ("Your edit is safe": the settings are saved before the thumbnail, so a failed thumbnail loses nothing.) |
| Out of memory on large allocations | `engine/gpu/alloc-check.ts` `AllocCheck`: the full-size develop (the scene texture and the RAW stage's temporary buffers, about 30 bytes a pixel with RCD; a JPEG's scene) and an export's images (the developed image, the encoded buffer and its read-back copy, the resize and sharpening textures) are each created inside an out-of-memory error scope of their own. Push, create and pop are one synchronous step, and WebGPU files an error in the scope on top of the stack when the call is made, so nothing else on the device can land in it: safe on Develop's shared device. If one failed, `process` and `renderOutput` throw `GpuWorkError('out-of-memory')` before the result is used (no black image); inside `guarded` that error passes through unchanged, so Export's retry rule is the same. Only out-of-memory scopes: validation errors go where they went before. |
| The AI runtime's graphics card | Develop asks for the high-performance adapter. The AI runtime's own checks did not: its fp16 check and adapter name (`ai/runtime.ts` `probeGpu`, and the worker's `probe`) asked for the browser's default adapter, which on a laptop with integrated and dedicated graphics can be the integrated one (the one that froze the owner's laptop). Checked in Chromium by wrapping `requestAdapter` around a session of ONNX Runtime Web 1.30.0: its JavaScript side checks WebGPU with `requestAdapter({})`, and its native WebGPU provider makes the device it runs on with `requestAdapter({ powerPreference: 'high-performance', forceFallbackAdapter: false })`. So the model already ran on the high-performance adapter; now the fp16 check, the adapter name and ONNX Runtime's check ask for it too (`ai/adapter.ts`, `env.webgpu.powerPreference`). On a computer with one GPU nothing changes; the same stand-in model gave the same output with and without the setting (SwiftShader). The `ai-depth` Diagnostics entry now names the card the model ran on (`gpu`: vendor, architecture, device, description of the runtime's device). |
| A gap found on the way | An engine whose device was lost while it was being made, or while the photo was being restored onto it, was installed lost: its `onLost` was attached after the loss, so Develop stayed on "Developing…". It is now checked once it is installed, and recovered. |
| Unchanged | No rendering code: the scopes only decide where errors go. Develop's other read-backs (white balance picker, loupe, Auto) still aren't guarded with scopes; their failures reach Develop through `onGpuError`. The Detail stage's and the warps' allocations aren't wrapped (their out-of-memory errors arrive the same way, uncaught). |

### 2. Speed check on each release

PLAN.md promised "agreed slider-to-preview time budgets, checked by an automated speed test before each release", with budgets confirmed on the owner's GPU first. GitHub's runners have no GPU, so what can be checked there is limited; the check is built to warn, never to fail.

| What | Detail |
|---|---|
| When | `.github/workflows/speed.yml` ("Speed check"): every push to main, every pull request, and by hand. Separate from "Build and deploy site", so it never holds up a deploy. |
| What runs | `web/scripts/speed-gate.mjs run`: starts the Vite development server, opens headless Chromium (Playwright 1.56.1, installed outside `web/` so the app's package files are untouched) on the software GPU (SwiftShader, `?gpu=readback`), and runs System Check's GPU benchmark (Part 16) in **Quick** mode on its synthetic test images scaled to **0.3 MP** (at 24 MP the software GPU needs minutes per stage), 1 warm-up and 3 measured runs per stage, **without** the full-size export rows of the masks and effects and the end-to-end export (`SKIPPED_STAGES`): with them a run took 683 s here, about 60 % of it in those rows. The slider-to-preview rows (fit, 100 %), Stage A, Detail, the plain export render and the thumbnail stay. |
| Compared with | `web/scripts/speed-baseline.json`, committed. **Time** as each stage's *share* of the whole run (its median ÷ the sum of the medians of the stages in both), which cancels out a machine that is uniformly faster or slower: a stage warns when its share **doubled** (grew by more than 100 %) and that is at least 40 ms at this machine's speed (two runs of the same code here differed by up to 84 % in one stage's share). The **whole run** warns past 2× the baseline's. **Memory**: each stage's peak engine allocations (the benchmark's own estimate, deterministic for the same code and image size) warn past +10 %. Failed stages warn; the benchmark's own skips (no AI model on the machine) don't. |
| Reported | The job summary: the warnings, a table of every stage (baseline, this run, change of share, change of memory), what it measures and what it can't catch; each warning is also a GitHub annotation. The result is kept as the `speed-result` artifact for 30 days. A run that fails to complete gives a warning and the summary says so; the workflow stays green. |
| New baseline | After a change meant to change speed: download the artifact, `node scripts/speed-gate.mjs baseline speed-result.json` in `web/`, commit. |
| What it can't catch | Speed on a real graphics card (memory bandwidth, drivers, integrated graphics); a stage that gets slower without doubling its share, or by under 40 ms; a slowdown that hits every stage alike unless the run takes twice as long; work at full size that 0.3 MP images don't reach; the masks' and effects' export rows (left out); the interface's own responsiveness; the driver's real memory use. Time budgets aren't set: they need the owner's GPU. |
| Why warnings only | Timings on shared CI machines and a software GPU are noisy (measured below); a hard gate would fail builds for nothing. |

### 3. Release tags and one-click rollback

| What | Detail |
|---|---|
| Tags | `deploy.yml`, new job `tag` after `deploy`, on pushes to main only: tags the deployed commit `v<web/package.json version>` if the tag doesn't exist (a tag is never moved). Its `GITHUB_TOKEN` has `contents: write` for that job only; the rest of the workflow keeps `contents: read`. |
| Rollback | `workflow_dispatch` input **Roll back the website** (a tag such as `v0.6.12`): checked (format, run from main), that tag checked out, `npm ci`, built with the site's base path, its own pinned AI models fetched by the same step as a normal deploy (skipped for releases without the models code), deployed. Lint and tests aren't run again. The summary names the version and commit and warns when main's library format (catalog schema) is newer than the tag's. Owner's steps: [ROLLBACK.md](ROLLBACK.md). |
| Update button (owner, 2026-10-21) | The "Update ready" notice was easy to miss: it popped up once at the bottom right and **Later** hid it until the next load. Now a green **Update ready** button (or **Earlier version ready**, or **Reload needed** when another window took the update) is pinned in the top bar, left of Settings, for as long as a new version waits. Clicking it opens the notes under the top bar with **Reload** and **Later**; Later or Escape closes the notes and keeps the button. Nothing opens by itself and nothing changes until Reload is clicked. (`pwa/UpdateNotice.tsx` `UpdateButton`, `UpdateNotice`; `pwa/register.ts` `updateLabel`.) |
| Installed apps | A rolled-back build's `sw.js` differs from the newer one, so browsers install it as an update with its own cache (`lightbench-<hash of its files and build info>`; files are copied from the newer cache when their hash matches, else downloaded and checked). The waiting update is offered as before. New: the running build compares versions (`pwa/notes.ts` `compareVersions`, `updateInfo`), and for an earlier one shows **Earlier version ready** — "The LightBench website went back to version 0.6.12. Reload to use it." — instead of "Update ready" with "No release notes for this update." Nothing else depended on the version going up: the release notes are only shown in that notice, and the browser storage the app versions (IndexedDB 1 for diagnostics and folder handles) is unchanged. An earlier version refuses a library a newer one upgraded ("This library was saved by a newer version…", changing nothing) — as before; the rollback run warns about it. |

### 4. Docs

[PLAN.md](PLAN.md) and [USABILITY.md](USABILITY.md) now match what is built (Parts up to 25, Diagnostics, Import speed, Zoom levels, the AI Depth mask) and record the owner's decisions of 2026-10-21; [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) is new, from Cowork's roadmap with the owner's answers; [research/PHASE4_MODELS.md](research/PHASE4_MODELS.md) and [research/AI_MODELS.md](research/AI_MODELS.md) record the relaxed model rule, the AI masks decisions and AI Remove's in-app Hugging Face download (replacing the GitHub Release text), with Cowork's report as [research/cowork-2026-10-21-ai-masks/AI_MASKS.md](research/cowork-2026-10-21-ai-masks/AI_MASKS.md). This file keeps all of Phase 3, with a table of contents at the top.

### Verified

Headless Chromium 141 with SwiftShader WebGPU (`?gpu=readback`), the Vite development server without HMR; scripts in the scratchpad's `hd/` folder.

- **Old edits unchanged** (`identity.mjs`, one case per browser): the JPEG defaults, a Colour Mixer edit (bands, guided filter) and a masks edit (radial + brush + luminance with the old local sliders): full-size and 1024 px export SHA-256, 512 px thumbnail, histogram, and the Develop canvas at fit and at 100 % — **identical** on main `cfab53a` (an archive of its `web/`) and on this branch, and identical to the hashes recorded earlier in `fx/id-main.json` (main matched them too, so nothing had drifted). The JPEG defaults and masks cases were run again on the final code: identical. The RAW cases weren't run (minutes per case on SwiftShader); the engine changes are error scopes around allocations, which don't touch pixels.
- **GPU guard in Develop** (`e2e-gpu-guard.mjs`, the 1600 × 1072 JPEG): **25 of 25**. Develop opened and drew the photo (smoke check); after `device.destroy()` the first notice showed, the photo was drawn again on a new engine with the same mean (131.0), one canvas, no error; a synthetic uncaught `GPUOutOfMemoryError` sent to Develop's device gave the out-of-memory notice and a new engine (the old device freed); a synthetic `GPUValidationError` gave the validation notice and no new engine; two more losses then stopped the preview with the "keeps stopping" message and **Try again** (the third restart in the window, then the stop), and no engine was made in the next 10 s; Try again drew the photo again; an Exposure change then showed (mean 131.0 → 159.8). No WebGPU errors other than the test's own appeared in the console during normal drawing. The first run of the script failed the limit part because the test destroyed the same device repeatedly before its loss was reported (a test bug, fixed).
- **ONNX Runtime's adapter** (`ort-adapter.mjs`, a 1 KB stand-in model in the page with `requestAdapter` wrapped): the JavaScript check asked for `{}`, the native provider for `{ powerPreference: 'high-performance', forceFallbackAdapter: false }`; with `env.webgpu.powerPreference` set, the check asked for high performance too and the output's SHA-256 was the same (`bb25e08d…`). The real Depth Anything model wasn't run here.
- **Speed check** (`speed-gate.mjs run` here, Playwright 1.56.1 from `/opt/node-tools`): the full Quick plan at 0.3 MP took 683 s; without the left-out rows 259 s and 328 s. The second of those is the committed baseline's run; compared with it, the third gave 5 warnings at a 50 % share tolerance (Colour Grading, Masks, Texture + Clarity, Dehaze and Glow at fit, +66 to +84 %, while other stages went −13 to −32 %, and the whole run 1.29×) and none at 100 %, which is why the tolerance is 100 %. Peak memory was identical between those two runs for every stage; it differs when the set of stages changes (stages leave textures cached for the next), so a baseline must come from the same plan.
- **Workflows**: `actionlint` 1.7.7 (built from source earlier in the scratchpad; its shellcheck rule was off because `shellcheck` isn't installed): no errors in `deploy.yml`, `speed.yml` and `ai-models.yml`; every `run:` script parsed with `bash -n`. **Not run on GitHub**: the tag job, a rollback, the speed check on a runner (its time, Chrome's WebGPU there, the baseline's fit to that machine) are unverified until they run.
- **Unit tests** (40 new): `develop/gpuRecovery.test.ts` (12: restarts per kind with plain messages, one restart per failure, the limit and staying stopped, the memory message, the window forgetting old restarts, a failed new engine, Try again, validation notices at most every 30 s, no technical words), `engine/gpu/alloc-check.test.ts` (8: success, the first out-of-memory error, only its own call caught on a shared device, validation left to enclosing scopes, a throwing allocation, a lost device; error kinds), `engine/gpu/guard.test.ts` (+1: an out-of-memory error from inside `guarded` passes through and is retried), `pwa/pwa.test.ts` (+5: version comparison, a newer, an earlier and the same version, no answer), `scripts/speed-gate.test.mjs` (11: rows compared, uniform slowdown, one stage slower, short stages, memory, the whole run, failures and skips, new and missing stages, the summary, the baseline's size and contents), `ai/adapter.test.ts` (3).
- From a clean `npm ci`: `npx tsc -b`, `npx oxlint` (the two existing warnings), `npx vitest run` in four parts (134 files, 1,319 tests) and `npm run build` passed.

**Not verified:** a real driver reset, a real out-of-memory error and the integrated-GPU freeze on hardware (only `device.destroy()` and synthetic error events on SwiftShader); the AI runtime on a computer with two GPUs; anything on GitHub's servers (above).

## Part 26: AI Remove

**What it does.** A fourth mode for the Remove brush, **AI Remove**: paint over an object and an AI model on the user's own computer, **Moebius** (Huazhong University of Science and Technology and vivo AI Lab, ECCV 2026; Apache-2.0), draws what could be behind it. It is the optional best-quality Remove (PHASE4_MODELS.md §4); Remove, Heal and Clone stay as they were, without AI. The model is about 1.24 GB, too big for LightBench's GitHub Pages site, so the app downloads it **itself, straight from Hugging Face**, once, after the user agrees, resumably and checked by SHA-256. Each spot's generated patch is **stored inside the edit and the .xmp** (as Part 24's depth map), with the model version and seed, so the spot looks the same everywhere and later, without the model and without running it again. Edits without AI Remove spots render byte-identically (below).

The brief is the owner's decisions of 2026-10-21 (after [research/cowork-2026-10-21-hf-moebius/HF_MOEBIUS.md](research/cowork-2026-10-21-hf-moebius/HF_MOEBIUS.md), committed with this build) and of 2026-10-17 ([research/PHASE4_MODELS.md](research/PHASE4_MODELS.md) "Owner's decisions so far", [research/cowork-2026-10-17-ai-remove/AI_REMOVE_MODELS.md](research/cowork-2026-10-17-ai-remove/AI_REMOVE_MODELS.md)); the delivery and runtime are Part 24's. PHASE4_MODELS.md's "GitHub Release + user picks the files" delivery text is replaced by the owner's 2026-10-21 decisions; the hardening build records them there.

### Owner's decisions and how they are built

| # | Decision | Built as |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `simonw/Moebius-ONNX` pinned at `5bf1ef5d2861ec01a727183a3f95dc64f352120e` (the Conv2d `unet.onnx`; never `9f6cfb3` or earlier), all fp32 | `web/models/manifest.json` entry `moebius`: `vae_encoder.onnx` 136,757,093 bytes `b8b81d41…`, `unet.onnx` 906,698,976 `e3f90f52…`, `vae_decoder.onnx` 198,078,671 `d90ef0b7…` (1,241,534,740 in all). `models.mjs pin` leaves it alone. A unit test checks the pin, the sizes, the hashes and that the commit isn't `9f6cfb3`/`82c258b`. |
| 2 | Checkpoint `ft_places2`, source "export script, not model card"; licence Apache-2.0 per hustvl/Moebius GitHub (b88d462), with the MIT tag on HF in plain words; VAE from PixelHacker (accepted); unnamed teacher data accepted as an exception, said in plain words | The manifest's `checkpoint`, `licence.statement`, `licence.notes` and `trainingData`; shown in the download dialog, About this model and Settings → AI models; THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md; `web/models/notices/moebius.LICENSE.txt` (the LICENSE at `b88d462`). |
| 3 | In-app download from Hugging Face (`"delivery": "huggingface"`); the deploy skips it (Pages' 1 GB) | `delivery` in the manifest (`site` for DA-V2, `huggingface` for Moebius). `models.mjs fetch`/`verify`/`pin` skip it ("skipped moebius: the app downloads it from Hugging Face itself"); the deploy still fetches and checks DA-V2 Small. |
| 4 | Consent dialog: "about 1.24 GB", plus the runtime if not installed, licence and training-data notes | `ModelDialog`: "Download Moebius?", straight from Hugging Face at the exact version, checked by SHA-256, "about 1.24 GB" (+ ONNX Runtime Web 1.30.0, 26.8 MB, from LightBench's own site, when it isn't installed), the licence with the MIT-tag note, the training data in plain words, About this model. Nothing is fetched before Download. |
| 5 | Size and SHA-256 checked before keeping; an interrupted install records nothing; cache keyed by commit + file name; resumable with Range; progress and Cancel; 429 → wait `t=`; no token; `persist()` and `estimate()` first | `store.ts installHf` (below). |
| 6 | Settings → AI models lists Moebius like the depth model (status, About, Remove) | `AiModelsSettings`: "Moebius (AI Remove)"; Not downloaded / Partly downloaded (x of y): Download continues where it stopped / On this device (3 files, size); Download… / Continue download…, Delete partial download, Remove from this device, About this model, Check for updates. |
| 7 | Manual "Check for updates" only; Hub API `?blobs=true`; per-file LFS sha256 (conversion and upstream `ft_places2`), never the repo `sha`; reports only | `updates.ts checkForUpdates`, on the button only (below). |
| 8 | AI option in Remove; the agreed UX | PHASE4_MODELS.md §4: "An 'AI' mode is one more entry" of `SPOT_MODES`. Built as that: a fourth mode button, **AI Remove**, beside Remove, Heal and Clone. |
| 9 | The model card's pipeline; strength 1.0 vs 0.99 tested and chosen by results | `ai/inpaint.ts` (below); strength: "Strength". |
| 10 | Old edits never change; each AI spot stores its patch (8-bit or better, compressed) with model version and seed; re-render from it; never re-run on load | `engine/develop/ai-patch.ts`, `spots.ts`, `retouch/run.ts` (below). Identity: "Checks". |
| 11 | Shared worker runtime and GPU gate; graphs run sequentially, sessions freed between phases; GPU limits checked; a watchdog; CPU fallback only if it works and says it is slow; diagnostics | `ai/aiRemove.ts`, `runtime.ts`, `models.ts`, `useAiRemove.ts` (below). |
| 12 | Plain user words; no comparisons or implementation notes in the UI; tooltips on every new control | Every new button has a `title`; the Size slider of an AI spot has its own help entry (`removeAiSize`). |

### Using it

| Behaviour | Detail |
|---|---|
| Painting | Remove → **AI Remove** → paint. The first time on a device the download dialog opens (decision 4). Then the panel shows "AI Remove: loading the model…", "looking at the photo…", "step n of 20…", "finishing…" with a progress bar and **Cancel**; the spot's pin shows the same progress. When it finishes the spot is added as one history step, "AI Remove spot n", and selected. Declined, cancelled or failed: nothing is added. |
| Selected AI spot | **Try again** draws a new fill with a new seed (one history step "Spot n: AI Remove, try again"); Feather and Opacity change the blend as for other spots; **Size is fixed** (its tooltip says why: the fill was drawn for that shape); no dashed source (there is none). Mode buttons: switching a Remove/Heal/Clone spot to AI Remove generates its fill ("Spot n: AI Remove"); switching an AI spot back drops its patch and picks a source as for a new spot. |
| Paste, sync, presets | Spots pasted to another photo arrive **without** their AI patch (it was made from the photo it belongs to): such a spot changes nothing until **Generate** (the panel says "Spot n has no AI fill on this photo yet: Generate draws one"). |
| Commands | "Spot mode: AI Remove" in the command search (no default key). |
| Messages | No WebGPU: "AI Remove needs WebGPU, which this browser or computer doesn't offer … Remove, Heal and Clone work everywhere." A GPU whose buffers are too small: "This GPU can't hold Moebius: it allows GPU buffers of up to N MiB, and the model needs M MiB …". A step over 3 minutes or a load over 5: stopped, "AI Remove didn't finish within its time limit … Nothing was changed and Develop is not affected". Out of GPU memory, invalid (non-finite) numbers, a lost GPU: said plainly, nothing changed. Not in this build: "AI Remove isn't available in this build of LightBench". |

### Delivery from Hugging Face (`store.ts installHf`)

Facts it relies on (the "AI models" workflow's hf-check, run 39644358791, from GitHub's servers with `Origin: https://shawneburke88-afk.github.io`; Node, not a browser): `/resolve/<commit>/<file>` answers 302 to `us.aws.cdn.hf.co/xet-bridge-us/…` (signed, expiring) with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` echoing the origin; the CDN answers GET 200 with `ACAO: *` and `Accept-Ranges: bytes`, `Range: bytes=0-99` with 206 and exactly 100 bytes; preflights for `range` succeed on both; the Hub API allows the origin anonymously.

| Step | Detail |
|---|---|
| Before | Only after Download. `navigator.storage.estimate()`: if quota − usage is below what is still to download (+ 3 %, at least 64 MB), it stops before downloading anything and says so plainly ("There isn't enough storage space: the download needs about …"). `navigator.storage.persist()` is asked before and after. |
| Files | Each from `https://huggingface.co/simonw/Moebius-ONNX/resolve/5bf1ef5…/<file>` (`credentials: 'omit'`, no token, `cache: 'no-store'`); the browser follows the redirect. The runtime (if missing) comes from LightBench's own site as in Part 24. |
| Kept | In Cache Storage `ai-models`, in parts of 32 MiB keyed `models/moebius/<40-digit commit>/<file>/part-NNNN` — the pinned commit and file name, never the signed CDN address. |
| Resume | On the next try (after a network error, a Cancel or a closed tab) the parts already kept are read back in order (any part of the wrong size and everything after a gap is dropped) and the rest is asked for with `Range: bytes=<start>-`. A 206 must start at that byte (`Content-Range`, when readable); a 200 means the server ignored the range: that file starts again. A connection that drops or ends early is retried after 2, 4, 8 and 16 s, then stops with "What was downloaded is kept: Download continues where it stopped". |
| Check | Each file's SHA-256 is worked out part by part as the parts are kept (`lib/sha256.ts`; the browser's digest needs the whole file at once) and compared with the manifest's, with its byte count, before anything is recorded. A mismatch deletes that file's parts and reports it; `installed.json` is written only when all three matched. |
| 429 | Waited out: the `RateLimit` header's `t=` seconds when the browser may read it, else `Retry-After`, else 60 s (at most 300), up to six times; the progress line says "Hugging Face asked to wait: continuing in n s". **Note:** Hugging Face's `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` (hf-check) lists neither `RateLimit` nor `Retry-After`, so a browser most likely waits the 60 s default; and whether a 429 carries CORS headers at all is **unverified** (if not, the browser sees a network error, retried as above). HF's anonymous limit is 3,000 file requests per 5 minutes per address; an install makes 3 or 4. |
| Progress, Cancel | Bytes of all three files (and the runtime), the file name, waiting messages; Cancel aborts at once and keeps the parts; the dialog then offers "Continue download about … (Already downloaded: …)". |
| Loading | `loadPart` puts a file back together from its parts, checks its size and SHA-256 again (the browser's digest) and hands the bytes to the runtime worker (moved, not copied: `rpc.ts transfer`). A damaged part is reported, never run. |
| Removal | Remove from this device (and Delete partial download) deletes every key under `models/moebius/`; the runtime goes with the last model, as before. |
| Service worker | Requests to `huggingface.co` and `*.hf.co` are left to the browser (`sw.ts`): the app keeps them itself and they need no isolation headers. Everything else is unchanged. |

### Check for updates (`updates.ts`)

Only when **Check for updates** is clicked. Two anonymous requests: `https://huggingface.co/api/models/simonw/Moebius-ONNX?blobs=true` and `…/hustvl/Moebius?blobs=true`.
