It compares each pinned file's LFS sha256 with the conversion's current files, and `ft_places2/diffusion_pytorch_model.bin`'s with the baseline `6525afb8…` (905,298,356 bytes; hustvl/Moebius `cd01f47`). Never the repository `sha` (README-only commits change it). It reports only: "No newer files: Moebius is the latest published version.", "A newer conversion of Moebius is published (not installed: LightBench keeps the files it was tested with).", "The original Moebius weights have changed since this conversion was made (…)", files no longer listed, or "Couldn't check for updates: …".

### The pipeline (`ai/inpaint.ts`)

Pure code: the app runs it in the page with the runtime worker, and the "AI models" workflow runs the same file in Node on the real model. Read from the authors' code (hustvl/Moebius `removal/v1_2/pipeline.py`, `infer/utils.py`, `utils_infer.py` at `b88d462`) and the conversion's own pipeline (simonw/moebius-web `python/onnx_pipeline.py`, `web/src/pipeline.ts` at `080be6e`).

| Step | Detail |
|---|---|
| Input | The photo **as the spot sees it**: the full-resolution scene (linear Rec.2020, sensor orientation, before geometry) with the spots before this one applied (`engine.readSceneRegion`). |
| Crop | A square of 2.5 × the spot's longer side, at least 512 px, centred on the spot and kept inside the photo (smaller photos: as much as there is). |
| Picture | Rec.2020 → linear sRGB × a per-spot exposure `gain` (the crop's 99th percentile of max(R, G, B) to 0.85, from a log-scale histogram; within 1/64…64), sRGB curve, resized to fit 512 × 512 (area average when shrinking, bilinear when enlarging); a non-square crop is **letterboxed** with mirrored margins (marked known). Values −1…1. |
| Hole | Every pixel the brush reaches (any coverage), so feathered edges are regenerated too; on the model grid, any covered model pixel. Masked picture = picture × (1 − hole) (the hole is 0). |
| Latents | The encoder's `moments`, first 4 channels (the mean; the authors sample, which differs by the VAE's tiny variance) × 0.13025. The hole on the 64 × 64 grid: each 8 × 8 block's top-left pixel (PyTorch's nearest). |
| Sampler | DDIM, 1000 training steps, scaled-linear β 0.00085 → 0.012, "leading" spacing, η 0, no clipping, final ᾱ 1: 20 steps. **Strength 1**: pure noise at t = 950 (20 steps). Strength 0.99 (the authors' default): 19 steps from t = 900 and the photo's own latent noised to t = 900 (`add_noise`). Noise: seeded (mulberry32 + Box–Muller, the port's) + the authors' offset 0.0357 per channel. |
| Guidance | Each step one UNet run on a batch of two: `latent` [2, 9, 64, 64] = (noisy latent 4, hole 1, masked latent 4), `timesteps` [t, t], `input_ids` row 0 = 10…19 (unconditional), row 1 = 0…9 (conditional); noise = uncond + **2.5** × (cond − uncond). 2.5 is the authors' inference script's default (`--cfg`); their README's example uses 2.0 and the pipeline class 4.5 (the three defaults PHASE4_MODELS.md noted). |
| Decode | latent / 0.13025 → `image` [1, 3, 512, 512]; non-finite values stop it ("invalid numbers", nothing changed). |
| Patch | The decoded picture over the spot's bounds plus a ring of 4 px, cut at the model's resolution (or the photo's, when the crop was enlarged), 8-bit sRGB; pixels farther than the ring from the stroke are stored as 0. |

### The stored patch and drawing it (`ai-patch.ts`, `retouch/run.ts`)

| Piece | Detail |
|---|---|
| Stored | In the spot (`ai`): `model`, `modelVersion` (`5bf1ef5/fp32`), `runtime` ("onnxruntime-web 1.30.0 webgpu"), `seed`, `steps`, `strength`, `guidance`, `rect` (the patch's rectangle normalised to the sensor, 9 decimals), `gain`, `width`, `height`, `encoding` "rgb8-med-deflate-b64", `data`. So the catalog's edit, the `lightbench:Develop` sidecar field, snapshots and virtual copies carry it; the saved history keeps each patch once ("@r<n>", as Part 24's maps). |
| Encoding | The R, G and B planes, each byte's difference from the median-edge predictor (Part 24's), raw DEFLATE, base64. Caps: sides ≤ 1024, ≤ 4 MB of text; a damaged patch is dropped on load (the spot stays and waits for Generate) or, if it passes the checks but doesn't decode, the spot changes nothing. |
| Drawing | Where every spot is applied (the scene, before geometry; Part 11), in order: the patch, decoded once and cached, sampled bilinearly at each photo pixel (back to linear Rec.2020 ÷ gain), **healed** into the photo as it is now — the log-ratio of photo to patch on the 4 px ring made harmonic over the spot (Remove's own membrane), so a later white balance or exposure change, or a colour shift from the model, leaves no seam — and blended by the brush's feather × opacity. Nothing outside the brush's footprint changes. The model is never needed or run to draw. |
| Old edits | A spot without `mode: 'ai'` is stored and keyed exactly as before (`normalizeSpot` adds nothing; `spotsKey` unchanged), so edits without AI Remove spots don't change. |

### Running the model (`ai/aiRemove.ts`, `runtime.ts`)

| Piece | Detail |
|---|---|
| Runtime | Part 24's: ONNX Runtime Web 1.30.0 in the runtime worker, its WebGPU provider on its own GPU device. New: named int64/float32 inputs (`runFeeds`), several graphs per model (`loadGraph`, `runGraph`, `releaseGraph`). |
| Phases | Encoder loaded (read and checked from the cache) and run; the 907 MB denoiser loaded, then the encoder **freed**; the denoiser run 20 times and **kept** until the worker's 2-minute idle end (a second spot needn't load it again); decoder loaded, run, freed. At most the denoiser and one image graph are on the GPU at once. |
| Gate | Each run is foreground GPU work (Part 14's gate), so Export and the background developer wait between runs, not for the whole spot. |
| Watchdogs | Per load 5 minutes, per run 3 minutes on WebGPU (15 on the CPU): a slow GPU that needs minutes in all is never stopped, a hung step is found within minutes; then the worker (and its GPU device) is ended and the message says so. |
| GPU limits | The adapter's `maxBufferSize` and `maxStorageBufferBindingSize` (asked on the main thread with the probe) against the manifest's `gpu` needs; below them, the plain message above and nothing runs. |
| Phase order | The denoiser is loaded **before** the encoder is freed: ONNX Runtime ends its GPU device when its last session is released (the e2e found the next load then failing as "GPU lost"). A device destroyed on purpose isn't reported as lost; freeing never waits more than 10 s. |
| CPU fallback | On, slow (see "Speed"): the dialog says "without WebGPU here it runs on the processor: several minutes per spot" and the panel "(on the processor, without WebGPU: this takes several minutes)". |
| Trace | `globalThis.__lbAiDebug = true` prints each phase to the console (tests and support; off by default). |
| Diagnostics | One `ai-remove` entry per run: model id and version, adapter (80 characters), provider, crop and patch sizes, the patch's characters, load / encode / mean step / decode / total ms; or the error, or "cancelled". No file names, no pixels. |

### The real model on GitHub's servers ("AI models" → `moebius-check`)

huggingface.co is blocked from this build machine, so the real files were checked only by the workflow (task **`moebius-check`**, read-only; `web/scripts/moebius-check.mjs` and `moebius-parity.py`). It downloads the three pinned files (size and SHA-256 checked), runs **the app's own code** — the crop, model input, sampler and patch of `ai/inpaint.ts`, the stored encoding of `ai-patch.ts` and the drawing of `retouch/run.ts`, bundled for Node by Vite — with ONNX Runtime 1.30.0 on the runner's CPU (onnxruntime-node, installed in the run only), on a committed public-domain test photo (`web/scripts/fixtures/ai-remove/philadelphia-768.png`, 768 × 512, a crop of `immich-app/test-assets`' `philadelphia.nef` preview) with four removals (`cases.json`: a litter bin; a bollard and its shadow; another bollard with a wide brush; a bollard with a large brush, which makes the crop wider than 512 px, so it is scaled and letterboxed), each at strength 1 and 0.99, seed 1234. It writes the pictures (input with the spot outlined, the model's raw 512 × 512 output, the result, a side-by-side sheet per case) to the run's **moebius-check** artifact, times the app's own WebAssembly runtime (the CPU fallback), and lists each graph's operators and largest tensor. Two runs (the owner asked for one or two):

| Run | Result |
|---|---|
| [39650099385](https://github.com/shawneburke88-afk/Photo-Editing-App/actions/runs/39650099385) (26 min) | Files: 3 of 3 size and SHA-256 ok. Graphs as expected: `vae_encoder` image [B, 3, 512, 512] → moments [B, 8, 64, 64]; `unet` latent [B, 9, 64, 64] + timesteps int64 [B] + input_ids int64 [B, 10] → noise [B, 4, 64, 64]; `vae_decoder` latent [B, 4, 64, 64] → image [B, 3, 512, 512]. **8 of 8 removals: all values finite, the spot changed (mean 39–63 levels of 255), nothing outside the brush changed (0 pixels)**; CPU 7.1 s per guided step (batch of two), encoder 6.3 s, decoder 14.2 s: **163 s per spot**. WebAssembly, 4 threads: encoder 22.5 s, one step 20.2 s, decoder 49.8 s (finite). Inspection: opset 18; the UNet uses Conv (229), Einsum (105), InstanceNormalization, LayerNormalization, Softmax, Gemm, MatMul, BatchNormalization, Resize and shape operators; largest weight 56.2 MiB; largest tensor found 128.0 MiB (the encoder's first convolution, [1, 128, 512, 512]). The parity step failed before running (see below). |
| [39654799449](https://github.com/shawneburke88-afk/Photo-Editing-App/actions/runs/39654799449) (14 min, a faster runner) | The same with the log-scale exposure: **8 of 8 passed**; CPU **3.4 s per step, 76–80 s per spot**; WebAssembly 4 threads: encoder 13.9 s, step 12.0 s, decoder 31.0 s. The pictures were read (below). Parity failed again before running (below). |

**Strength: 1.0 (chosen).** Per case, strength 1 against the authors' 0.99 (run 39654799449; "resemblance" is the correlation of the spot's luminance with the removed object's, 1 meaning the object is still there; "ring" is the colour difference between the spot and a 16 px ring around it):

| case | resemblance 1.0 / 0.99 | ring 1.0 / 0.99 (8-bit) | spot changed 1.0 / 0.99 (mean, 8-bit) |
|---|---|---|---|
| bin | 0.543 / 0.540 | 13.7 / 13.1 | 52.0 / 52.3 |
| bollard | 0.244 / 0.240 | 14.9 / 15.7 | 62.9 / 63.2 |
| bollard, wide brush | 0.370 / 0.383 | 26.8 / 26.7 | 47.0 / 46.9 |
| large brush (letterboxed) | 0.507 / 0.511 | 22.9 / 22.3 | 39.4 / 39.2 |

Looking at the sheets, both strengths removed all four objects cleanly (the bin is replaced by the fountain's rim, paving and lawn; the bollards and their shadows by paving and the kerb); the two results differ only in fine texture. No object came back at 0.99 in these cases. **1.0 is kept** because the results are equal here and 1.0 cannot let the object back by construction: it starts from pure noise, and the object's pixels reach the model only as the hole (0), whereas 0.99 mixes the photo's own latent, with the object in it, into the start (`add_noise` at t = 900), the leak the secondary source reports. It also needs one encoder run fewer (2.8 s against 5.3 s of encoding on the CPU) for one more denoising step, so the time is the same (76–80 s per spot both ways on the CPU). The faint vertical lines at the bollard's edges in the "bollard" case are the brush's feather (the test's 25 %: the outer quarter of the radius blends the original back in, as for every Remove mode), not the model; the wide and large brushes show none.

**Parity with the authors' PyTorch model: matches.** The step (`moebius-parity.py parity`: hustvl/Moebius at `b88d462`, `ft_places2` weights from `hustvl/Moebius` at `cd01f47`, checked against SHA-256 `6525afb8…`; the authors' model against `unet.onnx` on the same seeded input at t = 950, 500 and 50) failed twice on the authors' imports (fixed: a stand-in for the unused GLA teacher; `removal_model.py` loaded alone with `opencv-python-headless`) and passed on the third run, [39670089066](https://github.com/shawneburke88-afk/Photo-Editing-App/actions/runs/39670089066): max |Δ| 3.10e-6 / 5.07e-6 / 7.57e-6 and mean |Δ| 3.43e-7 / 6.07e-7 / 8.58e-7 at t = 950 / 500 / 50 (largest output values 4.40 / 4.06 / 5.02), all keys loaded. So the current Conv2d `unet.onnx` matches the original numerically. The runner installed torch 2.14.1 (not the pinned 2.7.1) and ONNX Runtime 1.31.0 on the CPU; the app's removal step in the same run also passed.

### Speed

| Where | Per spot | Source |
|---|---|---|
| GitHub runner CPU, onnxruntime-node | 76–163 s (3.4–7.1 s per guided step) | the two runs above |
| GitHub runner, the app's WebAssembly (4 threads) | about 5–8 minutes (encoder 14–23 s + 20 × 12–20 s + decoder 31–50 s) | the two runs, one run of each graph |
| A real GPU with WebGPU | **unmeasured** | No GPU here; SwiftShader's software WebGPU (the e2e) says nothing about a GPU's speed. The port's author estimated "several hundred ms to ~1.5 s/step" (research.md, an estimate). The authors report 26 ms per step on a server GPU (unnamed). |

The CPU fallback is therefore **on** (`INPAINT_WASM_FALLBACK`): it works (finite results with the app's own WebAssembly runtime) and the dialog and the panel say it runs on the processor and takes several minutes per spot. Its memory use in a browser tab (the 907 MB denoiser inside WebAssembly's 4 GB) is unmeasured.

### GPU feasibility (unverified)

- Every graph's largest tensor found is 128 MiB (the encoder's first convolution output; ONNX shape inference could not resolve every intermediate shape: 260, 3990 and 332 unresolved in the three graphs, so this is a lower bound). WebGPU guarantees 128 MiB per storage binding and 256 MiB per buffer, and ONNX Runtime asks for the adapter's own limits; the manifest's `gpu` need is set to 128 MiB, so every WebGPU adapter passes the check, which stays for a future model.
- **Raised to 1 GiB (Part 27, 2026-10-22):** ONNX Runtime 1.30.0's WebGPU kernels compute a tensor larger than the adapter's binding limit silently wrong (MASKS_PREP_2.md), and Moebius has tensors of at least 128 MiB, so on adapters limited to 128 MiB its fills could be corrupted. The manifest's `gpu` need is now 1 GiB for `maxStorageBufferBindingSize` and `maxBufferSize`; a GPU below it runs AI Remove on the processor (the CPU fallback, with its "several minutes" notice) instead of WebGPU.
- The real question is total GPU memory, which WebGPU doesn't report: about 0.9 GB of weights for the denoiser, plus one image graph (0.14–0.2 GB) and activations. Untested on any real GPU. A failure is reported as "not enough memory" or a lost GPU, without touching Develop's own device; a hang is stopped by the watchdogs.

### Limitations and unverified

- **A real GPU:** the real 907 MB denoiser has never run on WebGPU (only on the runner's CPU, and stand-ins on SwiftShader). Its speed, its total GPU memory need (about 1 GB of weights plus activations, not reported by WebGPU) and ONNX Runtime's WebGPU coverage of its operators (Einsum, InstanceNormalization, LayerNormalization, Conv, Resize …) are **unverified** until the owner's in-browser test. The CPU fallback's memory use in a tab is unmeasured.
- **Hugging Face from a browser:** the redirect, CORS, `Range` and preflight answers are from the workflow's Node requests with an `Origin` header (hf-check), not a browser; a browser following the 302 to the CDN with a `Range` header (the CDN then sees `Origin: null`) is **unverified**. 429 answers likely don't expose `RateLimit` to the page (60 s default wait), and whether they carry CORS at all is unverified. The owner's final in-browser test covers these.
- **Parity:** obtained on the CPU (run 39670089066, max |Δ| 7.6e-6). Parity on WebGPU, in a browser, is not measured.
- **Strength 1.0** was chosen on four removals of one photo; 0.99 gave equal results there.
- **Quality limits:** the model works at 512 × 512: a spot whose crop is larger than 512 px is filled at that resolution and enlarged (softer); the brush's feather blends a ring of the original back in (paint beyond the object). The class-id guidance 2.5 is the authors' script default, not tuned.
- **The temporary branch** `lightbench/ai-remove-check-output` (the second run's comparison pictures) could not be deleted from this session; the owner may delete it. The workflow no longer creates it.
- Sources: the HF facts are from `HF_MOEBIUS.md` and run 39644358791; the pipeline from the authors' code and the port at the commits named above.

## Part 27: AI masks, part 1: groundwork and Object select

**What it does.** The first of four AI masks builds (owner's order, 2026-10-21: click/hover object, people and faces, sky, subject). It lays the groundwork every AI mask part needs, and builds **Object select** with **SAM 2.1 Hiera Tiny** (Meta; Apache-2.0): in the Masks group's new **AI** row, **Object** turns on a picker; pointing at something in the photo previews what would be selected, a click makes it a mask ("Object 1"), Shift-click adds to it, Alt-click removes from it, and a drag selects inside a box. The model runs on the user's computer; the selection (a matte) is **stored inside the edit and the .xmp**, so the mask draws the same everywhere without the model, and old edits render byte-identically (below).

The brief is `AI_MASKS_BRIEF.md` (research, 2026-10-21) with the owner's decisions of 2026-10-21 (`DECISIONS_2026-10-21.md`, to be recorded in research/PHASE4_MODELS.md and AI_MODELS.md with the hardening docs); the model facts are from the masks-prep run (`MASKS_PREP.md`, GitHub Actions run 39653265084); delivery and runtime build on Parts 24 and 26.

### Owner's decisions and how they are built

| # | Decision | Built as |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | AI parts combine **like shapes**; ranges stay their own family | `kind: 'ai'` is in `SHAPE_KINDS` (`masks.ts`): brushes, gradients and other AI selections add to, subtract from or intersect it (darktable's max, a·(1 − c), min, as every part); Luminance, Colour and Depth ranges never join it, in the model (`canAdd`), the panel (Part to combine lists "Object (AI)" with the shapes) and on every load (`normalizeMask`). The range note now says "a brush, gradient or object selection". |
| 2 | SAM 2.1 Tiny served from LightBench's own site | `web/models/manifest.json` entry `sam2-1-hiera-tiny`, `"delivery": "site"`: the deploy's `models.mjs fetch` fetches all eight files (both variants' graphs and weights, 233.6 MB) by exact URL, checks size and SHA-256, and writes them under `models/sam2-1-hiera-tiny/814a066/<file name>` with the licence beside them. With Depth Anything (148.7 MB) and the runtime (26.8 MB) the site's models come to about 409 MB of Pages' 1 GB. |
| 3 | LightBench's own conversions in GitHub Release assets | Not needed in part 1 (SAM 2.1's files are onnx-community's, unchanged). |
| 4 | Stored raster up to **2048 px** on the long side; **revised to 1024 px** (owner, 2026-10-22, after the real model's sizes) | `AI_RASTER_LONG = 1024` for every AI part (parts 2–4 inherit it): the matte has the sensor's aspect and at most 1024 texels on its long side (the photo's own size when smaller). Caps: 1024 × 1024 texels and 1 MB of text per matte, 8 MB for an edit's mattes together. Sizes: "Size in the edit". |
| 5 | Pasted AI parts **wait**, with one-click **Compute for this photo**; **presets leave Object selections out**, as brush masks (owner, 2026-10-22) | Paste drops the matte and keeps `what`, the model and the prompt (`withoutRasters`); the mask isn't drawn until **Compute for this photo** (one history step) runs the model on the new photo with the stored prompt. `presetMasks` leaves out masks holding a brush or an Object selection (their points belong to one photo); later AI parts (sky, subject) will be kept in presets, waiting. |
| 6 | Lightroom's AI masks left out on import, said plainly | Unchanged from Part 23: the import leaves out `Mask/Image` and the Masks group says "an AI mask (Subject, Sky, …) was left out: Lightroom keeps its pixels outside the .xmp." |
| 7 | CPU fallback when a cost twin runs in ≤ 15 s | **On** (`OBJECT_WASM_FALLBACK`), measured on the **real** model rather than a twin: the app's own WebAssembly runtime, 4 threads, on GitHub's runner, ran the fp32 encoder in 5.6–6.6 s (first run, with warm-up) and the decoder in 86–106 ms ("Speed"). |

Model rule: SAM 2.1 is eligible under option B (owner, 2026-10-21): its training data is mostly named; about 15 % is Meta's unpublished licensed video, said in plain words in the dialog and in About this model.

### Using it

| Behaviour | Detail |
|---|---|
| The AI row | Under the five New buttons, labelled **AI**: **Object** and **Depth** (the Depth range of Part 24, moved here). Each has a tooltip. Command search: "New mask: Object (AI)" (`develop.maskObject`), no default key, as Depth. |
| First use | The download dialog (below). Nothing is fetched before Download. Declined: no mask, no step, the picker ends. |
| Preparing | Once per photo the photo is encoded: "Preparing the photo for selection (once for this photo)…", or "…on the processor (once for this photo; without a suitable graphics card this takes longer)…". The pointer shows a busy cursor until it is ready. |
| Hover | Pointing previews what a click would select: the selection tinted in the overlay colour with a white outline, following the pointer. The prompt decoder runs on each pointer move; while one runs, newer moves replace each other and only the latest runs next. |
| Click, refine | A click makes **Object n** (one history step "New mask: Object 1"), and the picker stays on to refine it: **Shift-click** adds what is there ("Object 1: add to selection"), **Alt-click** removes it ("Object 1: remove from selection"), a plain click selects something new, a **drag** selects what is inside a box. With Shift or Alt held the preview shows the refined selection. **Esc** or **Done** ends the picker. |
| As a part | Part to combine → **Object (AI)** → + Add / − Subtract / ∩ Intersect turns the picker on for a part of the selected mask. |
| Touch | No hover: a **tap** previews (a ring marks it) and the panel offers **Select** (and **Add**, **Remove** while refining). |
| The part | The selected AI part says where it was made ("Selected at 2 points, 1 removed") and with which model; **Refine on photo…** turns the picker on again; **Compute again** runs the model again on the same prompt (one step). |
| Nothing there | A click where the model finds no object: "Nothing to select there. Point at something else in the photo." Nothing is added. |
| Messages | Not in this build; no suitable GPU and the fallback off ("Object select needs a graphics card with half-precision (fp16) support…"); and Part 24's runtime messages (time limit, lost GPU, out of memory), each with a link to Settings → AI models. |
| Settings → AI models | "SAM 2.1 Hiera Tiny (Object select)": status (variant, files, size), Download…, Remove from this device, About this model (licence, credit, training data, every file with its size and SHA-256, those for this computer marked). |

### Download dialog

"Download SAM 2.1 Hiera Tiny?": Object select needs it; downloaded **once**, from **LightBench's own site**, only if the user agrees, checked against SHA-256, kept on the device (works offline), removable; the size, e.g. "104.8 MB (the model, fp16, 4 files: 78.0 MB; the runtime it needs, ONNX Runtime Web 1.30.0: 26.8 MB)" (fp32: 155.6 MB + runtime); **Licence: Apache-2.0** with the note that the ONNX export has no licence tag of its own and LightBench relies on Meta's statement; **Training data, in plain words**: SA-1B (11 million photos, published by Meta for research use only), SA-V (videos, CC BY 4.0) and about 15 % of Meta's own licensed videos, which are not published, accepted by the owner under the rule that a small unpublished share may be used if stated plainly; About this model; progress and Cancel. On the processor the purpose line says so.

### The groundwork (for parts 2–4)

| Piece | Detail |
|---|---|
| Manifest schema 2 | `schema: 2` (1 still parses). `task`: `depth`, `inpaint`, `segment` (`TASKS`; parts 2–4 add theirs). Files may be several **graphs** (`part`) of a model with their **weights files** beside them (`data: true`, ONNX external data); each variant must hold every graph its task needs (`TASK_PARTS`: segment → encoder, decoder; inpaint → its three). `delivery`: `site` or `huggingface` (Part 26). `source.pinnedBy: "owner"`: pinned from a checked run; `models.mjs pin` leaves it alone. Served names: `<variant>.onnx` for one-file models (unchanged), else the file's own name. |
| Install and load | `store.ts install` takes every file of one variant from the site, checks each, records them (`files`, with `part` and `data`); another variant's files go. `loadPart` returns a graph with its weights (named as the graph refers to them), each checked by SHA-256 again. `installed` checks every file of the installed variant is still there. |
| Runtime | The worker loads a graph with its weights (`externalData`); `runNamed` takes named inputs of several types (sent, or **kept** in the worker), returns several named outputs, and keeps outputs under handles (`keep`). `runtime.ts runGraphNamed`, `dropKept`, `isKept`, `KeptMissingError` (a kept input gone: the caller computes it again, the worker isn't ended). Decoder runs are serialised (one session runs one call at a time). |
| Memory policy | `runtime.ts`: every loaded graph is recorded with its model and weight bytes. After a graph loads, **other** models' graphs are freed, least recently used first and models **held** by an open tool last, until the weights loaded fit `RESIDENT_BYTES` (600 MB: SAM 2.1 and Depth Anything fit together, AI Remove's 1.04 GB doesn't). Freeing comes after the new graph loaded (ONNX Runtime ends its device when its last session goes, Part 26); a model's kept outputs go with its graphs. `holdModel` (the masking tool while Object select is on) also keeps the worker from its 2-minute idle end. |
| Storage | `AiShape` (`kind: 'ai'`): `what` (`object`; later builds' values are kept and drawn), `model`, `modelVersion` (`<commit 7>/<fp16|fp32>`), `runtime` ("onnxruntime-web 1.30.0 webgpu" or "… wasm"), `prompt` (`points`: x, y in sensor coordinates with label 1 add / 0 remove, up to 64; or `box`), `raster`: 8 bits in the **sensor frame**, 255 selected, `u8-med-deflate-b64` (Part 24's encoding). Caps: sides ≤ 1024, ≤ 1024 × 1024 texels and ≤ 1 MB of text per matte; an edit's mattes together ≤ 8 MB of text (past it on load the rest wait; before a change the panel says so). A damaged matte is dropped and its part waits. The saved history keeps each matte once (`@r<n>`). |
| Drawing | `mask-render.ts` case `ai` (`KIND_CODE.ai = 7`): the matte, decoded once and cached, through Part 24's `sensorGrid`; `mask.wgsl` **case 7** samples it (r8unorm, bilinear) at the pixel's sensor uv. A part without a matte, or one that doesn't decode, leaves its mask out of drawings. Drawing never runs a model. The mask stage keeps up to 16 uploaded maps (48 MB), the decoder cache 24 maps (64 MB) (was 4 and 6). |
| Diagnostics | `ai-mask` entries (see "Diagnostics" above): per encoding, per selection, one summary per hover session; no prompt coordinates, names or pixels. |

### Object select's pipeline (`ai/segment.ts`, `ai/objectSelect.ts`, `develop/useObjectSelect.ts`)

| Step | Detail |
|---|---|
| Photo | Develop's rendering **as shot** (Part 24's: the edit's tones and colours, no geometry, lens, masks, Detail or effects, camera orientation) at 1.5 × 1024 on its long side. |
| Encoder input | SAM 2's own (`SAM2Transforms` / transformers' `Sam2Processor`): resized to **1024 × 1024 without keeping the aspect** (SAM 2 doesn't pad, unlike SAM 1), RGB / 255, ImageNet mean and std. |
| Encoder | Once per photo; its three image embeddings (16 MB) stay in the worker (`keep`). The decoder graph is loaded first, so the first pointer move answers at once. |
| Prompt | Points from the sensor frame to the as-shot rendering (the inverse of its uv → sensor map), × 1024 / side (the stretch): `input_points` [1, 1, n, 2], `input_labels` int64 [1, 1, n]; a box as `input_boxes` [1, 1, 4] (the real model gives the same masks with the box as corner points labelled 2 and 3: run 39702229699), else an empty [1, 0, 4]. |
| Output | Of the three masks (logits, 256 × 256 over the stretched photo) the one with the highest predicted IoU, as SAM does; object score ≤ 0: nothing there. Non-finite values: "invalid numbers", nothing changed. |
| Matte | Each sensor-frame texel mapped to the rendering, the logits read **bilinearly** (half-pixel centres, `align_corners=False`, as SAM 2's postprocessing), then a ramp over logits −2…+2 (½ at SAM's threshold 0, flat beyond) in 8 bits. A plain sigmoid (the first version) left SAM's uneven inside and outside in the matte and took 3 times the space ("Size in the edit"). |
| Preview | The same logits read on a 128-cell grid over the photo as drawn (through the drawing's geometry), tinted and outlined (marching squares). |
| Variant | One function, `models.ts objectPlan`: fp16 on WebGPU where the GPU has `shader-f16`; **fp32 on WebGPU where the GPU binds at least 512 MiB in one storage buffer** (`SAM_FP32_MIN_BINDING`; see "ONNX Runtime's binding-limit bug"); else fp32 on the processor (WebAssembly, 4 threads) when `OBJECT_WASM_FALLBACK`; else the plain message. fp32 on the processor: it was faster than fp16 there (5.6 vs 8.4 s) and is the reference precision. |

### The real model on GitHub's servers ("AI models" → `sam-check`)

huggingface.co is blocked here, so the real files were checked by the workflow's new read-only task **`sam-check`** (`web/scripts/sam-check.mjs`, `sam-parity.py`): it downloads the eight pinned files (size and SHA-256), checks each graph's inputs and outputs, runs **the app's own code** (`segment.ts`, the stored encoding, bundled by Vite) with the app's WebAssembly runtime (its CPU fallback) and with onnxruntime-node, on the committed public-domain photo `web/scripts/fixtures/ai-masks/astronaut.png` (NASA, via scikit-image; the photo and point masks-prep used) with five prompts — the suit point (110, 330), that plus a removed point at the selection's centre, plus an added point, a box around the selection given both ways — and then runs **Meta's own PyTorch SAM 2.1 Hiera Tiny** (`SAM2ImagePredictor`, sam2 at `2b90b9f`, checkpoint `sam2.1_hiera_tiny.pt` 156,008,466 bytes, SHA-256 `7402e0d8…`) on the same prompts and compares the masks. Two runs (logs: `scratchpad/am/action-logs/`):

| Run | Result |
|---|---|
| [39702229699](https://github.com/shawneburke88-afk/Photo-Editing-App/actions/runs/39702229699) | Files 8 of 8 ok; I/O as expected; every output finite; suit coverage 0.2247 (binary) with predicted IoU 0.836 / 0.836 (fp32 / fp16), as masks-prep found; **parity with PyTorch: IoU 1.0000 (fp32) and 0.9996–0.9999 (fp16) on all five prompts**; fp16 vs fp32 IoU ≥ 0.9996. Matte (sigmoid): 512 × 512 = 67,360 characters, 2048 × 2048 = 513,900. WebAssembly 4 threads: fp32 encoder 6.55 / 6.37 s, decoder 106 ms; fp16 7.78 / 7.01 s, 142 ms. onnxruntime-node: fp32 2.11 s and 40 ms. |
| [39702694184](https://github.com/shawneburke88-afk/Photo-Editing-App/actions/runs/39702694184) | The ramp matte: same parity (1.0000 / 0.9996–0.9999); suit matte 24,580 characters at 512 × 512, **175,464 at 2048 × 2048, 68,316 at 1024 × 1024**. WebAssembly 4 threads: fp32 encoder 5.63 / 5.47 s, decoder 86 ms; fp16 8.39 / 5.98 s, 108 ms. The onnxruntime-node step couldn't download its native binary on the runner (a network time-out; that step is allowed to fail). |

### Size in the edit

Measured on the real model (run 39702694184): the suit selection (a large, detailed shape in a 512 × 512 photo) takes about **68,000 characters (67 kB) at 1024 × 1024** in the edit and the .xmp, and 175,000 at 2048 × 2048 (the brief's synthetic estimate for 2048 was 15–80 KiB). The owner chose **1024** (2026-10-22): the model's own detail is 256 × 256 over the whole photo, so little is lost. Several Object selections on one photo each carry theirs.

### ONNX Runtime's binding-limit bug (MASKS_PREP_2.md)

The masks-prep investigation found why SAM 2.1 fp32 was wrong on WebGPU: ONNX Runtime 1.30.0 binds a tensor larger than the adapter's `maxStorageBufferBindingSize` as 128 MiB segments, and its WebGPU Softmax (also LayerNormalization, Pad and Where) reads and writes it directly, ignoring the segments: the result is **silently wrong** (Conv errors instead). The upstream fix is the open onnxruntime PR #32897. ONNX Runtime asks for the adapter's own limit, so it hits adapters below the tensor's size, including any that report only WebGPU's 128 MiB minimum. LightBench gates each model on the probed limit instead of rewriting graphs:

| Model | Largest tensor | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| SAM 2.1 Tiny fp32 | global-attention Softmax [1, 4, 4096, 4096], 256 MiB (correct in a browser at a 1 GiB limit, wrong at 128 MiB) | fp32 on WebGPU only at ≥ 512 MiB per binding (`SAM_FP32_MIN_BINDING`); below, the processor |
| SAM 2.1 Tiny fp16 | the same Softmax, exactly 128 MiB: not over any limit | none (unchanged) |
| Moebius (AI Remove, Part 26) | at least 128 MiB (Part 26's graph inspection; a lower bound) | the manifest's `gpu` need raised from 128 MiB to **1 GiB** for both `maxStorageBufferBindingSize` and `maxBufferSize`; a smaller GPU now runs AI Remove on the processor (`INPAINT_WASM_FALLBACK`, on), where before it would have stopped with the "can't hold" message |
| Depth Anything V2 Small | the attention scores [1, 6, T, T], T = 14 × 14 patches + 1 (`depth.ts depthLargestTensor`; inferred from ViT-S/14, the export's graph not inspected): 103 MB fp32 for a 3:2 photo (784 × 518), **143 MB fp32 for 16:9** (924 × 518), more for wider photos; fp16 half | a photo whose scores exceed the binding limit runs on the processor (`useDepth`; the panel says why); so at a 128 MiB limit 16:9 and wider photos in fp32, and panoramas in fp16 |

### Speed

| Where | Encoder (once per photo) | Decoder (each pointer move) | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| GitHub runner, the app's WebAssembly, 4 threads | fp32 5.5–6.6 s; fp16 6.0–8.4 s | fp32 86–106 ms; fp16 108–142 ms | the two runs |
| GitHub runner, onnxruntime-node | fp32 2.1 s; fp16 2.4 s | 40–53 ms | run 39702229699 |
| A real GPU (WebGPU, fp16) | **unmeasured** | **unmeasured** | No GPU here; SwiftShader (the e2e) says nothing about a GPU's speed. |

The CPU fallback is therefore on (≤ 15 s; hover at about 10 previews a second on the runner's CPU).

### Test stand-ins (never shipped)

`scratchpad/am/make_sam_standin.py`: an encoder and a decoder with SAM 2.1's exact input and output names, types and shapes, each graph's weights in a file beside it (external data, named as the real export's); the "encoder" averages pixel blocks, the "decoder" selects a disc of radius 216 (in the 1024 frame) around each positive point, minus discs around negative points, or a box's inside, with three candidates whose third has the highest predicted IoU. Served by the dev server through a test-only manifest with a fake commit (`feedface…`, `mk_test_manifest.mjs`).

- `npx tsc -b`, `npx oxlint` (only the two accepted warnings), `npm run build` pass; `actionlint` passes on the workflow; `models.mjs fetch` on the real manifest fails here only on reaching huggingface.co (as on every build machine).

### Limitations and unverified

- **A real GPU:** SAM 2.1 has never run on WebGPU in a browser: fp16 matched the CPU on lavapipe through onnxruntime-node's WebGPU provider (MASKS_PREP.md), but its speed (encoder, hover decoder), its memory and `shader-f16` behaviour on real GPUs are **unverified** until the owner's test. The hover was exercised only with the stand-ins on SwiftShader (median 116–135 ms per preview there).
- **The binding-limit gates** rest on MASKS_PREP_2.md's kernel sweep and browser runs (SwiftShader, real and emulated 128 MiB limits); which limits users' real adapters report is unverified. Depth Anything's largest tensor is inferred from its architecture, not read from the export's graph; the gate was unit-tested, not run end to end (the test photo is 3:2, under the limit).
- **The CPU fallback in a browser tab**: timed in Node with the same WebAssembly runtime on GitHub's 4-core runner, not in a browser or on the owner's computer; its memory in a tab is unmeasured.
- **Pages:** `vision_encoder.onnx_data` (fp32) is 134 MB, one file; Pages documents a 1 GB site limit but no per-file limit for Actions deployments that was checked here (**unverified** until the next deploy).
- **Edge quality:** the matte's edge comes from 256 × 256 logits over the whole photo (about 23 px of a 6000 px photo per logit cell), enlarged bilinearly; Feather and Refine act on it at render time. Hair and fine detail will be soft; not judged by eye on a real photo here (the artifact's PNGs couldn't be downloaded from this machine).
- The stand-ins' mattes (soft discs) aren't representative of real sizes; the sizes above are the real model's.

### The owner's answers (2026-10-22)

1. Mattes at **at most 1024 px** on the long side, for every AI part (above).
2. **Presets leave Object selections out**, as brush masks; paste still carries them, waiting.
3. Ship with fp32 on WebGPU off; then, after MASKS_PREP_2.md, **gate it on a ≥ 512 MiB binding limit** instead, and raise Moebius's GPU need to 1 GiB (above).

## Part 29: AI masks, part 3: Sky

**What it does.** A **Sky** button in the Masks group's AI row finds the sky in the photo and makes it a mask ("Sky 1") in one history step. The model is **EfficientViT-Seg B1 trained on ADE20K** (MIT Han Lab; Apache-2.0), in **LightBench's own ONNX conversion**, published as a GitHub Release of this repository and served from LightBench's site like the other models. The model's coarse answer (one class per 8 × 8 input pixels) is refined against the photo so branches, wires and lattices against the sky come out of the mask. Like every AI mask (Part 27) the matte is kept in the edit and the .xmp (at most 1024 px on its long side), so it draws everywhere without the model; old edits are unchanged (below). Part 2 (People) is Part 28, built in parallel.

### Owner's decisions and how they are built

| Decision | Built as |
|---|---|
| Sky model: EfficientViT-Seg B1 ADE20K, **our own ONNX export** | `web/scripts/sky/export_efficientvit.py`, run on GitHub's runner by the "AI models" workflow's new **`efficientvit-export`** task: the checkpoint the manifest pins (`convertedFrom`: `han-cai/efficientvit-seg` at `cf3ccaf9…`, `efficientvit_seg_b1_ade20k.pt`, 19,339,726 bytes, SHA-256 `59cca6a7…`) is downloaded and checked, the model is built with the authors' code at its pinned commit (`mit-han-lab/efficientvit` `de7d7733…`; `create_efficientvit_seg_model` loads every weight strictly; the authors' own `onnx_export.py` uses `pretrained=False`), and exported with `torch.onnx.export` (torch 2.5.1, TorchScript exporter, opset 17, onnx-simplifier 0.8.1) with **height and width free**: input `pixel_values` float32 [1, 3, H, W], output `logits` [1, 150, H/8, W/8]. The candidate check (MASKS_PREP.md) exported a fixed 512 × 512 graph; a free size lets each photo keep its aspect as the authors' ADE20K evaluation does. |
| Licence and training data | Apache-2.0 (`web/models/notices/efficientvit.LICENSE.txt`, the repository's LICENSE at `de7d773`; checked byte for byte against the clone in the export job). Training data in plain words in the manifest, the download dialog and Settings → AI models: ADE20K (about 20,000 scene photos outlined into 150 kinds of thing, published by MIT; non-commercial research and education only) and ImageNet-1K for the backbone (likewise non-commercial research and education only); both named. Credit: Cai, Li, Hu, Gan and Han, ICCV 2023. |
| Hosting: **GitHub Release assets in this repo**; the deploy copies them into the site | Manifest `"source": { "host": "github-release", "repo": "shawneburke88-afk/Photo-Editing-App", "tag": "models-efficientvit-seg-b1-ade20k-1", "revision": "36abc1dd…" }` (the commit the tag points at). `models.mjs` (`releaseUrl`, `sourceUrl`) fetches `https://github.com/<repo>/releases/download/<tag>/<asset>` and checks size and SHA-256 like any other file; `pin` leaves it alone; `parseManifest` requires the tag, plain asset names and site delivery. Served at `models/efficientvit-seg-b1-ade20k/36abc1d/efficientvit_seg_b1_ade20k.onnx` with `LICENSE.txt` beside it. |
| Publishing: the workflow's token, `contents: write` scoped to that job, `gh release`, only when the check passes, never overwriting | The workflow's default permission is now **read**; the `models` job keeps `contents: write` (it commits the pinned manifest), the export-and-check job `sky` is read-only, and a separate **`sky-publish`** job, the only other one with `contents: write`, runs only for `efficientvit-export` with **publish** ticked and only when the `sky` job's PyTorch comparison **and** the app's sky-check passed. It re-checks the artifact's SHA-256 against the `sky` job's output, refuses if the release **or** the tag exists ("never overwritten: give a new export a new tag"), runs `gh release create <tag> --target <commit> --latest=false` with the ONNX file, `LICENSE.txt`, `SOURCE.md` (where it came from, how it was made and checked, licence, training data) and `export.json`, then downloads the asset by its public URL and checks the bytes. |
| Mattes are shapes, at most 1024 px; pasted parts wait for Compute | `kind: 'ai'`, `what: 'sky'` (`AI_WHATS`), no prompt; stored as Part 27's mattes. Paste drops the matte (`withoutRasters`) and the part waits; **Compute for this photo** finds the photo's sky once and gives it to every waiting Sky part (one history step). |
| Presets keep Sky parts, waiting | `presetMasks` already kept AI parts other than Object selections without their matte (Part 27); a preset with a Sky mask (its sliders, Invert, its other parts) applies it waiting for Compute. Tested in `sky-mask.test.ts` and the e2e. |
| No notes in the UI; a tooltip on every new control; the legal notices kept | Tooltips on the Sky button, the Sky part's two buttons and the "Part to combine" list; the dialog's licence and training data; THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md has the model's row and licence section. |

### Using it

| Behaviour | Detail |
|---|---|
| The AI row | **Object**, **Depth**, **Sky**. Sky's tooltip says what it does, that the model runs on this computer, and (in the help) that Invert gives "everything except the sky". No keyboard shortcut (as Object and Depth have none by default). |
| First use | The download dialog (below). Nothing is fetched before Download; Not now: no mask, no step. |
| A click | "Finding the sky…" (or "…on the processor (…)" with the reason), then **Sky 1**: one history step "New mask: Sky 1", undone by one Undo. |
| As a part | Part to combine → **Sky (AI)** → + Add / − Subtract / ∩ Intersect: e.g. a Linear gradient minus the sky. The photo's sky is found once while the photo is open and reused; **Find the sky again** runs the model again (one step). |
| Everything except the sky | The Sky part's **Everything except the sky** button (shown when the sky is the mask's only part) is the mask's Invert, one step ("Sky 1: invert"); with other parts, the part's own **Invert** leaves the sky out. |
| Messages | Not in this build; no WebGPU or a GPU limit too small (it runs on the processor and says so); Part 24's runtime messages; each with a link to Settings → AI models. |
| Settings → AI models | "EfficientViT-Seg B1 (ADE20K) (Sky mask)": status, Download…, Remove, About this model (what it does, licence, training data, credit, the file with its size and SHA-256, and "LightBench's own conversion of efficientvit_seg_b1_ade20k.pt (han-cai/efficientvit-seg at cf3ccaf, SHA-256 …) with the authors' code (mit-han-lab/efficientvit at de7d773), published as the release models-efficientvit-seg-b1-ade20k-1 …; served by LightBench's own site"). |

### Download dialog

"Download EfficientViT-Seg B1 (ADE20K)?": the Sky mask needs it; downloaded **once**, from **LightBench's own site**, only if the user agrees, checked against SHA-256, kept on the device, removable; the size: **19.3 MB** for the model (fp32, the only variant) plus ONNX Runtime Web's 26.8 MB if it isn't on the device yet; **Licence: Apache-2.0** with the note that the file is LightBench's own conversion published with the licence and a note of where it came from; **Training data, in plain words** (above); About this model.

### The pipeline (`ai/sky.ts`, `ai/skySelect.ts`, `develop/useSky.ts`)

| Step | Detail |
|---|---|
| Photo | Develop's rendering **as shot** (Part 24's), its long side 1024 (or the model's input if larger; never more than the photo). |
| Input | EfficientViT's ADE20K evaluation: the **short side 512**, the long side up to the next multiple of 32 at the same scale (a 3:2 photo: 768 × 512), at most 2048 (a panorama is squeezed a little); area resize; RGB / 255 minus ImageNet's mean, over its std. |
| Scores to one number | Per cell, the sky's **margin over the strongest other class**, z_sky − max z_other: positive exactly where the model's own answer is sky. (The softmax log-odds against all 149 others stayed below 0 where many classes scored a little; dropped.) |
| Enlarged | Bilinear (half-pixel centres) to the rendering's size, then the logistic function. |
| Edges, a: two-colour fit | Within about two model cells of each pixel, the typical sky colour (colours weighted p²) and the typical other colour ((1 − p)²); where they differ by at least 0.06 (sRGB 0…1), the pixel's colour projected on the line between them gives its sky share, mixed in as far as both are present (fully from a 5 % share each), so inside the sky (clouds included) and inside the land the model's answer stands. A pixel rises into the sky only as far as its colour is like the photo's sky (Mahalanobis distance in the spread of the colours the model is surest are sky, fading beyond 3); and where the model is unsure (below 0.95) and the colour is nothing like the sky, the pixel leaves the sky first (the launch pad's lamps and their glare). |
| Edges, b: the guided filter | The engine's guided filter as the masks' Refine slider runs it (`mask-math.ts refineCpu`: He and Sun's fast guided filter on the photo's log luminance), radius 2 px, eps 1e-4, its rises gated the same way. |
| Stored | Into the sensor frame through the rendering's uv → sensor map, 8 bits, at most 1024 px long (`AI_RASTER_LONG`), Part 24's encoding. |
| Tried and dropped | The guided filter alone at one or two cells: the synthetic edge error went only from 0.21 to 0.19 (the model's matte barely varies where a thin branch crosses the sky). Dehaze's colour guided filter (`effects.ts guidedFilterCpu`): worse, its darktable guard against a near-singular 3 × 3 solve turns it into a box blur at a clean two-colour edge unless eps is large. A two-tone fit on luminance only: missed the dark lattices against the dark twilight sky of `rocket.jpg` and let its lamps into the sky. |
| Edge quality | Synthetic test (`sky.test.ts`: branches and twigs 1–3 px against a sky gradient, the model's view stood in for by the truth averaged over 8 px cells): edge error **0.209 → 0.003** (an easy case: the tree is far from the sky in colour). The real model's `rocket.jpg` matte, looked at by eye: all four lattice towers come out crisp where the plain enlargement was a grey blur; the lamps stay out. Not tuned on a set of real photos. |

### The GPU limit gate

The export job measured every node's output at 768 × 512 (ONNX Runtime, CPU): the largest tensor is **25,165,824 bytes = 64 bytes per input pixel** (`/backbone/stages.0/op_list.0/main/inverted_conv/conv`, [1, 64, 256, 384], and its HardSwish); the next is half that. The manifest's `input.bytesPerPixel: 64` gives `skyLargestTensor(mw, mh)`; `models.ts skyPlan` runs the model on WebGPU only where that fits the adapter's `maxStorageBufferBindingSize` (part 1's gate: ONNX Runtime 1.30.0's WebGPU kernels compute a larger tensor wrong), else on the processor (`SKY_WASM_FALLBACK`, on). At 3:2 the default input needs 25 MB, the capped 2048 × 512 panorama 64 MB: both fit WebGPU's minimum 128 MiB, so the gate matters only for a GPU reporting less (tested with 16 MiB in the e2e). The graph has Shape, Gather, Unsqueeze and Concat nodes for its free size; on WebGPU ONNX Runtime places such int64 shape arithmetic on the CPU (MASKS_PREP.md); the candidate check ran the fixed-size export's 243 nodes all on WebGPU (lavapipe), the free-size graph's placement in a browser is **unverified**.

### Input size: quality against speed

The app's own code with the app's WebAssembly runtime (4 threads) on GitHub's runner (run 40407134681), model run (second run) and the share of cells the model calls sky:

| Short side (input, 3:2) | rocket.jpg | astronaut.png | trees (CC0, 1280 × 1707) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 384 (576 × 384) | 266 ms, 0.891 | 493 ms, 0 | 285 ms, 0.794 |
| **512 (768 × 512), the app's** | **385 ms, 0.885** | **263 ms, 0** | **356 ms, 0.794** |
| 640 (960 × 640) | 577 ms, 0.877 | 412 ms, 0 | 530 ms, 0.797 |
| 768 (1152 × 768) | 831 ms, 0.849 | 556 ms, 0 | 753 ms, 0.802 |
| 1024 (1536 × 1024) | 2,256 ms, 0.882 | 956 ms, 0 | 1,236 ms, 0.803 |

The model was trained and evaluated at 512 on the short side; larger inputs cost 1.5–6 × the time and don't make the class map better (rocket's sky share even dips at 768), and the edges come from the refinement at the guide's resolution, not from the model. **512 stays.** (Run 1, an earlier refinement: 225 ms at 512 for rocket; runner times vary about 2 ×.) The matte step took 0.2–0.3 s for rocket (640 × 427) and about 1.5 s for the trees photo, which sky-check refines at its full 1280 × 1707 (2.2 Mpx); the app refines at most 1024 px on the long side (0.7 Mpx for a 3:4 photo), on the page's main thread (**unmeasured in a browser**; a worker would move it off).

Its matte was read only as a text picture in the log; the PNGs are in the runs' artifacts, which can't be downloaded here.

### Diagnostics

`ai-mask` entries with `what: "sky"`: the step (new, part, compute, recompute), GPU or CPU, the variant, the model's input size, `gate: "binding"` when the limit sent it to the processor, the model, matte and total times and those of the rendering and loading, the matte's size and characters, the model and the card; or the error. The times come first, so an entry over the recorder's 600-character limit keeps them. No pixels or names.

### Checks

- **End to end** (headless Chromium, SwiftShader WebGPU, `?gpu=readback`, the TEST stand-in model `make_sky_standin.py` with the real input and output names and free size: an 8 × 8 average and a 1 × 1 convolution whose sky score grows with blue over red; `scratchpad/ams/e2e-sky.mjs`):
  - *main* (30 of 30): opening Develop fetches nothing; Settings → AI models lists "EfficientViT-Seg B1 (ADE20K) (Sky mask)", not downloaded, and About this model gives Apache-2.0, ADE20K, ImageNet-1K, non-commercial research and education, LightBench's own conversion of the pinned checkpoint and the release tag; the AI row's Sky button and its tooltip; the dialog (once, LightBench's own site, size and runtime, licence, training data) with no request while open; Not now: no request, mask or step; Download fetched exactly the runtime and the one model file; **one** history step "New mask: Sky 1"; the edit holds `what: "sky"`, the model, `feedfac/fp32`, "onnxruntime-web 1.30.0 webgpu", no prompt, a 640 × 427 matte; the console and the `ai-mask` Diagnostics entry (input [768, 512], provider, times; no pixels); White on Black: sky 254, land 0; Undo removes it in one step, Redo puts it back; Exposure +1.5 in the mask: sky +43.6, land 0; **Everything except the sky**: one step "Sky 1: invert", then land +65.9, sky 0; Part to combine offers Sky (AI); Subtract the sky reused the photo's sky (no second run); Find the sky again: one step, a new run; R1.xmp holds the part and matte; pasted onto R2 it keeps its model, has no matte, stays inverted and waits; the panel offers Compute for this photo, one step, one run for both waiting Sky parts.
  - *gate* (3 of 3): with the page's checks told the GPU binds only 16 MiB (the 768 × 512 input needs 25 MB) the model ran on the processor ("… wasm") and the Diagnostics entry has `gate: "binding"`. (The "on the processor" status line was too brief to be caught; its text is not checked.)
  - *presets* (3 of 3, on the second try): a preset saved with Masks ticked carries "Sky 1" (its Exposure −0.7) without a matte; applied to R2 it waits, and Compute for this photo made it in one step. The first try hit the runtime's 3-minute watchdog on SwiftShader while the machine was loaded (the stand-in's WebGPU run took 7–60 s here); the retry passed.
  - *real* (2 of 2, the **real model** served by the test server): it ran on SwiftShader's WebGPU in Chromium (55–60 s on that software GPU), "onnxruntime-web 1.30.0 webgpu", and its 640 × 427 matte agrees with the one the same code made in Node with the WebAssembly runtime from the JPEG's own pixels at IoU 0.9715 (the app's input is Develop's rendering, not the JPEG's pixels, so they aren't identical).
- **Old edits unchanged** (`scratchpad/ams/identity.mjs`, Part 27's copy plus an **Object select edit** and a **Sky edit**; each case in its own browser; full-size and 1024 px export SHA-256, 512 px thumbnail, histogram, canvas at fit and 100 %, output size; this branch against main `ea0abfd`): **42 of 42 identical**: C.JPG at defaults, a mask edit (radial + brush + luminance), the Depth range edit, an Object select edit, a Sky edit (inverted) and A.NEF with masks, a tone curve and spots, 7 of 7 measures each. The Sky edit draws identically on main, which draws an unknown AI part's matte as Part 27 provides.

New: `ai/sky.test.ts` (8: input sizes, normalisation, the margin, box sums, half-pixel enlarging, the synthetic edge test, a cloud and a snow scene, the sensor frame), `ai/sky-manifest.test.ts` (4: the entry, release URL and served path, malformed entries, the binding gate), `engine/develop/sky-mask.test.ts` (4: names, sidecar round trip, paste, presets), `scripts/models.test.mjs` (+1: release URLs).

### Limitations and unverified

- **A real GPU:** the free-size graph has run on the CPU (ONNX Runtime 1.31.0 in Python, 1.30.0's WebAssembly in Node) and on SwiftShader's WebGPU in headless Chromium with the stand-in; the real model has not run on WebGPU in a browser or on a real GPU here.
- **Edge refinement** is LightBench's own, tuned on one synthetic scene and one real photo by eye (`rocket.jpg`); the trees photo was seen only as a text picture. Dark storm clouds or sunset colours near the horizon that the model is unsure of and that differ from the rest of the sky could leave the mask; snow or white walls the same colour as an overcast sky can't be told apart by colour. Not tested on a set of real photos.
- **Speed in a browser:** the model's speed is from Node on GitHub's runner; the matte step runs on the main thread (about 0.3 s for 640 × 427 in Node) and its time for a 1024-px guide in a browser tab is unmeasured.
- **Release permanence:** the release's tag points at `36abc1d` on this branch; the commit stays reachable through the tag whatever the merge does.
- ADE20K's and ImageNet's terms are summarised from their published terms of use, not legal advice.
